## Rules

<a id="syntax"></a>
### syntax

**Syntax error** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

The source code does not parse. The message says, like the compiler, what was expected and what was found ("';' expected but 'end' found (like E2029).") and carries the compiler code: missing ';' (E2066 between statements, E2029 in declarations), ';' before else (E2153), unterminated string (E2052) or comment with its start line (E2057), statements in the interface part (E2050), identifier after a trailing dot (E2372), strict without private or protected (E2375), class before something other than a routine, var or property (E2123), a number with an empty exponent such as 1.5e (E2053), absolute with an address instead of a variable (E2088), try without except or finally (E2125), for without a variable (E2088). Checks on the finished tree: procedure with a result type (E2025), function declaration without one (E2023; the short form of an implementation is fine), value parameter without a type (E2067), initialised local variable (E2195) or several variables with one initial value (E2196), class, interface or object type in a var section (E2058, in a routine also E2059) or local to a routine (E2059), class operator in a class, interface or object type (E2123; operators exist only in records). The codes follow Delphi 13; older versions reported E2357, E2060 and E2061 instead, label in the interface part (E2049) and goto to an undeclared label (E2031; a label of a surrounding routine is reported by label-misuse as E2093). After an unterminated string or comment the follow-up errors are dropped, as the compiler stops there. Inactive {$IFDEF} branches are not reported. Quick fixes: remove ';' before else (safe, also on save), add ';', close the string, close the comment at the end of its first line.

Matches the compiler message `E2023`, `E2025`, `E2029`, `E2031`, `E2049`, `E2050`, `E2052`, `E2057`, `E2058`, `E2059`, `E2060`, `E2061`, `E2066`, `E2067`, `E2088`, `E2125`, `E2153`, `E2195`, `E2196`, `E2357`, `E2372`, `E2375`, `E2053`, `E2123`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix syntax`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
if Gueltig then
  Speichern;
else
  Verwerfen;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Gueltig then
  Speichern
else
  Verwerfen;
```

<a id="language-feature"></a>
### language-feature

**Language feature of a newer Delphi version** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

The code uses a language feature that the project's language level does not know yet: inline variable and constant (Delphi 10.3), managed record with class operator Initialize/Finalize (Delphi 10.4), digit separator 1_000 and binary literal %1010 (Delphi 11), multiline string ''' (Delphi 12), if expression, "is not" and "not in" (Delphi 13). The language level is the older of delphiVersion and minDelphiVersion in atlas.json: minDelphiVersion names the oldest Delphi version the project must still compile with (e.g. "12" for code shared by Delphi 12 and 13). Without either setting nothing is reported. Inactive {$IF CompilerVersion >= …} branches are not reported, so code for several versions can live side by side. Reported in the editor, by --check, check_code and the Claude Code hook (hook_check). Code actions and slash commands do not generate features beyond the language level.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
// minDelphiVersion "12"
if Sender is not TButton then
  Exit;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if not (Sender is TButton) then
  Exit;
```

<a id="asm-invalid"></a>
### asm-invalid

**Invalid statement in inline assembler** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Statements in asm blocks that dcc rejects: a missing operand (mov eax, ) and esp as an index register with a scale factor ([eax + esp * 2]) like E2105, two registers of different size (mov al, ebx), lea with a number and a number as the target of mov like E2107 (dcc reports E2107 here instead of E2108 and E2116), db with a local variable of the routine like E2109, OFFSET a + OFFSET b like E2111, a number beyond 64 bits like E2113, a string constant of more than 4 characters for a 32-bit register like E2114 and division by zero (/ 0, mod 0) like E2118. Only forms confirmed with dcc are reported, at most one finding per statement. Statements with a compiler directive or a { } comment in the same line (Free Pascal writes ARM register lists that way), the other data directives, Win64 registers (rax, r8 …, which can be variable names under Win32) and files for Free Pascal ({$MODE …}, {$ASMMODE …}) stay silent.

Matches the compiler message `E2105`, `E2107`, `E2109`, `E2111`, `E2113`, `E2114`, `E2118`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
asm
  mov al, ebx
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
asm
  mov al, bl
end;
```

<a id="unused-suppression"></a>
### unused-suppression

**Unused suppression** · Code smell · Hint · Group Atlas internal

An lsp-ignore comment suppresses no finding and can be removed.

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix unused-suppression`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
// lsp-ignore: empty-except
X := 1;
```

Correct:

```pascal
X := 1;
```

<a id="unknown-rule-id"></a>
### unknown-rule-id

**Unknown rule id** · Code smell · Hint · Group Atlas internal

An lsp-ignore comment names a rule id that does not exist, so it suppresses nothing. The message suggests the most similar known id (typo); such a comment is not also reported as unused-suppression.

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix unknown-rule-id`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
// lsp-ignore: empty-exept
try X; except end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
// lsp-ignore: empty-except
try X; except end;
```

<a id="unit-too-deep"></a>
### unit-too-deep

**Unit too deep to check** · Code smell · Hint · Group Atlas internal

The syntax tree of the unit is more than 100000 levels deep, so the rules do not run for it. Such trees only come from chains with as many terms, such as X := A - B - C - … The hint marks the deepest point; splitting the chain into partial expressions makes the unit checkable again.

Flagged:

```pascal
X := A - B - C - D - E; // … 100000 terms
```

Correct:

```pascal
Part := A - B - C;
X := Part - D - E;
```

<a id="bidi-control"></a>
### bidi-control

**Bidirectional control character** · Vulnerability · Error · Group Security

Bidirectional control characters can make code display differently from how it compiles (Trojan Source); also in form files. Direction marks in RTL text only with low confidence.

Flagged:

```pascal
if IsAdmin then { U+202E } begin
```

Correct:

```pascal
if IsAdmin then begin
```

<a id="invisible-char"></a>
### invisible-char

**Invisible character** · Security hotspot · Warning · Group Security

Invisible or zero-width characters in the source code and form files; medium confidence in strings and comments.

Flagged:

```pascal
Count{U+200B} := 0;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Count := 0;
```

<a id="control-char"></a>
### control-char

**Control character** · Bug · Error · Group Security

NUL or control characters or invalid UTF-8 bytes in the source code and the form file; invalid bytes with exact line and column. Like dcc, a file without a BOM counts as UTF-8 only if the whole file is valid UTF-8; an ANSI file is checked for invalid bytes only if its valid UTF-8 sequences outweigh them. U+FFFD is an error in code, a hint in strings and ignored in comments.

Flagged:

```pascal
S := 'abc{U+0000}';
```

Correct:

```pascal
S := 'abc' + #0;
```

<a id="confusable-identifier"></a>
### confusable-identifier

**Confusable identifier** · Security hotspot · Warning · Group Security

Identifiers that mix scripts or contain non-ASCII characters can be confused with others.

Flagged:

```pascal
var Pаssword: string; { Cyrillic 'а' }
```

Correct:

```pascal
var Password: string;
```

<a id="forbidden-uses"></a>
### forbidden-uses

**Forbidden uses** · Code smell · Error · Group Architecture

A uses entry violates a layer rule configured under "layers" in atlas.json, e.g. *.Common.* must not use Vcl.*.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `layers` | json | `[]` | Layer rules: [{"from": glob, "deny": [globs], "allow": [globs]}]. |

Flagged:

```pascal
unit App.Common.Model;
interface
uses Vcl.Forms;
```

Correct:

```pascal
unit App.Common.Model;
interface
uses System.Classes;
```

<a id="uses-cycle"></a>
### uses-cycle

**Circular unit reference via uses** · Code smell · Warning · Group Architecture

A uses entry leads, through the uses clauses of other units, back to the unit itself. If every edge of the cycle is in an interface section, the compiler rejects it (like F2047; a unit naming itself like F2092) and the finding is an error; only then does the message name the code (#1383); cycles with at least one implementation edge are allowed by Delphi but couple the units tightly (initialization order, recompiling, testing) and are reported with the rule's severity (option implementation). The message shows the complete shortest path (A → B → C → A), the finding hover each edge with its section. Based on the shared uses graph over the symbol index (#268), with inactive conditional branches hidden; the checked unit counts with its editor text. Silent without projects (linter, file without project), while projects are loading or the index is incomplete, and for units the uses graph does not know.

Matches the compiler message `F2047`, `F2092`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `implementation` | boolean | `true` | Also report cycles with at least one edge in an implementation section (allowed by the compiler). |

Flagged:

```pascal
unit uKunde;
interface
uses uAuftrag;   // uAuftrag uses uKunde in its interface

```

Correct:

```pascal
unit uKunde;
interface
// common types moved to uTypen, used by both
uses uTypen;
```

<a id="architecture-role-import"></a>
### architecture-role-import

**Import violates the role matrix** · Code smell · Warning · Group Architecture

A uses entry violates the import matrix of the role concept (output artefact, feature, role: Contract, Internals, Providers, Adapters, Composition, Hosting). Off by default; switch it on in atlas.json with the option "prefixes". Approved exceptions go to "allow" as "Source -> Target" globs. Test units (DUnitX, DUnit or TestInsight in the uses clause) are not checked.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `prefixes` | string-list | `[]` | Prefixes of the output artefacts, e.g. "Atlas.Common"; without them the rule reports nothing. |
| `hostRoles` | string-list | `["Foundation","Core","Shell","Adapters","Platform"]` | First name segments of host units without prefix. |
| `allow` | string-list | `[]` | Approved exceptions "Source -> Target" (both globs). |

Flagged:

```pascal
unit Atlas.Common.Logging.Contract;
interface
uses Atlas.Common.Logging.Providers.Logger;
```

Correct:

```pascal
unit Atlas.Common.Logging.Contract;
interface
uses System.SysUtils;
```

<a id="architecture-contract-content"></a>
### architecture-contract-content

**Class in a contract unit** · Code smell · Warning · Group Architecture

A contract unit declares a class in its interface section. Contracts hold only records, interfaces, enumerations and exceptions. Off by default; switch it on in atlas.json with the option "prefixes". Approved exceptions go to "allow" as "Unit.Type" globs. Test units are not checked.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `prefixes` | string-list | `[]` | Prefixes of the output artefacts, e.g. "Atlas.Common". |
| `allow` | string-list | `[]` | Approved exceptions "Unit.Type" as glob. |

Flagged:

```pascal
unit App.Orders.Contract;
interface
type
  TOrderList = class
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
unit App.Orders.Contract;
interface
type
  IOrderList = interface
  end;
```

<a id="forbidden-symbol"></a>
### forbidden-symbol

**Forbidden symbol used** · Code smell · Warning · Group Architecture

A list under "symbols" in atlas.json forbids routines, types, constants, fields, properties, enum values or identifiers, each with a message and a replacement. Matching uses name resolution, not text: System.SysUtils.FileExists also hits FileExists, but not an own method of the same name. An entry may shorten the name from a dot boundary (TThread.Queue) and contain *. Identifiers of System without source (Writeln, Halt) count as System.<Name>. If the left part of a qualified name cannot be resolved at all (unit not indexed), the written name is compared with the end of the entry. Per entry: kind (routine, type, constant, field, property, enumValue, identifier = by name without resolution), message, replacement, severity, units (unit name globs, "!" excludes), firstArgument (only with this first argument, e.g. nil) and allowFirstArgument (allowed with this first argument, e.g. ErrOutput); both compare the argument text or the name of its type (TextFile). Off by default and silent without a list; on in the strict profile. High confidence. Quick fix: replace with the configured replacement.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `symbols` | object-list | `[]` | Table of forbidden symbols, one object per entry: name (qualified, * allowed), kind (routine, type, constant, field, property, enumValue or identifier), message, replacement, severity, units (globs, ! excludes), firstArgument and allowFirstArgument (argument texts or type names). |

Flagged:

```pascal
// atlas.json: "forbidden-symbol": {"symbols": [{"name": "System.Write*", "kind": "routine", "message": "Only via ILogger"}]}
procedure TImport.Report(const AText: string);
begin
  Writeln(AText);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Report(const AText: string);
begin
  FLogger.Info(AText);
end;
```

<a id="thread-vcl-access"></a>
### thread-vcl-access

**VCL access from a thread** · Bug · Warning · Group Threads

TThread.Execute, TThread.CreateAnonymousThread or TTask accesses the VCL (Application, Screen, ShowMessage, MessageDlg …) outside Synchronize or Queue. The VCL is not thread-safe.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TWorker.Execute;
begin
  Application.Title := 'Done';
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TWorker.Execute;
begin
  TThread.Queue(nil, procedure begin Application.Title := 'Done' end);
end;
```

<a id="lock-without-finally"></a>
### lock-without-finally

**Lock without try/finally** · Bug · Warning · Group Threads

A lock (TCriticalSection.Enter/Acquire, TMonitor.Enter, BeginRead/BeginWrite, TSpinLock.Enter, TMutex.Acquire, EnterCriticalSection or a pair from the lockPairs option) is not directly followed by try/finally releasing it. An exception leaves the lock held.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `lockPairs` | json | `[]` | Additional lock pairs as [["Lock", "Release"]]. |

Flagged:

```pascal
CS.Enter;
DoWork;
CS.Leave;
```

Correct:

```pascal
CS.Enter;
try
  DoWork;
finally
  CS.Leave;
end;
```

<a id="thread-use-after-free-on-terminate"></a>
### thread-use-after-free-on-terminate

**Thread used after start with FreeOnTerminate** · Bug · Warning · Group Threads

A thread with FreeOnTerminate := True may free itself as soon as it is started; using the variable afterwards can access a freed object.

Flagged:

```pascal
T.FreeOnTerminate := True;
T.Start;
Log(T.ThreadID);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Log(T.ThreadID);
T.FreeOnTerminate := True;
T.Start;
```

<a id="thread-free-with-free-on-terminate"></a>
### thread-free-with-free-on-terminate

**Free on a thread with FreeOnTerminate** · Bug · Error · Group Threads

Free on a thread with FreeOnTerminate := True frees it twice.

Flagged:

```pascal
T.FreeOnTerminate := True;
T.Start;
T.Free;
```

Correct:

```pascal
T.FreeOnTerminate := True;
T.Start;
```

<a id="synchronize-in-destructor"></a>
### synchronize-in-destructor

**Synchronize in thread destructor** · Bug · Warning · Group Threads

Synchronize in the destructor of a TThread descendant (or a method it calls) deadlocks: the main thread waits in WaitFor while the thread waits for Synchronize.

Flagged:

```pascal
destructor TWorker.Destroy;
begin
  Synchronize(Notify);
  inherited;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
destructor TWorker.Destroy;
begin
  inherited;
end;
```

<a id="thread-resume-suspend"></a>
### thread-resume-suspend

**Deprecated TThread.Resume/Suspend** · Code smell · Warning · Group Threads

TThread.Resume and Suspend are deprecated. Start a created thread with Start; Suspend has no safe replacement, wait with an event instead. Silent when the receiver's declared type has a complete ancestor chain without TThread (own classes with Resume/Suspend methods).

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix thread-resume-suspend`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
FWorker.Resume;
```

Correct:

```pascal
FWorker.Start;
```

<a id="result-not-set"></a>
### result-not-set

**Function result possibly not set** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A path through the function ends without assigning Result. The caller then reads an undefined value. A for loop runs at least once when both bounds are number literals or true constants of the unit (cAttempts = 3) and the range is not empty, as Delphi evaluates it.

Flagged:

```pascal
function Find: Integer;
begin
  if Ok then
    Result := 1;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function Find: Integer;
begin
  Result := 0;
  if Ok then
    Result := 1;
end;
```

<a id="possibly-uninitialized"></a>
### possibly-uninitialized

**Variable possibly uninitialized** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A local variable is read on a path where it was never assigned.

Flagged:

```pascal
var I: Integer;
begin
  if Ok then
    I := 1;
  Writeln(I);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
var I: Integer;
begin
  I := 0;
  if Ok then
    I := 1;
  Writeln(I);
end;
```

<a id="unreachable-code"></a>
### unreachable-code

**Unreachable code** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A statement after Exit, raise, Abort, Halt, Break, Continue or goto is never executed. Usually a leftover or a misplaced jump. Only the first statement of an unreachable section is reported.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Save;
begin
  Exit;
  Store;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Save;
begin
  if not Ready then
    Exit;
  Store;
end;
```

<a id="unused-local"></a>
### unused-local

**Unused local variable** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A local variable is declared but never used. Like compiler hint H2164, but while typing.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ignore` | string-list | `[]` | Name patterns that are never reported (* and ?). |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Foo;
var
  I, Count: Integer;
begin
  I := 1;
  Bar(I);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Foo;
var
  I: Integer;
begin
  I := 1;
  Bar(I);
end;
```

<a id="unused-parameter"></a>
### unused-parameter

**Unused parameter** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A parameter is never used in the routine body. Overridden and virtual methods, event handlers (also methods without a visibility section in classes with RTTI such as TForm, TFrame and TDataModule, and methods the form file assigns to an event), published methods, interface implementations and callbacks are exempt, including routines in tables of routines (typed constants, array literals). A routine that is only called from an anonymous method has a free signature and is reported. Passing a routine as a value in another unit is not visible to the rule; suppress it there with lsp-ignore and a reason.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ignore` | string-list | `[]` | Name patterns that are never reported (* and ?). |

Flagged:

```pascal
function Twice(A, B: Integer): Integer;
begin
  Result := 2 * A;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function Twice(A: Integer): Integer;
begin
  Result := 2 * A;
end;
```

<a id="unused-assignment"></a>
### unused-assignment

**Value assigned is never used** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A value assigned to a local variable is overwritten or the routine ends before it is read. Like compiler hint H2077, which dcc gives only for unmanaged types: for strings, dynamic arrays, variants and records with such fields, and for variables without a declared type, the message names no code (#1383). Variables of an interface type are exempt: assigning them holds or releases a reference (scope guards, Writer := nil). Clearing a value that was set before (X := nil, '', Default(T), T.Empty) is not reported either: it releases memory and references early or guards after Free. Clearing without a previous value is still reported. Result counts too when the function has an unmanaged result type (Integer, Boolean, enumerations, classes): a preset such as Result := False before a try/except, a case or a loop whose every path assigns Result again or leaves with Exit(Value) is reported (#1345). Result is read where the routine ends normally, not when it is left through an exception.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ignore` | string-list | `[]` | Name patterns that are never reported (* and ?). |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Foo;
var
  I: Integer;
begin
  I := 0;
  I := Calc;
  Bar(I);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Foo;
var
  I: Integer;
begin
  I := Calc;
  Bar(I);
end;
```

<a id="duplicate-assignment"></a>
### duplicate-assignment

**Field or property assigned twice** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

The same field or property (Panel.Left, Self.FWert, P^.X) gets a value twice in a row without being read or anything being called in between; usually the second line should have a different target (Left/Top). Between the two only assignments to other objects with values that call nothing and do not name the target may stand; the second value must not read the target. Local variables are covered by unused-assignment, targets with an index or a call do not count. Silent for a False/True toggle (it often triggers something, like Active), for a second assignment compiled only under an additional condition ({$IFDEF DEBUG}), for different branches of the same {$IFDEF} and for properties from allowProperties. If the counterpart (Left/Top, Width/Height …) is missing from the sequence, the message suggests it. High confidence. No quick fix; jump to the first assignment.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowProperties` | string-list | `["Visible","Enabled"]` | Properties where setting twice may be intended (setter with side effects), patterns with * and ?. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Panel.Left := 8;
Panel.Left := 40;
Panel.Width := 200;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Panel.Left := 8;
Panel.Top := 40;
Panel.Width := 200;
```

<a id="declared-not-implemented"></a>
### declared-not-implemented

**Declared but not implemented** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

A method is declared in a class, record or object but has no body in the implementation section. The compiler reports this only when compiling (E2065). A body of the same name with a different header is reported by signature-mismatch instead.

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TFoo = class
    procedure Run;
  end;

implementation

end.
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TFoo = class
    procedure Run;
  end;

implementation

procedure TFoo.Run;
begin
end;

end.
```

<a id="implemented-not-declared"></a>
### implemented-not-declared

**Implemented but not declared** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

A method body TFoo.Bar has no declaration of that name in the type. A declaration of the same name with a different header is reported by signature-mismatch instead.

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TFoo = class
  end;

implementation

procedure TFoo.Run;
begin
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TFoo = class
  private
    procedure Run;
  end;

implementation

procedure TFoo.Run;
begin
end;
```

<a id="destroy-without-override"></a>
### destroy-without-override

**Destroy without override** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A destructor Destroy is declared without override. Free calls TObject.Destroy, so this destructor never runs.

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TFoo = class
    destructor Destroy;
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TFoo = class
    destructor Destroy; override;
  end;
```

<a id="setter-recursion"></a>
### setter-recursion

**Setter or getter calls itself** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

A setter assigns to its own property (Name := Value in SetName) or a getter reads it (Result := Name in GetName). The accessor calls itself endlessly and ends in a stack overflow at run time; the compiler stays silent. Quick fix: use the field instead (the field from the property declaration, otherwise F plus the name, if the class has it). Formerly recursive-property-accessor; settings and suppressions with the old ID still apply.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TKunde.SetName(const Value: string);
begin
  if Name <> Value then
    Name := Value;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TKunde.SetName(const Value: string);
begin
  if FName <> Value then
    FName := Value;
end;
```

<a id="component-create-without-override"></a>
### component-create-without-override

**Component constructor without override** · Bug · Warning · Group Components

A TComponent descendant declares constructor Create(AOwner: TComponent) without override. Components created from a form file are built through the virtual constructor and skip this one.

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TMyPanel = class(TCustomPanel)
    constructor Create(AOwner: TComponent);
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TMyPanel = class(TCustomPanel)
    constructor Create(AOwner: TComponent); override;
  end;
```

<a id="hides-virtual"></a>
### hides-virtual

**Method hides virtual method** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A method has the same name as a virtual method of an ancestor but neither overrides nor reintroduces it (compiler warning W1010).

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TBase = class
    procedure Run; virtual;
  end;
  TFoo = class(TBase)
    procedure Run;
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TBase = class
    procedure Run; virtual;
  end;
  TFoo = class(TBase)
    procedure Run; override;
  end;
```

<a id="virtual-instead-of-override"></a>
### virtual-instead-of-override

**virtual instead of override** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A descendant declares a method virtual although the ancestor already has a virtual method with the same signature; this starts a new chain instead of overriding.

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TFoo = class(TBase)
    procedure Run; virtual;
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TFoo = class(TBase)
    procedure Run; override;
  end;
```

<a id="captured-loop-variable"></a>
### captured-loop-variable

**Captured loop variable** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

An anonymous method passed to a deferred API (TThread.Queue, TTask.Run, ...) captures a variable that changes before it runs, such as a for-in loop variable. Delphi captures the variable, not its value. Silent when the anonymous method assigns the variable itself (event handlers that keep a state).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `deferredApis` | string-list | `[]` | Additional deferred APIs (besides TThread.Queue, TTask.Run and others). |

Flagged:

```pascal
for S in List do
  TThread.Queue(nil, procedure begin Memo1.Lines.Add(S); end);
```

Correct:

```pascal
function CaptureS(const AValue: string): TProc;
begin
  Result := procedure begin Memo1.Lines.Add(AValue); end;
end;
...
for S in List do
  TThread.Queue(nil, CaptureS(S));
```

<a id="component-missing-inherited"></a>
### component-missing-inherited

**Component method without inherited** · Bug · Warning · Group Components

An overridden Loaded, CreateParams, CreateWnd, Notification, SetParent, Destroy or Paint of a component does not call inherited, so the VCL skips its own work there. Paint is reported only if an ancestor's Paint draws something itself (TCustomPanel, TImage, a grid or a class from the file or index that declares Paint), with medium confidence; TCustomForm and TPaintBox only fire OnPaint at run time, so a Paint without inherited is reported there only if the unit uses OnPaint or has a form file; TCustomControl.Paint and TGraphicControl.Paint are empty, so leaving out inherited there is normal.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TMyControl.Loaded;
begin
  UpdateLayout;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TMyControl.Loaded;
begin
  inherited;
  UpdateLayout;
end;
```

<a id="component-default-mismatch"></a>
### component-default-mismatch

**default does not match constructor** · Bug · Warning · Group Components

A property declares a default value, but the constructor sets its field to a different literal. The streaming system does not store values equal to default, so the property is loaded wrongly from the DFM.

Flagged:

```pascal
property Size: Integer read FSize write FSize default 5;
...
FSize := 10;
```

Correct:

```pascal
property Size: Integer read FSize write FSize default 10;
...
FSize := 10;
```

<a id="component-missing-free-notification"></a>
### component-missing-free-notification

**Component reference without FreeNotification** · Bug · Warning · Group Components

A component property refers to another component, but its setter does not call FreeNotification. When the other component is freed, a dangling pointer remains. Subcomponents created in the constructor are ignored.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TMyControl.SetLink(const Value: TControl);
begin
  FLink := Value;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TMyControl.SetLink(const Value: TControl);
begin
  FLink := Value;
  if Value <> nil then Value.FreeNotification(Self);
end;
```

<a id="component-setter-without-invalidate"></a>
### component-setter-without-invalidate

**Setter without Invalidate** · Code smell · Hint · Group Components

A property setter changes a field that Paint reads, but does not call Invalidate (or Repaint, Refresh, Changed). The control keeps showing the old state.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TMyControl.SetColor(const Value: TColor);
begin
  FColor := Value;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TMyControl.SetColor(const Value: TColor);
begin
  FColor := Value;
  Invalidate;
end;
```

<a id="component-fixed-pixels"></a>
### component-fixed-pixels

**Fixed pixel values** · Code smell · Warning · Group Components

Paint or Resize uses fixed pixel values that are too small above 96 dpi. Values passed through ScaleValue or MulDiv or through a method of the class that scales itself (calls ScaleValue, MulDiv or a Scale... routine), 0, 1 and -1, and factors of multiplications and divisions are not reported.

Flagged:

```pascal
Canvas.FillRect(Rect(0, 0, 16, 16));
```

Correct:

```pascal
Canvas.FillRect(Rect(0, 0, ScaleValue(16), ScaleValue(16)));
```

<a id="control-fixed-bounds"></a>
### control-fixed-bounds

**Fixed control bounds** · Code smell · Warning · Group Components

Code in a VCL unit sets Width, Height, Left, Top, ClientWidth or ClientHeight to a fixed pixel value (a number or a constant of the unit such as cRowHeight = 24). Controls created in code are not scaled by the VCL, so dialogs and columns stay too small above 96 dpi. Values passed through ScaleValue, MulDiv or a method of the class that scales itself, 0, 1 and -1, factors of multiplications and divisions, values from 10000 on (order for Align), Pen.Width, Font.Height and assignments in Paint or Resize of a component (component-fixed-pixels) are not reported. The fix scales with ScaleValue of the control itself (for Margins, Padding and Constraints of the control they belong to): it is right whether the control is already parented or is scaled along when it is.

Flagged:

```pascal
mEdit.Width := 240;
```

Correct:

```pascal
mEdit.Width := ScaleValue(240);
```

<a id="component-hardcoded-color"></a>
### component-hardcoded-color

**Hardcoded color in Paint** · Code smell · Hint · Group Components

Paint uses an RGB literal, a $00BBGGRR constant or a TColor cast. Such colors ignore VCL styles; system colors like clBtnFace are fine.

Flagged:

```pascal
Canvas.Brush.Color := $00FF8000;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Canvas.Brush.Color := StyleServices.GetSystemColor(clHighlight);
```

<a id="form-field-without-object"></a>
### form-field-without-object

**Form field without object** · Bug · Warning · Group Forms and data

A published component field of the form class has no object in the .dfm/.fmx next to the unit. After loading, the field stays nil. Fields outside published are not checked.

Flagged:

```pascal
Edit1: TEdit;  // object Edit1 was deleted from the form
```

Correct:

```pascal
// remove the field or restore the object
```

<a id="form-object-without-field"></a>
### form-object-without-field

**Form object without field** · Bug · Warning · Group Forms and data

An object of the form file has no field in the form class. Inherited objects, objects inside inline frames, unnamed components and collection items are skipped. The fix adds the field.

Flagged:

```pascal
object Button2: TButton  // no Button2 in TForm1
```

Correct:

```pascal
Button2: TButton;
```

<a id="form-field-type-mismatch"></a>
### form-field-type-mismatch

**Form field type differs from object** · Bug · Warning · Group Forms and data

The field type differs from the class of the object in the form file. Without source for the class in the form file, nothing is compared. The fix changes the field type.

Flagged:

```pascal
Edit1: TEdit;  // object Edit1: TMaskEdit
```

Correct:

```pascal
Edit1: TMaskEdit;
```

<a id="security-command-injection"></a>
### security-command-injection

**Command injection** · Vulnerability · Warning · Group Security

ShellExecute, ShellExecuteEx, CreateProcess or WinExec receives an argument built with +, Format or Concat from non-constant values. Input could inject further commands. Without data flow analysis only this simple pattern is found (medium confidence).

Flagged:

```pascal
ShellExecute(0, 'open', PChar('cmd /c ' + Edit1.Text), nil, nil, SW_SHOW);
```

Correct:

```pascal
ShellExecute(0, 'open', 'tool.exe', PChar(QuotedArgument), nil, SW_SHOW);  // validated
```

<a id="security-path-traversal"></a>
### security-path-traversal

**Path traversal** · Vulnerability · Warning · Group Security

A file access (TFileStream.Create, LoadFromFile, AssignFile, TFile.*) uses a path from input data (Text, ParamStr, Request) without TPath.GetFullPath check. Only the path (first argument) counts, not the written content (WriteAllText(Path, List.Text)). Experimental until data flow analysis is available.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
List.LoadFromFile('C:\Data\' + Edit1.Text);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Path := TPath.GetFullPath(TPath.Combine(Root, Edit1.Text));  // then check it starts with Root
```

<a id="security-weak-crypto"></a>
### security-weak-crypto

**Weak cryptography** · Security hotspot · Hint · Group Security

MD5, SHA-1, DES or RC4 is used. For checksums this is fine; for passwords and signatures it is not. Low confidence, medium when the statement mentions a password, secret or signature.

Flagged:

```pascal
Stored := THashSHA1.GetHashString(PasswordText);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Stored := THashSHA2.GetHashString(Salt + PasswordText);
```

<a id="security-tls-verification-disabled"></a>
### security-tls-verification-disabled

**TLS certificate check disabled** · Vulnerability · Warning · Group Security

Indy SSLOptions.VerifyMode := [], an OnVerifyPeer handler that always returns True, or a ValidateServerCertificate handler that sets Accepted := True without a check.

Flagged:

```pascal
IdSSL.SSLOptions.VerifyMode := [];
```

Correct:

```pascal
IdSSL.SSLOptions.VerifyMode := [sslvrfPeer];
```

<a id="security-insecure-http"></a>
### security-insecure-http

**Unencrypted http:// URL** · Security hotspot · Hint · Group Security

A string contains an http:// URL. XML namespaces and schema URIs, comments and localhost, 127.0.0.1 and [::1] are skipped. The fix replaces http:// with https://.

Flagged:

```pascal
Client.Get('http://api.example.com/data');
```

Correct:

```pascal
Client.Get('https://api.example.com/data');
```

<a id="security-insecure-random"></a>
### security-insecure-random

**Random for security values** · Security hotspot · Warning · Group Security

Random is assigned to a token, password, salt, nonce or key. Random is predictable; use a cryptographic random generator. Missing Randomize is a separate topic (#153).

Flagged:

```pascal
Token := IntToStr(Random(MaxInt));
```

Correct:

```pascal
Token := TNetEncoding.Base64URL.EncodeBytesToString(CryptoRandomBytes(32));
```

<a id="security-hardcoded-credential"></a>
### security-hardcoded-credential

**Credentials in code** · Vulnerability · Warning · Group Security

A password, key or token is written into the source or the form file. Three patterns in string literals (comments do not count): Password= or Pwd= with a value in a connection string (ConnectionString, Params, Params.Add, also in the form file; no blank before =, so SQL such as WHERE Password = :Pwd stays silent), high confidence; known key formats (GitHub tokens, AWS access keys, private keys, Slack and Google keys) in any literal, also in resourcestring, high confidence; a literal assigned to or compared with a name that ends in Password, Passwd, Passwort, Kennwort, Pwd, Secret, ApiKey, AccessKey, Token or PrivateKey (const, initialized variable, :=, =, Params.Values['Password'], component property in the form file), medium confidence. Secret, key and token values count from eight characters. Empty values, placeholders ('***', 'xxx', '<…>', '%s', '{0}', 'changeme', :Param), texts with blanks and values that look like a key name stay silent. The finding never shows the value in clear text: at most the first four characters, followed by ****. Form file findings appear at the component field in the unit. No quick fix; read credentials from the configuration, the environment or a vault.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `patterns` | string-list | `[]` | Additional regular expressions for key formats of your own (e.g. \bACME-[0-9]{6}\b); they report like the built-in formats. |

Flagged:

```pascal
const
  DbPassword = 'geheim';
…
Connection.ConnectionString := 'Provider=SQLOLEDB;User ID=sa;Password=geheim';
```

Correct:

```pascal
Connection.Params.Values['Password'] := Settings.ReadString('Db', 'Password', '');
```

<a id="dfm-circular-datasource"></a>
### dfm-circular-datasource

**Circular master-detail or data source link** · Bug · Warning · Group Forms and data

MasterSource/DataSource links in the form file form a cycle (tblA → dsB → tblB → dsA → tblA). Opening the datasets recurses or never shows consistent data.

Flagged:

```pascal
tblA.MasterSource = dsB; tblB.MasterSource = dsA
```

Correct:

```pascal
tblB.MasterSource = dsA  // tblA has no master
```

<a id="dfm-master-without-fields"></a>
### dfm-master-without-fields

**Master-detail without MasterFields** · Bug · Warning · Group Forms and data

A dataset has MasterSource but no MasterFields. The detail is not filtered and shows all records.

Flagged:

```pascal
MasterSource = dsKunden
```

Correct:

```pascal
MasterSource = dsKunden; MasterFields = 'KundenID'
```

<a id="dfm-required-field-without-control"></a>
### dfm-required-field-without-control

**Required field without visible input control** · Bug · Warning · Group Forms and data

A persistent field with Required = True is bound to no visible data-aware control (a grid without DataField counts for all fields). The user cannot fill it and posting fails.

Flagged:

```pascal
tblKundenName: Required = True, no TDBEdit for Name
```

Correct:

```pascal
edName: TDBEdit; DataSource = dsKunden; DataField = 'Name'
```

<a id="dfm-control-field-type-mismatch"></a>
### dfm-control-field-type-mismatch

**Data-aware control does not fit the field type** · Bug · Warning · Group Forms and data

The control does not suit the persistent field, e.g. TDBEdit on a TBooleanField or blob field, TDBImage on a non-graphic field.

Flagged:

```pascal
edAktiv: TDBEdit; DataField = 'Aktiv'  // TBooleanField
```

Correct:

```pascal
cbAktiv: TDBCheckBox; DataField = 'Aktiv'
```

<a id="dfm-duplicate-binding"></a>
### dfm-duplicate-binding

**Two controls bound to the same field** · Bug · Warning · Group Forms and data

Two input controls use the same DataSource and DataField. Usually a copy-and-paste error. Display controls (TDBText, TDBImage) are ignored.

Flagged:

```pascal
ed1, ed2: DataSource = dsA; DataField = 'Name'
```

Correct:

```pascal
ed1: DataField = 'Name'; ed2: DataField = 'Vorname'
```

<a id="dfm-property"></a>
### dfm-property

**Component property violates a property rule** · Code smell · Warning · Group Forms and data

A component property in the form file does not meet a rule from dfmPropertyRules in atlas.json (class, property, condition, value). A property that is not stored counts with its default value; objects of the ancestor form are only reported where this form sets the value itself. The quick fix sets the value in the form file (fix: auto also with source.fixAll); the command "Apply property rules" corrects one form, the project or the group with a preview.

Flagged:

```pascal
// dfmPropertyRules: TLabel.Transparent = True
object Label1: TLabel
  Caption = 'Name'
end
```

Correct:

```pascal
object Label1: TLabel
  Caption = 'Name'
  Transparent = True
end
```

<a id="foreign-form-control-access"></a>
### foreign-form-control-access

**Access to controls of another form** · Code smell · Warning · Group Forms and data

Code accesses a field of a form or frame from another unit through its global variable (Form2.Edit1.Text). This couples the units tightly; use a method or property of the form.

Flagged:

```pascal
Form2.Edit1.Text := 'x';
```

Correct:

```pascal
Form2.Kundenname := 'x';
```

<a id="security-sql-injection"></a>
### security-sql-injection

**SQL text built from values (SQL injection)** · Vulnerability · Warning · Group Security

SQL.Text, SQL.Add, CommandText, ExecSQL or Open receives text built with + or Format from non-constant values. User input could inject SQL. Use parameters (:Name, ParamByName). Constants, resourcestrings, IntToStr and QuotedStr are not reported; user input (TEdit.Text, TMemo.Lines) raises the confidence to high.

Flagged:

```pascal
Q.SQL.Text := 'select * from K where N = ''' + Edit1.Text + '''';
```

Correct:

```pascal
Q.SQL.Text := 'select * from K where N = :N';
Q.ParamByName('N').AsString := Edit1.Text;
```

<a id="slash-command"></a>
### slash-command

**Quick command not executed** · Code smell · Hint · Group Atlas internal

A line starting with /name was not executed and stays in the code: the command is unknown, switched off (slashCommands.disabled) or does not fit at this place. The message names the reason. "/ignore rule" is also reported when that rule finds nothing in the line below.

Flagged:

```pascal
/gibtsnicht X
```

Correct:

```pascal
/uses System.Classes
```

<a id="empty-except"></a>
### empty-except

**Empty except block** · Code smell · Warning · Group Exceptions

An except block without statements swallows every exception. A block that contains only a comment counts as deliberate and is not reported.

Flagged:

```pascal
try
  Save;
except
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
try
  Save;
except
  on E: EInOutError do
    Log(E.Message);
end;
```

<a id="interface-guid"></a>
### interface-guid

**Interface without GUID** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

An interface declares no GUID. Supports, as and QueryInterface need one. The confidence is high when the interface is used with them in the same file, otherwise low; with such a use Delphi reports E2232 there (like E2232). The quick fix inserts a new GUID. A dispinterface without GUID does not compile; dispinterface-invalid reports it.

Matches the compiler message `E2232`.

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  ISaver = interface
    procedure Save;
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  ISaver = interface
    ['{6C1D2A57-0E3B-4F8A-9B2D-5A7E4C3F1D08}']
    procedure Save;
  end;
```

<a id="duplicate-interface-guid"></a>
### duplicate-interface-guid

**Two interfaces with the same GUID** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

Two interfaces in the file declare the same GUID, usually after copying a declaration. Supports and as then mix them up. The quick fix generates a new GUID.

Flagged:

```pascal
ILoader = interface
  ['{6C1D2A57-0E3B-4F8A-9B2D-5A7E4C3F1D08}']
end;
ISaver = interface
  ['{6C1D2A57-0E3B-4F8A-9B2D-5A7E4C3F1D08}']
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
ILoader = interface
  ['{6C1D2A57-0E3B-4F8A-9B2D-5A7E4C3F1D08}']
end;
ISaver = interface
  ['{0B9E4F21-7C6D-4A3E-8F15-2D9C7B6A4E31}']
end;
```

<a id="file-header"></a>
### file-header

**File header missing or different** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The file does not start with the header from the rule option "template". Off by default; switch it on in atlas.json with a template. The quick fix inserts the header.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `template` | string | `""` | Expected file header; placeholders as documented. |

Flagged:

```pascal
unit Tools;
```

Correct:

```pascal
// Copyright (c) Example GmbH
unit Tools;
```

<a id="hardcoded-string"></a>
### hardcoded-string

**Text in code instead of resourcestring** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A string literal with words for people stands in a statement. Move it to a resourcestring so it can be translated. Silent for texts nobody translates: arguments of logging and check calls (…Log, Log…, Diagnose, Trace, Debug, Assert, OutputDebugString), comparison and registry calls (StartsText, Pos, ReadString …), masks of FormatDateTime and FormatFloat, markup (HTML from <, JSON from { or "key":, Markdown headings # and ##) and Font.Name; option ignoreCalls names further routines. Off by default, low confidence.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
ShowMessage('File not found');
```

Correct:

```pascal
resourcestring
  SFileNotFound = 'File not found';
...
ShowMessage(SFileNotFound);
```

<a id="self-assignment"></a>
### self-assignment

**Assignment to itself** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A variable, field or property is assigned to itself. Usually a different value was meant, for example a parameter or another field. If a parameter or local variable hides a field of the same name (Name := Name), Atlas offers Self.Name := Name as the fix.

Flagged:

```pascal
FName := FName;
```

Correct:

```pascal
FName := AName;
```

<a id="for-high-to-low"></a>
### for-high-to-low

**for loop counts down with to** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

The loop starts at the upper bound (High, Count - 1) and ends at the lower one, but uses to. It never runs; downto was meant. Also reports loops with constant bounds that never run, such as for I := 10 to 1, with signed, hexadecimal or character literals (like H2135). Equal bounds, expressions, named constants and ascending bounds stay silent. Quick fix: replace to with downto.

Matches the compiler message `H2135`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
for I := 10 to 1 do
  Countdown(I);
```

Correct:

```pascal
for I := 10 downto 1 do
  Countdown(I);
```

<a id="conditional-expression-version"></a>
### conditional-expression-version

**Conditional expression before Delphi 13** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

The conditional expression X := if C then A else B exists only from Delphi 13 on; a project that must still compile with Delphi 12 or older fails here (like E2029). Reported is the outermost expression, not inside {$IF} branches that test CompilerVersion, RTLVersion, VERnnn or Declared. Silent without a known Delphi version. Quick fix (also for the whole unit): convert to an if statement, the statement repeated per branch; not offered when the rest of the statement has side effects.

Matches the compiler message `E2029`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
Caption := if Done then 'Ready' else 'Busy';
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Done then
  Caption := 'Ready'
else
  Caption := 'Busy';
```

<a id="exception-not-raised"></a>
### exception-not-raised

**Exception created but not raised** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

An exception object is created as a statement on its own. Without raise nothing happens and the object leaks.

Flagged:

```pascal
if X = nil then
  EArgumentException.Create('X missing');
```

Correct:

```pascal
if X = nil then
  raise EArgumentException.Create('X missing');
```

<a id="assert-side-effect"></a>
### assert-side-effect

**Assert with side effect** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

The argument of Assert changes data (Inc, Dec, a Try… function with an out parameter or a method from the option mutatingMethods). With {$C-} the compiler removes the whole call including the side effect.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mutatingMethods` | string-list | `["Add","Delete","Remove","Extract","Clear","Insert"]` | Methods that change their object. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Assert(List.Remove(X) >= 0);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Index := List.Remove(X);
Assert(Index >= 0);
```

<a id="ifthen-side-effect"></a>
### ifthen-side-effect

**IfThen evaluates both branches** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

IfThen is a function: both branches are evaluated before the call. A nil access or a function call in the branch that is not chosen still happens. High confidence when a branch dereferences a name checked in the condition, medium for a function call.

Flagged:

```pascal
S := IfThen(Assigned(X), X.Name, '');
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Assigned(X) then S := X.Name else S := '';
```

<a id="random-without-randomize"></a>
### random-without-randomize

**Random without Randomize** · Code smell · Hint · Group Robustness and patterns

Random is used, but the unit never calls Randomize, so every start yields the same sequence. A Random of your own (enumeration value, routine) is not System.Random. Randomize often sits in the .dpr, so the rule is off by default and has low confidence.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
Dice := Random(6) + 1;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Randomize; // once at program start
...
Dice := Random(6) + 1;
```

<a id="int32-mul-overflow"></a>
### int32-mul-overflow

**Int32 multiplication assigned to Int64** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

Both operands have at most 32 bits, so the product is computed in 32 bits and overflows before it is assigned to the Int64 variable. Cast one operand to Int64.

Flagged:

```pascal
Total := Width * Height; // Total: Int64; Width, Height: Integer
```

Correct:

```pascal
Total := Int64(Width) * Height;
```

<a id="sizeof-pointer"></a>
### sizeof-pointer

**SizeOf of a pointer in Move or FillChar** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

SizeOf of a pointer, string or dynamic array is the size of the reference (4 or 8 bytes), not of the data. Move and FillChar then copy or clear too little.

Flagged:

```pascal
FillChar(Buffer[0], SizeOf(Buffer), 0); // Buffer: TBytes
```

Correct:

```pascal
FillChar(Buffer[0], Length(Buffer) * SizeOf(Buffer[0]), 0);
```

<a id="char-to-pchar"></a>
### char-to-pchar

**Char cast to PChar** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

PChar(C) with a Char variable turns the character code into an address. A string was meant.

Flagged:

```pascal
ShowMessage(PChar(C)); // C: Char
```

Correct:

```pascal
ShowMessage(PChar(string(C)));
```

<a id="unrelated-class-cast"></a>
### unrelated-class-cast

**Hard cast between unrelated classes** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

TButton(Obj) checks nothing. If the static type of Obj is neither an ancestor nor a descendant of TButton (e.g. TEdit or TForm), the access reads memory of an object with a different layout. Reported only when both types are known for sure, both inheritance chains are known up to TObject and both declarations are always compiled together with the cast. Interfaces, pointers, numbers, TObject as source and casts to or from class references (class of) stay silent, as does the accessor class trick: the target and its ancestors up to a common ancestor declare neither fields nor new virtual methods (THackControl = class(TControl)), so the layout is the same. High confidence. Quick fix: (Obj as TButton) checks the type at run time.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowTypes` | string-list | `[]` | Types that may be hard cast on purpose: a type name (as source or target) or a pair "Source=Target". |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TForm1.EditExit(Sender: TObject);
var
  mEdit: TEdit;
begin
  mEdit := Sender as TEdit;
  TButton(mEdit).Caption := 'OK';
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TForm1.EditExit(Sender: TObject);
var
  mEdit: TEdit;
begin
  mEdit := Sender as TEdit;
  (mEdit as TButton).Caption := 'OK';
end;
```

<a id="first-char-address"></a>
### first-char-address

**Address of the first character via S[1]** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

@S[1] and PChar(@S[1]) raise a range check error for an empty string with {$R+}; without range checking they yield nil or garbage. The index access also forces a unique copy (UniqueString). PChar(S) and Pointer(S) are safe for empty strings too. Applies to string, UnicodeString, WideString, AnsiString, RawByteString and UTF8String, also S[Low(S)]; ShortString, arrays and other indexes stay silent. Reported only when the type of S is known for sure. High confidence. Quick fix for const parameters: replace with PChar(S) (PAnsiChar for AnsiString types), a surrounding PChar(…), PAnsiChar(…), PWideChar(…), PByte(…) or Pointer(…) is replaced as a whole. For other strings the pointer may be used for writing, where the unique copy is intended, so there is no quick fix and the message names UniqueString(S).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Send(const AText: string);
begin
  WriteBuffer(@AText[1], Length(AText) * SizeOf(Char));
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Send(const AText: string);
begin
  WriteBuffer(PChar(AText), Length(AText) * SizeOf(Char));
end;
```

<a id="float-equality"></a>
### float-equality

**Floating-point comparison with = or <>** · Bug · Hint · Group Find bugs

Floating-point values are rarely exactly equal after calculations. Compare with SameValue from System.Math and a tolerance. Comparisons with 0 are not reported.

Flagged:

```pascal
if Total = Expected then
```

Correct:

```pascal
if SameValue(Total, Expected) then
```

<a id="constructor-on-instance"></a>
### constructor-on-instance

**Constructor called on an instance** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

Create is called on a variable instead of the class. That does not create a new object; with an unassigned variable it crashes. Class references (class of …) are not reported.

Flagged:

```pascal
List.Create; // List: TStringList
```

Correct:

```pascal
List := TStringList.Create;
```

<a id="format-without-settings"></a>
### format-without-settings

**Formatting depends on the locale** · Code smell · Hint · Group Robustness and patterns

Format with %f, %n, %m, %g or %e and functions like FloatToStr, StrToFloat or DateToStr without a TFormatSettings parameter use the user's locale (decimal separator, date format). For files, protocols and logs pass fixed TFormatSettings. Format('%d', …) is not reported, nor are FormatDateTime and FormatFloat with a fixed picture without separators (: / . ,), such as 'yyyy"-"mm"-"dd' or 'ddddd t'. Own routines of the same name stay silent when name resolution finds them: local routines, methods of the own class, routines of the own unit and of a unit that comes after System.SysUtils in uses.

Flagged:

```pascal
Line := Format('%.2f', [Amount]);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Line := Format('%.2f', [Amount], TFormatSettings.Invariant);
```

<a id="format-args"></a>
### format-args

**Format string does not match its arguments** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

Format, FmtStr, FormatBuf, StrFmt, StrLFmt, WideFormat, WideFmtStr, Exception.CreateFmt or CreateFmtHelp and TStringBuilder.AppendFormat with a known format string (a literal, literals joined with +, a const or resourcestring of the file or the index) whose placeholders do not match the argument list [...]: an invalid placeholder (%q, '%' or '%5' at the end, %0: without a type letter), fewer arguments than placeholders, or an argument of the wrong type: %d %u %x take only integers, %e %f %g %n %m only floating-point numbers, %s strings, characters and variants, %p pointers and objects, a * for width or precision an integer; variants fit everywhere. Boolean and enumeration values are reported at every type letter with Ord(...) or BoolToStr(..., True) as advice. All of this raises EConvertError at runtime. An argument behind the last used one is reported with medium confidence (only without an index in the format string), it is never printed. Types are only checked when they are known for sure; format strings from variables, index * and ranges stay silent. Own routines named Format stay silent when name resolution finds them. Shares its format string parser with format-without-settings. No quick fix. High confidence.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `formatMethods` | string-list | `[]` | Further routines with a format string followed by an array of const argument list, e.g. "TLogger.LogFmt" or "Protokoll". With a dot the method name after any qualifier counts when name resolution cannot find the routine. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Result := Format('%d orders for %s', [ACustomer.Name]);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Result := Format('%d orders for %s', [ACustomer.OrderCount, ACustomer.Name]);
```

<a id="generic-exception-raised"></a>
### generic-exception-raised

**Generic exception raised** · Code smell · Hint · Group Exceptions

Raising the base class Exception prevents callers from handling the error specifically. Declare a dedicated exception class.

Flagged:

```pascal
raise Exception.Create('Customer missing');
```

Correct:

```pascal
raise ECustomerMissing.Create('Customer missing');
```

<a id="catch-all-without-raise"></a>
### catch-all-without-raise

**Catch-all handler without re-raise** · Code smell · Warning · Group Exceptions

A handler for Exception that neither re-raises nor ends the program swallows every error, including programming errors. Off by default; intended for library code.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
except
  on E: Exception do
    Log(E.Message);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
except
  on E: Exception do
  begin
    Log(E.Message);
    raise;
  end;
end;
```

<a id="exception-freed"></a>
### exception-freed

**Exception object freed manually** · Bug · Error · Group Exceptions

The RTL frees an exception object after its handler or after raise. Freeing it manually causes a double free.

Flagged:

```pascal
on E: Exception do
  E.Free;
```

Correct:

```pascal
on E: Exception do
  Log(E.Message);
```

<a id="string-concat-in-loop"></a>
### string-concat-in-loop

**String concatenation in a loop** · Code smell · Hint · Group Performance

A string variable is extended step by step inside a loop. Delphi usually grows strings in place, so this is reported only with low confidence and is off by default; for many iterations TStringBuilder is faster. Silent when the variable is assigned anew earlier in every iteration, directly in the loop body and not behind then, else or a case label (it does not grow across the loop).

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
for I := 1 to 10000 do
  S := S + IntToStr(I);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Builder := TStringBuilder.Create;
try
  for I := 1 to 10000 do
    Builder.Append(I);
  S := Builder.ToString;
finally
  Builder.Free;
end;
```

<a id="loop-invariant-call"></a>
### loop-invariant-call

**Expensive call in a loop condition** · Code smell · Hint · Group Performance

The condition of while and until is evaluated on every iteration. Calls like Trim or Format and properties like Lines.Text there are recomputed each time; compute them once before the loop. Bounds of for loops are evaluated only once and are not reported, nor is .Text when it is a field (record field of a type in the same file, or a field or a property reading a field according to the name resolution), nor a property whose receiver the loop changes (Items[I].Text), nor FileExists and DirectoryExists (waiting for a file), nor calls whose argument the loop changes (assignment, Inc, Dec, Delete, SetLength, Include, Exclude). Off by default.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
while I < Length(Trim(Memo.Lines.Text)) do
  Inc(I);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Text := Trim(Memo.Lines.Text);
while I < Length(Text) do
  Inc(I);
```

<a id="missing-begin-update"></a>
### missing-begin-update

**Adding items in a loop without BeginUpdate** · Code smell · Hint · Group Performance

Items.Add, Lines.Add and similar calls in a loop repaint the control on every call. Wrap the loop in BeginUpdate and try … finally EndUpdate. Silent for menus (TMenuItem has no BeginUpdate, also through fields such as FParts.Menu.Items, including records nested in the class), for lists without a control (TObjectList<T>, TList, TDictionary), for types in the index without BeginUpdate and for for loops with at most 10 iterations (fixed bounds or a static array of the same file). Off by default.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
for I := 1 to 1000 do
  ListBox1.Items.Add(IntToStr(I));
```

Correct:

```pascal
ListBox1.Items.BeginUpdate;
try
  for I := 1 to 1000 do
    ListBox1.Items.Add(IntToStr(I));
finally
  ListBox1.Items.EndUpdate;
end;
```

<a id="setlength-in-loop"></a>
### setlength-in-loop

**Array grows by one element in a loop** · Code smell · Hint · Group Performance

SetLength(A, Length(A) + 1) inside a loop copies the dynamic array on every iteration. The rule only reports when A is surely a dynamic array (array of, TArray<T>, an array type of the file or a known RTL type such as TBytes), never for strings. In a for loop the count is known: the message names it, and when A is a local variable not used before the loop and only written as A[High(A)] or A[Length(A) - 1] inside it, the quick fix sets the length once before the loop and indexes with the loop variable. Otherwise collect the values in a TList<T>.

Flagged:

```pascal
for I := 0 to Liste.Count - 1 do
begin
  SetLength(Namen, Length(Namen) + 1);
  Namen[High(Namen)] := Liste[I].Name;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
SetLength(Namen, Liste.Count);
for I := 0 to Liste.Count - 1 do
  Namen[I] := Liste[I].Name;
```

<a id="managed-param-without-const"></a>
### managed-param-without-const

**Managed parameter without const** · Code smell · Hint · Group Performance

A value parameter of type string, interface, dynamic or open array, or a record of the file with at least minRecordSize bytes, that the routine never changes. Every call pays reference counting or a copy; const avoids both. Changed means: assigned, a part assigned (characters, fields, elements), passed to var or out, @Param, a method call on a record, a for loop variable. The rule only reports when this is certain: calls with unknown signature (methods without the symbol index, overloads), with blocks, captures in anonymous methods, bare inherited and asm make it stay silent. Virtual, dynamic, abstract and message methods, calling conventions, external and exported routines, methods of classes implementing interfaces, published methods and methods used as values (event handlers, @Routine, form file) are skipped because their signature is given from outside; overrides only with ignoreOverrides = false and then without fix. The fix adds const in the implementation and the declaration and splits parameter groups when needed. With the project files (IDE, MCP) a use as a value in units that use this unit counts too, such as an assignment to a TFunc<string, …> whose parameter has no const; without them (command line without project) the compiler reports such references after the fix.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `minRecordSize` | integer | `16` | Report record parameters from this size in bytes on. |
| `ignoreOverrides` | boolean | `true` | Do not report overrides; their signature is given by the ancestor. |

Flagged:

```pascal
function Formatieren(Text: string; Kunde: TKundenDaten): string;
begin
  Result := Text + Kunde.Name;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function Formatieren(const Text: string; const Kunde: TKundenDaten): string;
begin
  Result := Text + Kunde.Name;
end;
```

<a id="bool-compare"></a>
### bool-compare

**Comparison with True or False** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

Comparing a Boolean with True or False is redundant; use the expression or not directly.

Flagged:

```pascal
if Visible = True then
  Show;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Visible then
  Show;
```

<a id="redundant-assigned-free"></a>
### redundant-assigned-free

**Assigned check before Free** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

Free already checks for nil, so testing Assigned before Free or FreeAndNil is redundant.

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix redundant-assigned-free`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
if Assigned(List) then
  List.Free;
```

Correct:

```pascal
List.Free;
```

<a id="raise-in-finally"></a>
### raise-in-finally

**raise in a finally block** · Bug · Warning · Group Exceptions

An exception raised in a finally block replaces the exception that is currently propagating, which is then lost.

Flagged:

```pascal
try
  Work;
finally
  raise Exception.Create('Fehler');
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
try
  Work;
finally
  Cleanup;
end;
```

<a id="redundant-parens"></a>
### redundant-parens

**Redundant parentheses** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

Parentheses around a whole condition or a single identifier add nothing. Off by default.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
if (Done) then
  Exit;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Done then
  Exit;
```

<a id="redundant-cast"></a>
### redundant-cast

**Redundant type cast** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

TStringList(List) with List: TStringList, X as TFoo with X: TFoo or Integer(Count) with Count: Integer change nothing. Likewise TObject(Obj).Free: Free calls the virtual destructor and needs no cast (option checkFree). Reported only when the type of the source is known for sure and is declared with exactly the target name; aliases (TColorIndex = Integer), constants, routines without a call, overloaded routines as source, arguments of overloaded calls (the cast may steer the overload), as with interfaces (QueryInterface) and Free redeclared in the class chain stay silent. High confidence. Quick fix: remove the cast.

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix redundant-cast`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `checkFree` | boolean | `true` | Also report a class cast that only precedes Free (CastAndFree). |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Cleanup;
begin
  TStringList(FLines).Clear;
  TObject(FStream).Free;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Cleanup;
begin
  FLines.Clear;
  FStream.Free;
end;
```

<a id="redundant-begin-end"></a>
### redundant-begin-end

**begin … end around a single statement** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A begin … end block that holds exactly one statement can be removed. Off by default.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Silent while [`missing-begin-end`](#missing-begin-end) is on.

Flagged:

```pascal
if Done then
begin
  Exit;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Done then
  Exit;
```

<a id="with-statement"></a>
### with-statement

**with statement** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

Inside a with block it is unclear which object an identifier belongs to, and a new member of the with object silently changes the meaning. Write the expression out or use a local variable.

Flagged:

```pascal
with Customer do
  Name := Edit.Text;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Customer.Name := Edit.Text;
```

<a id="metric-method-length"></a>
### metric-method-length

**Method too long** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The method has more code lines than the limit (default 60). Blank lines, comments, compiler directives and local routines do not count. Split the method into smaller ones. Set the limit with the option max, e.g. "rules": {"metric-method-length": {"max": 80}}.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `60` | Highest allowed number of statement lines. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Run;
begin
  // 120 lines: read, check, convert and save
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Run;
begin
  Read;
  Check;
  Convert;
  Save;
end;
```

<a id="metric-nesting-depth"></a>
### metric-nesting-depth

**Nesting too deep** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The method nests if, case, loops and try deeper than the limit (default 4). else if stays on the level of its if. Leave early with Exit or Continue, or move inner parts into their own methods. Set the limit with the option max.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `4` | Highest allowed nesting depth. |

Flagged:

```pascal
for Item in Items do
  if Item.Active then
    for Line in Item.Lines do
      if Line.Valid then
        try
          Save(Line);
        except
          Log;
        end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
for Item in Items do
  if Item.Active then
    SaveLines(Item);
```

<a id="metric-cyclomatic-complexity"></a>
### metric-cyclomatic-complexity

**Cyclomatic complexity too high** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The cyclomatic complexity of the method is above the limit (default 15). It starts at 1 and adds 1 for every if, while, repeat and for, every case branch without else, every and/or in a condition and every except on; an except without on counts once. Many paths need many tests. Set the limit with the option max.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `15` | Highest allowed cyclomatic complexity. |

Flagged:

```pascal
case Kind of
  1: A;
  2: B;
  // ... 20 branches
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Handlers[Kind]();
```

<a id="metric-cognitive-complexity"></a>
### metric-cognitive-complexity

**Cognitive complexity too high** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The cognitive complexity of the method is above the limit (default 15). Following SonarSource, if, case, loops and except handlers count 1 plus their nesting level; else, else if, goto and every sequence of equal logical operators count 1. Nested code is harder to read than code in sequence. Set the limit with the option max.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `15` | Highest allowed cognitive complexity. |

Flagged:

```pascal
if A then
  for I := 0 to N do
    if B and C or D then
      while E do
        F;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if not A then
  Exit;
for I := 0 to N do
  ProcessItem(I);
```

<a id="metric-parameter-count"></a>
### metric-parameter-count

**Too many parameters** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The method has more parameters than the limit (default 6). Group related values in a record or class. Set the limit with the option max.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `6` | Highest allowed number of parameters. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Book(const AName, AStreet, ACity, AZip: string; AAmount: Currency; ADate: TDate; ANote: string);
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Book(const AAddress: TAddress; const AOrder: TOrder);
```

<a id="metric-boolean-operands"></a>
### metric-boolean-operands

**Condition with too many operands** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A condition of if, while, until or a conditional expression joins more operands with and, or and xor than the limit (default 5); parentheses and not are looked through, a comparison or call counts as one operand. The finding sits on the condition. Quick fix: Extract condition to function (#50) turns the whole condition into a Boolean function (a method of the class or a routine before the current one) with the local names it reads as const parameters. Also shown in the metrics lens.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `5` | Highest allowed number of operands in one condition. |

Flagged:

```pascal
if (Kunde.Aktiv) and (Kunde.Land = 'DE') and (Betrag > 0) and
   (not Gesperrt) and (Datum >= Start) and (Datum <= Ende) then
```

Correct:

```pascal
if IstBuchbar(Kunde, Betrag, Gesperrt, Datum, Start, Ende) then
```

<a id="metric-local-count"></a>
### metric-local-count

**Too many local variables** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The routine declares more local variables than the limit (default 12): names in its var section, inline variables and for var loops. Local routines and anonymous methods count on their own. Many locals usually mean the routine does several things; extract parts into own methods. Also shown in the metrics lens.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `12` | Highest allowed number of local variables. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Abschluss;
var
  Summe, Netto, Steuer, Rabatt, Skonto, Porto: Currency;
  Kunde, Konto, Beleg, Datei, Zeile, Text, Pfad: string;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Abschluss;
var
  Beleg: TBeleg;
begin
  Beleg := BerechneBeleg;
  SchreibeBeleg(Beleg);
```

<a id="metric-exit-points"></a>
### metric-exit-points

**Too many exit points** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The routine leaves more often through Exit and raise than the limit (default 4); Exit(Value) and a bare raise in an except block count too. Local routines and anonymous methods count on their own. Many exits make the flow hard to follow; combine guard clauses or extract parts. Also shown in the metrics lens.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `4` | Highest allowed number of Exit and raise statements. |

Flagged:

```pascal
if A = nil then Exit;
if B = nil then Exit;
if not Valid then raise EError.Create('...');
if Done then Exit(True);
if Cancel then Exit(False);
```

Correct:

```pascal
if not Ready(A, B) then
  Exit;
Result := Process(A, B);
```

<a id="todo-comment"></a>
### todo-comment

**Open TODO comment** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A comment starts with a marker for open work: // TODO, // FIXME or the IDE to-do format { TODO 1 -oOwner -cCategory : Text }. The hint shows marker, priority, category, owner and text, so open work appears in the problem list. Off by default; switch it on in atlas.json, e.g. "rules": {"todo-comment": {"severity": "hint"}}. Completed entries ({ DONE … }) are never reported. The option keywords sets the markers (default ["TODO", "FIXME"]); it also applies to get_todos and --todos.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `keywords` | string-list | `["TODO","FIXME"]` | Markers of open TODO entries (letters only). |

Flagged:

```pascal
// TODO 1 -oMarco -cGUI : set focus
Edit.Text := '';
```

Correct:

```pascal
Edit.Text := '';
Edit.SetFocus;
```

<a id="debug-display-placeholder"></a>
### debug-display-placeholder

**Debugger display names an unknown member** · Bug · Warning · Group Robustness and patterns

A debugger display rule above a type (// atlas-debug: '{Name}, {City}', [AtlasDebugDisplay('…')], // atlas-debug-children: Name, City or [AtlasDebugChildren('…')]) names a placeholder or child that is not a field, property or method of the type or one of its ancestors, or the template is invalid (unclosed or empty brace). In the debugger such a placeholder stays empty ({?Name}). Ancestors come from the unit and the index; when an ancestor is unknown the rule stays silent for that type. Members of TObject (ClassName, ToString, …) are always known.

Flagged:

```pascal
// atlas-debug: '{Name}, {Stadt}'
TKunde = class
  Name, Ort: string;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
// atlas-debug: '{Name}, {Ort}'
TKunde = class
  Name, Ort: string;
end;
```

<a id="component-palette-icon"></a>
### component-palette-icon

**Registered component without palette icon** · Code smell · Hint · Group Components

Every class passed to RegisterComponents needs a bitmap named after the class in upper case in a .dcr or .res included with {$R}. From Delphi 11 the sizes 16, 24 and 32 px (NAME16, NAME24, NAME32) are expected as well. Silent when the unit includes no readable resource file.

Flagged:

```pascal
{$R *.dcr} // without TMYBUTTON
RegisterComponents('Samples', [TMyButton]);
```

Correct:

```pascal
{$R *.dcr} // with TMYBUTTON16, TMYBUTTON24, TMYBUTTON32
RegisterComponents('Samples', [TMyButton]);
```

<a id="unresolved-identifier"></a>
### unresolved-identifier

**Unresolved identifier** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

An identifier in a statement is declared neither in the unit nor in the units it uses (like compiler error E2003). Checked while typing, without the compiler; with blocks, asm and anonymous methods are skipped. Old object syntax: in New(P, Init) and Dispose(P, Done) the second argument is a constructor or destructor of the object type behind P; if that type lacks it, the rule reports like E2601 or E2602, and Fail outside a constructor like E2078.

Matches the compiler message `E2003`, `E2601`, `E2602`, `E2078`.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Foo;
begin
  Lst.Add('x');
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Foo;
var
  Lst: TStringList;
begin
  Lst := TStringList.Create;
  Lst.Add('x');
end;
```

<a id="deprecated-symbol"></a>
### deprecated-symbol

**Deprecated or bound symbol** · Code smell · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

A routine, type, constant or member marked deprecated, experimental, platform or library is used (like W1000, W1003, W1002, W1001), or a unit whose header carries one of these hints is listed in uses (like W1006, W1007, W1005, W1004); the deprecated automated section of a class counts as deprecated symbol, too (like W1000). Reported only when the name resolves for sure. The editor strikes deprecated names through; the message carries the reason from deprecated 'reason'. The declaration and the head of its implementation are not reported.
