Whether a kind is reported follows the state of the warning as in dcc: all eight are on by default, then the project option of the .dproj (DCC_SYMBOL_PLATFORM = false and so on), then the switches {$WARN SYMBOL_DEPRECATED OFF|ON|ERROR|DEFAULT}, {$WARN UNIT_PLATFORM …} and so on and {$WARNINGS OFF} from their position; a switch in an {$IF} branch that is not surely compiled with the position keeps it silent. The option kinds narrows the kinds further. The .NET directive {$UNSAFECODE ON|OFF} is reported like a platform symbol (like W1002), as dcc for Windows does. Quick fix: replace with the name the reason gives (or, for IncludeTrailingBackslash and ExcludeTrailingBackslash, the platform-independent routine) when exactly one name fits, is visible at that position and is of the same kind. Without a project index only the symbols of the file itself are checked.

Matches the compiler message `W1000`, `W1001`, `W1002`, `W1003`, `W1004`, `W1005`, `W1006`, `W1007`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `kinds` | string-list | `["deprecated","experimental","platform","library"]` | Reported kinds: deprecated, experimental, platform, library (all four by default; a warning switched off in the project or file stays silent anyway). |

Flagged:

```pascal
Pfad := IncludeTrailingBackslash(Basis) + Name;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Pfad := IncludeTrailingPathDelimiter(Basis) + Name;
```

<a id="implicit-package-import"></a>
### implicit-package-import

**Implicit package import** · Code smell · Warning · Group Packages and tests

In a package (.dpk) a unit from contains uses a unit that is neither in contains nor provided by a package in requires. The compiler pulls it in implicitly (W1033), which later causes duplicate units when another package also contains it. The quick fix adds the unit to contains.

Flagged:

```pascal
package Werkzeuge;
requires rtl;
contains
  uHaupt in 'uHaupt.pas';  // uHaupt uses uMathe
end.
```

Correct:

```pascal
package Werkzeuge;
requires rtl;
contains
  uHaupt in 'uHaupt.pas',
  uMathe in 'uMathe.pas';
end.
```

<a id="type-used-before-declaration"></a>
### type-used-before-declaration

**Type used before its declaration** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

A class, interface, record or other type is used in a type declaration (field, parameter, result, property, generic argument, class of) but declared only further down in the same scope (like compiler error E2003). Ancestors, aliases and pointer types are not reported, nor is class of to a class declared further down in the same type block (dcc accepts that) or a name that appears further down only as an alias of the same-named type of another unit (TX = UnitY.TX: before it, UnitY.TX from uses applies). Quick fixes: add a forward declaration at the start of the type block, merge type blocks (const, var and label sections in between move in front of the first block) or move a record up in front of its first user; without a safe fix the message gives the reason.

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TTree = class
    FRoot: TNode;
  end;
  TNode = class
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TNode = class;
  TTree = class
    FRoot: TNode;
  end;
  TNode = class
  end;
```

<a id="unneeded-forward-declaration"></a>
### unneeded-forward-declaration

**Unneeded forward declaration** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A forward declaration whose type is not used before its full declaration in the same type block. Quick fix: remove it.

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TNode = class;
  TNode = class
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TNode = class
  end;
```

<a id="field-not-freed"></a>
### field-not-freed

**Field created but never freed** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

An object field that the constructor (or a private method it calls) creates with TClass.Create is never freed in the class (no Free, FreeAndNil, Destroy or DisposeOf): a memory leak. Components created with an owner other than nil, interface and record fields and the classes from the option ignoreTypes are exempt; TObjectList and TObjectDictionary fields are normal fields. Medium confidence. Quick fixes: free the field in the destructor, or free all owned objects of the class; a missing destructor is created with override and inherited. The objects are freed before inherited in reverse creation order, as Free or FreeAndNil (refactoring.freeFieldsStyle). Up to #918 the rule was called owned-field-not-freed; settings and suppressions with that id still apply.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ignoreTypes` | string-list | `[]` | Classes that are freed elsewhere; generic types match without type parameters. |

Flagged:

```pascal
constructor TImport.Create;
begin
  FZeilen := TStringList.Create;
  FFehler := TStringList.Create;
end;

destructor TImport.Destroy;
begin
  FZeilen.Free;
  inherited;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
destructor TImport.Destroy;
begin
  FFehler.Free;
  FZeilen.Free;
  inherited;
end;
```

<a id="owned-field-free-unclear"></a>
### owned-field-free-unclear

**Unclear whether a created field must be freed** · Bug · Hint · Group Find bugs

An object field created in the constructor and not freed is also returned (Result := FField, Exit(FField)), handed to a property of another object or to its Add…, Insert…, Register…, Attach…, Push or Enqueue, assigned without Create or writable through a property (write FField). Whether the class still owns the object is up to the developer, so there is no quick fix. Silent when the receiver owns its elements: a TObjectList, TObjectStack, TObjectQueue or TComponentList field not created with False, or a field whose class (from the file or the index) holds such a list for the element type. TArray<T> fields are dynamic arrays and never reported.

Flagged:

```pascal
function TCache.Take: TList;
begin
  Result := FList;  // created in Create, never freed
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function TCache.Take: TList;
begin
  Result := FList;
  FList := nil;  // ownership passes to the caller
end;
```

<a id="operator-precedence"></a>
### operator-precedence

**Precedence of not overlooked** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

In Pascal not binds more tightly than in and comparisons: not X in Set means (not X) in Set, not A = B means (not A) = B, and on integers not works bitwise. The rule reports these forms when the declared type of the operand shows that this cannot be meant (not Boolean). Quick fix: add parentheses, not (X in Set).

Flagged:

```pascal
if not Zustand in [zsOffen, zsGesperrt] then
  Bearbeiten;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if not (Zustand in [zsOffen, zsGesperrt]) then
  Bearbeiten;
```

<a id="empty-statement"></a>
### empty-statement

**Empty statement after then, do or else** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A semicolon right after then, do or else ends the statement: the following line always runs, or the loop spins empty. An empty begin … end after then, do or else is reported too, unless it contains an explanatory comment (option allowCommented). A semicolon right before end, until, except or finally changes nothing and is not reported. Quick fix: remove the semicolon.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowCommented` | boolean | `true` | Do not report begin { intentionally empty } end. |

Flagged:

```pascal
if Liste.Count = 0 then;
  raise EKeineDaten.Create('Keine Daten');
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Liste.Count = 0 then
  raise EKeineDaten.Create('Keine Daten');
```

<a id="loop-off-by-one"></a>
### loop-off-by-one

**Loop runs one step too far** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

for I := 0 to List.Count or to Length(A) over zero-based lists and arrays reads one element past the end when the body indexes List[I] or A[I]. With one-based strings it is the other way round: S[0] (Delphi reports E2157) and for I := 0 to Length(S) read before the first character; {$ZEROBASEDSTRINGS ON} is taken into account. Quick fix: add - 1 or use High(A); for strings Low(S) and High(S). The option countNames lists the names that return a count.

Matches the compiler message `E2157`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `countNames` | string-list | `["Count","Length"]` | Names that return a count (property like Count or function like Length). |

Flagged:

```pascal
for I := 0 to Kunden.Count do
  Ausgabe(Kunden[I].Name);
```

Correct:

```pascal
for I := 0 to Kunden.Count - 1 do
  Ausgabe(Kunden[I].Name);
```

<a id="collection-modified-in-loop"></a>
### collection-modified-in-loop

**List modified inside its own loop** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

Delete, Remove, Add or another method from the option mutatingMethods is called on the list that the loop is walking forwards by index or with for … in. Forwards, Delete skips the next element and runs past the end, Add is not visited and Insert visits the same element twice; with for … in the RTL raises an exception. A Break or Exit without a condition after the call in the same block ends the loop and stays silent (a Break in an inner loop does not count), downto loops too. Quick fix for index loops: rewrite as downto; for for … in collect the elements and change the list after the loop.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mutatingMethods` | string-list | `["Add","Insert","Delete","Remove","Extract","Clear"]` | Methods that change the list. |

Flagged:

```pascal
for I := 0 to Liste.Count - 1 do
  if Liste[I].Erledigt then
    Liste.Delete(I);
```

Correct:

```pascal
for I := Liste.Count - 1 downto 0 do
  if Liste[I].Erledigt then
    Liste.Delete(I);
```

<a id="use-after-free"></a>
### use-after-free

**Object used after Free** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

After X.Free, X.Destroy, X.DisposeOf, FreeAndNil(X) or Dispose(X), X is used again without a new assignment: a method, field, index or X^, with X, and after Free also Assigned(X) (always True) or a second Free. The rule follows the data flow of the routine; an assignment to X, passing X to a routine, @X and after FreeAndNil a check for nil end the path, ways only through a possible exception are left out. Confidence high when release and access stand one after the other in the same block, otherwise medium. No automatic fix; the action jumps to the release.

Flagged:

```pascal
Bericht.Free;
Protokoll.Add('Seiten: ' + IntToStr(Bericht.Seiten));
```

Correct:

```pascal
Protokoll.Add('Seiten: ' + IntToStr(Bericht.Seiten));
Bericht.Free;
```

<a id="deref-before-nil-check"></a>
### deref-before-nil-check

**Used first, checked for nil later** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

X is dereferenced (X.Field, X.Method, X^) and only afterwards checked with Assigned(X), X <> nil or X = nil. Either the check is superfluous or the access before it can crash. Reported only when every path to the check passes the access and X was not checked before; a new assignment, FreeAndNil(X), passing X or one of its fields or elements (X.Field, X[I]) to a routine and, for fields and global variables, any call in between end the path. Medium confidence. When the check is an if without else directly after the access in the same block, the quick fix moves the access into it.

Flagged:

```pascal
Titel := Kunde.Name;
if Assigned(Kunde) then
  Anzeigen(Kunde);
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Assigned(Kunde) then
begin
  Titel := Kunde.Name;
  Anzeigen(Kunde);
end;
```

<a id="field-free-without-nil"></a>
### field-free-without-nil

**Field freed, reference stays** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

FField.Free in a method outside the destructor, and a path to the end of the method does not set the field again. A later access or a second Free hits freed memory. Setting it again means an assignment or the call of a method of the same class that assigns the field. Fields are those of the class declaration in the unit. Destructors, methods with [TearDown] or [TearDownFixture], methods only called from these and the methods from the option ignoreMethods are exempt. Medium confidence. Quick fix: FreeAndNil(FField).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ignoreMethods` | string-list | `["Destroy","BeforeDestruction"]` | Methods in which Free without nil is allowed. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Zuruecksetzen;
begin
  FCache.Free;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Zuruecksetzen;
begin
  FreeAndNil(FCache);
end;
```

<a id="missing-inherited"></a>
### missing-inherited

**Constructor or destructor without inherited** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A constructor, or an override of Destroy, AfterConstruction or BeforeDestruction, does not call inherited, in any class whose ancestor is not TObject. Constructors are reported only if an ancestor declares a constructor of its own (file, index or a known RTL/VCL class) and the constructor does not call another constructor of the same class. For components, Destroy is reported only by component-missing-inherited. High confidence. Quick fix: insert inherited; at the end of Destroy and BeforeDestruction, at the start of constructors and AfterConstruction; a constructor with parameters without override gets inherited Create;.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `methods` | string-list | `["Create","Destroy","AfterConstruction","BeforeDestruction"]` | Checked methods; Create stands for all constructors whose name starts with Create. |

Flagged:

```pascal
destructor TExport.Destroy;
begin
  FDatei.Free;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
destructor TExport.Destroy;
begin
  FDatei.Free;
  inherited;
end;
```

<a id="inherited-order"></a>
### inherited-order

**inherited in the wrong place** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

In a destructor, inherited (inherited; or inherited Destroy) stands as a statement of the method body before a later statement frees a field of the class itself (F.Free, F.DisposeOf, FreeAndNil(F), also with Self.): the ancestor may still call virtual methods that need the field. Classes whose chain contains a class with Thread in its name stay silent, because TThread.Destroy waits for Execute and belongs at the start there. In a constructor, the statements before inherited access a field or property inherited from an ancestor (resolved through the file and the index; own fields, parameters and local variables do not count), while the ancestor is not set up yet. missing-inherited reports a missing inherited; this rule the order. Medium confidence. Quick fix: move inherited to the end of the destructor or the start of the constructor; not when the arguments of inherited Create use local variables set before.

Flagged:

```pascal
destructor TExport.Destroy;
begin
  inherited;
  FDatei.Free;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
destructor TExport.Destroy;
begin
  FDatei.Free;
  inherited;
end;
```

<a id="inherited-wrong-method"></a>
### inherited-wrong-method

**inherited calls a different method** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

In the overridden method Speichern there is inherited Laden(…) instead of inherited Speichern(…), usually after copying a method. Only calls count (inherited Name as a statement or with parentheses); inherited Caption without parentheses may be a property. Silent in constructors (inherited Create(nil), CreateNew), in methods without override and when the method also calls itself in the ancestor (inherited; or inherited Speichern), which makes the other call intentional. Pairs from the option allowed stay silent. High confidence. Quick fix: replace the name with the method's own, when the number of arguments matches its parameters.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowed` | string-list | `[]` | Allowed pairs "Method>inherited method", patterns with * and ?, e.g. "Speichern>Pruefen". |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TKundenForm.Speichern;
begin
  inherited Laden;
  Protokollieren;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TKundenForm.Speichern;
begin
  inherited Speichern;
  Protokollieren;
end;
```

<a id="getter-side-effect"></a>
### getter-side-effect

**Getter changes the state** · Code smell · Warning · Group Find bugs

The read method of a property assigns fields or properties of its own object (FAbrufe, Self.FWert, inherited fields as well), calls Inc/Dec/Include/Exclude on them or passes a newly created object to a method of a field (FListe.Add(TEintrag.Create)). The debugger evaluates getters when displaying them and so changes the state. Lazy initialization is allowed: an enclosing if tests something that its branch assigns (if FListe = nil then FListe := …, if Result = nil then … FListe := Result, if not FGeladen then … FGeladen := True). Local variables with the same name as a field, Result, methods without a property and anonymous methods stay silent. One message per getter and field. Medium confidence. No quick fix.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowLazyInit` | boolean | `true` | Allow lazy initialization in the getter (if FListe = nil then FListe := …). |

Flagged:

```pascal
function TZaehler.GetWert: Integer;
begin
  Inc(FAbrufe);
  Result := FWert;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function TZaehler.GetWert: Integer;
begin
  Result := FWert;
end;
```

<a id="equals-without-gethashcode"></a>
### equals-without-gethashcode

**Equals without GetHashCode** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A class overrides Equals but not GetHashCode (or the other way round). In TDictionary and THashSet with the default comparer equal objects then do not find each other. Silent when an ancestor overrides the counterpart, when the ancestor chain is not known completely and for Equals without override. Also applies to records of the file with class operator Equal used as keys of TDictionary, TObjectDictionary, THashSet or TMultiMap without a comparer (Create without arguments or only with constants such as capacity or ownership): the default comparer compares records byte by byte, which is only wrong for fields of type string, Variant, floating point, interface or TArray; records with only such ordinal fields stay silent. High confidence. Quick fix for classes: declare GetHashCode after Equals and implement it after the body of Equals with THashBobJenkins over the own fields that Equals compares (case-insensitively via LowerCase when Equals uses SameText), adding System.Hash to uses.

Flagged:

```pascal
  TKundenNr = class
    function Equals(Obj: TObject): Boolean; override;
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
  TKundenNr = class
    function Equals(Obj: TObject): Boolean; override;
    function GetHashCode: Integer; override;
  end;
```

<a id="destroy-called-directly"></a>
### destroy-called-directly

**Destroy called instead of Free** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

Obj.Destroy is called directly as a statement. If Obj is nil, the call crashes; Free checks for nil first and is the usual way to release an object. inherited Destroy and calls of a destructor with arguments are exempt. High confidence. Quick fix: Destroy becomes Free.

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix destroy-called-directly`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
Verbindung.Destroy;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Verbindung.Free;
```

<a id="managed-type-raw-memory"></a>
### managed-type-raw-memory

**FillChar, Move or GetMem on managed types** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

FillChar, ZeroMemory, FillMemory, Move, CopyMemory, GetMem or FreeMem on a variable whose type contains strings, interfaces, dynamic arrays or variants (records, static arrays and aliases are resolved in the file and in the index). Reference counts are overwritten or skipped: leaks, double frees, access violations. Only plain variables (X, @X, P^) are checked; array elements such as S[1] and types that cannot be resolved stay silent. High confidence. Quick fixes: FillChar(X, SizeOf(X), 0) becomes X := Default(T), Move(A, B, SizeOf(T)) becomes B := A, GetMem(P, SizeOf(T)) becomes New(P) and FreeMem(P) becomes Dispose(P); otherwise Initialize and Finalize help.

Flagged:

```pascal
FillChar(Adresse, SizeOf(Adresse), 0);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Adresse := Default(TAdresse);
```

<a id="threadvar-managed"></a>
### threadvar-managed

**threadvar with a managed type** · Bug · Warning · Group Threads

Delphi does not finalize threadvar variables. A string, interface, dynamic array or variant in a threadvar (also inside a record or static array) leaks in every thread that ends. Types are resolved in the file and in the index. High confidence. No quick fix: release the value at the end of the thread (for example set it to '' or nil in TThread.Execute), or store a pointer or a fixed-size buffer instead.

Flagged:

```pascal
threadvar
  LetzteMeldung: string;
```

Correct:

```pascal
threadvar
  LetzteMeldung: PChar;
```

<a id="pchar-of-temporary"></a>
### pchar-of-temporary

**PChar of a temporary string** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

PChar (PWideChar, PAnsiChar) of a temporary string, such as a concatenation with a string (S + '.bak') or a function result (IntToStr(N)), is stored in a variable, a field or Result, or passed to PostMessage, PostThreadMessage, SendNotifyMessage or SendMessageCallback (also behind LPARAM(…)). The temporary string is released after the statement, so the pointer points to freed memory. Passing it to an ordinary call such as CopyFile is fine. High confidence. Quick fix for local variables: keep the intermediate result in an inline string variable.

Flagged:

```pascal
Ziel := PChar(Pfad + '.bak');
CopyFile(PChar(Pfad), Ziel, False);
```

Correct:

```pascal
var ZielText: string := Pfad + '.bak';
Ziel := PChar(ZielText);
CopyFile(PChar(Pfad), Ziel, False);
```

<a id="out-param-read"></a>
### out-param-read

**out parameter read before it is set** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

An out parameter is read before the routine sets it on some path through the control flow. For managed types (string, interfaces, dynamic arrays, Variant) the caller has already cleared it, so the old value is gone; for other types the value is undefined. Usually var was meant. Uses the control flow graph cannot follow (passing the parameter to a routine, setting a field of it, @Param) stay silent. High confidence. Quick fix: out becomes var in the implementation and in the declaration.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Anhaengen(out Text: string);
begin
  Text := Text + sLineBreak + 'Ende';
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Anhaengen(var Text: string);
begin
  Text := Text + sLineBreak + 'Ende';
end;
```

<a id="noreturn-returns"></a>
### noreturn-returns

**noreturn routine can return normally** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A routine marked noreturn (Delphi 13 and later) may only end through raise, Abort, Halt, RunError or a call to another noreturn routine. When a path reaches the end of the body or an Exit, the routine returns normally; the compiler has dropped the code after the call, the behaviour is undefined. The directive counts on the implementation and on the declaration (interface, class). Calls whose target neither the file, the standard routines nor the symbol index knows might be noreturn themselves and end the path too, so only paths made of known statements are reported. Incomplete control flow (asm, syntax errors) stays silent. Exit is reported at the statement, the end of the body at the routine name. High confidence. No quick fix.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Fehler(const AText: string); noreturn;
begin
  if AText <> '' then
    raise EImport.Create(AText);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Fehler(const AText: string); noreturn;
begin
  if AText = '' then
    raise EImport.Create('Unbekannter Fehler');
  raise EImport.Create(AText);
end;
```

<a id="stringlist-unsorted"></a>
### stringlist-unsorted

**Find or Duplicates on an unsorted TStringList** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

Find on a TStringList or THashedStringList that is created in the unit but never sorted: Find searches binary and returns wrong results on an unsorted list. The same holds for Duplicates := dupIgnore or dupError, which only works on sorted lists. Sorted counts as Sorted := True (or any value other than False), Sort, CustomSort, Create(Duplicates, True, ...) and with List do. A list passed to a routine or assigned to another variable stays silent. Locals are tracked per routine, fields across the unit. Medium confidence. Quick fixes: Sorted := True after the creation (not offered when the list uses Insert, Move or Exchange, which raise on sorted lists) and, for if List.Find(S, I) then, I := List.IndexOf(S) with if I >= 0.

Flagged:

```pascal
Namen := TStringList.Create;
Namen.LoadFromFile(Datei);
if Namen.Find(Suche, Index) then
  Namen.Delete(Index);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Namen := TStringList.Create;
Namen.Sorted := True;
Namen.LoadFromFile(Datei);
if Namen.Find(Suche, Index) then
  Namen.Delete(Index);
```

<a id="writeln-in-gui-app"></a>
### writeln-in-gui-app

**Writeln in a program without console** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

Write, Writeln, Read or Readln on standard input or output in a program without a console: at run time Writeln raises EInOutError (I/O error 105), Readln I/O error 104. The application type comes from DCC_ConsoleTarget in the .dproj and {$APPTYPE CONSOLE} in the .dpr (also inside {$IFDEF}); units are only checked when they belong to the loaded project, a .dpr without project on its own. Libraries and packages stay silent. Calls with a text file as first argument, calls under if IsConsole, routines of the same name declared in the unit and Read/Write inside methods (TStream.Read) are exempt. High confidence. Quick fix for Write and Writeln: replace with OutputDebugString(PChar(...)) and add Winapi.Windows; numbers and Boolean go through IntToStr, FloatToStr and BoolToStr. Option replacement sets another target, e.g. Log.Info or Logger.Log(TLogLevel.Info, %s). No fix for width specifiers (X:5) or arguments of unknown type.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `replacement` | string | `"OutputDebugString"` | Replacement for Write and Writeln in the quick fix; %s stands for the text. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TfrmHaupt.btnStartClick(Sender: TObject);
begin
  Writeln('Start: ', TimeToStr(Now));
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TfrmHaupt.btnStartClick(Sender: TObject);
begin
  OutputDebugString(PChar('Start: ' + TimeToStr(Now)));
end;
```

<a id="exception-in-destructor"></a>
### exception-in-destructor

**raise in a destructor** · Bug · Warning · Group Exceptions

raise in a destructor (also class destructor) that no except inside the destructor catches. The exception aborts the destruction: inherited Destroy no longer runs, the object stays half freed, and in a finally of the caller it hides the original exception. A raise in the try part of a try..except is caught; raise in the except or finally part, including a bare raise; to re-raise, is reported. Nested routines and anonymous methods are exempt. Calls that raise are not followed. High confidence. No quick fix: log the state or release it without an exception.

Flagged:

```pascal
destructor TSitzung.Destroy;
begin
  if FOffen then
    raise ESitzung.Create('Sitzung noch offen');
  inherited;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
destructor TSitzung.Destroy;
begin
  if FOffen then
    Schliessen;
  inherited;
end;
```

<a id="duplicate-condition"></a>
### duplicate-condition

**Duplicate condition or identical branches** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

The same condition twice in an if ... else if chain (the later branch is never reached), identical then and else branches, the same operand twice in an and, or or xor chain, and comparisons of an expression with itself (X = X, X < X). Usually a copy error. Expressions are compared by their tokens without whitespace, comments and case. Chains with calls that may change state (anything but SameText, Length, Assigned, Pos and similar pure functions, or Read, Next, Pop, Random ...) stay silent, as do float self-comparisons (NaN check X <> X) and comparisons on class members such as TFoo.Shared = TFoo.Shared. High confidence. No quick fix; the action jumps to the first occurrence.

Flagged:

```pascal
if Betrag > 1000 then
  Stufe := 3
else if Betrag > 500 then
  Stufe := 2
else if Betrag > 1000 then
  Stufe := 1;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Betrag > 1000 then
  Stufe := 3
else if Betrag > 500 then
  Stufe := 2
else if Betrag > 100 then
  Stufe := 1;
```

<a id="case-missing-enum-value"></a>
### case-missing-enum-value

**case over an enumeration without all values** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A case over an enumerated type without else does not cover all values; a value added later silently falls through. The message names the missing values. The type of the selector (variable, parameter, field or chain A.B) comes from name resolution, the values from the index or the unit itself; aliases are followed. Ranges (bsNeu..bsBezahlt) and lists count as present. Selectors with calls, integer and unknown types stay silent. With option requireElse a case with else is reported as well. High confidence. Quick fix "Add missing values (n)" adds empty branches in declaration order before else or end, with type prefix when the labels use one ({$SCOPEDENUMS ON}).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `requireElse` | boolean | `false` | Also report case statements with else when values are missing. |

Flagged:

```pascal
case Bestellung.Status of
  bsNeu: Anlegen;
  bsBezahlt: Versenden;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
case Bestellung.Status of
  bsNeu: Anlegen;
  bsBezahlt: Versenden;
  bsStorniert: ;
  bsZurueck: ;
end;
```

<a id="pair-without-finally"></a>
### pair-without-finally

**Paired calls without try/finally** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

Generalises lock-without-finally (whose lock pairs stay there) to other pairs: DisableControls/EnableControls, BeginUpdate/EndUpdate, FindFirst/FindClose (also in if FindFirst(...) = 0 then begin ... end), Reset, Rewrite or Append/CloseFile, GetMem or AllocMem/FreeMem and Screen.Cursor := ... with a later Screen.Cursor := .... Reported when the closing call stands unprotected later in the same statement list with at least one statement in between; an exception there leaves the state hanging. Both calls must use the same receiver or argument. Overlapping pairs are reported together. High confidence. Quick fix: wrap the statements in between in try/finally with the closing calls, when the closing calls follow each other directly. Option pairs adds own pairs.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pairs` | json | `[]` | Additional pairs as [{"open": "Freeze", "close": "Thaw"}], optionally with openArg/closeArg. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Screen.Cursor := crHourGlass;
Tabelle.DisableControls;
Importieren;
Tabelle.EnableControls;
Screen.Cursor := crDefault;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Screen.Cursor := crHourGlass;
Tabelle.DisableControls;
try
  Importieren;
finally
  Tabelle.EnableControls;
  Screen.Cursor := crDefault;
end;
```

<a id="processmessages-in-loop"></a>
### processmessages-in-loop

**Application.ProcessMessages in loops** · Code smell · Hint · Group Threads

Application.ProcessMessages inside a for, while or repeat loop, or directly in an event handler (a method with a Sender parameter), opens the door for re-entrancy: the user clicks again, closes the form or starts the operation twice. Calls inside anonymous methods are left to thread-vcl-access; other objects' ProcessMessages methods are ignored. Medium confidence, no quick fix: move the work to a thread or TTask, or disable the trigger until it is done. Option allowIn lists methods where it is allowed.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowIn` | string-list | `[]` | Methods in which ProcessMessages is allowed, as Name or Class.Name. |

Flagged:

```pascal
while not Done do
begin
  Step;
  Application.ProcessMessages;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
StartButton.Enabled := False;
TTask.Run(
  procedure
  begin
    while not Done do
      Step;
    TThread.Queue(nil, procedure begin StartButton.Enabled := True; end);
  end);
```

<a id="unchecked-conversion"></a>
### unchecked-conversion

**Unchecked conversion of user input** · Bug · Hint · Group Find bugs

StrToInt, StrToInt64, StrToFloat, StrToDate, StrToDateTime or StrToCurr applied directly to user input (Edit.Text, Grid.Cells[...], Memo.Lines[...], also inside Trim) outside the try part of a try/except. Invalid input raises EConvertError with an English message. try/finally does not count as protection. Medium confidence. Quick fix for X := StrToInt(Edit.Text); as its own statement, when X is a variable or field of the matching type: if not TryStrToInt(Edit.Text, X) then raise EConvertError.Create('X ist keine Zahl'). format-without-settings additionally reports locale-dependent conversions without TFormatSettings. Options functions and sources choose the checked conversions and the properties that count as input.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `functions` | string-list | `["StrToInt","StrToInt64","StrToFloat","StrToDate","StrToDateTime","StrToCurr"]` | Checked conversions. |
| `sources` | string-list | `["Text","Cells","Lines"]` | Properties that count as user input. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Menge := StrToInt(edtMenge.Text);
```

Correct:

```pascal
if not TryStrToInt(edtMenge.Text, Menge) then
  raise EConvertError.Create('Menge ist keine Zahl');
```

<a id="dictionary-get-without-check"></a>
### dictionary-get-without-check

**Dictionary access without key check** · Bug · Hint · Group Find bugs

Dict[Key] or Dict.Items[Key] is read on a TDictionary or TObjectDictionary (also through a type alias of the file) although neither ContainsKey nor TryGetValue checked the same key earlier in the routine. The rule stays silent when the routine fills the dictionary itself beforehand (Add, AddOrSetValue, TryAdd, Dict[K] := ...) or when the key stems from Keys or the pairs (for K in Dict.Keys, Names := Dict.Keys.ToArray, for Pair in Dict with K := Pair.Key). A missing key raises EListError. Medium confidence. Quick fix for X := Dict[Key]; as its own statement, when X is a variable of the value type: if not Dict.TryGetValue(Key, X) then X := 0 (or '', False, Default(T)).

Flagged:

```pascal
Preis := Preise[Artikel.Nummer];
```

Correct:

```pascal
if not Preise.TryGetValue(Artikel.Nummer, Preis) then
  Preis := 0;
```

<a id="shadowed-name"></a>
### shadowed-name

**Local name shadows field or property** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A parameter, local or inline variable has the same name as a field or property of the class (also inherited and visible there, resolved through the file and the symbol index) or as a global variable of the unit declared before the routine. Especially treacherous in form methods: Caption, Name, Tag, Left. If VCL is not in the index, the members of TComponent and TControl are known when the ancestor chain ends at a VCL base class. Members inherited from RTL, VCL or another library (unit from the library path) are only reported where they could be meant: a local variable that is only assigned or only read, a value parameter that is assigned. Helper variables such as Left, Text or Name that are read and written stay silent; option frameworkMembers reports them as before. Parameters of event handlers (with Sender) and of methods with override or message are skipped because the event type, the ancestor or the message dictates the signature. High confidence. Quick fix: rename to AName (parameter, also in the declaration) or LName (local variable) within the routine; with prefixes in the project file .atlas (naming) these are used instead, e.g. mName. Option ignore lists name patterns that are never reported (default Sender, Value).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ignore` | string-list | `["Sender","Value"]` | Name patterns (* and ?) that are never reported. |
| `frameworkMembers` | boolean | `false` | Report every local name that shadows a member inherited from RTL, VCL or another library, not only suspicious ones. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TfrmKunde.Laden(const Caption: string);
begin
  Caption := Caption + ' (geladen)';
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TfrmKunde.Laden(const ACaption: string);
begin
  Caption := ACaption + ' (geladen)';
end;
```

<a id="naming-prefix"></a>
### naming-prefix

**Name without the prefix of its kind** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A local variable (also inline and for var), field (also class var), parameter or constant (const, also local, in classes and inline; not resourcestring) lacks the prefix of its kind. Defaults: local m, field F, parameter A, constant c; after a prefix ending in a letter an upper-case letter or digit must follow (mIndex, not menu). Global variables are not checked. Per kind the prefix comes from the option in atlas.json, else from the project file .atlas (naming, also constant), else the default; an empty value switches the kind off. Off by default: the rule runs only when atlas.json switches it on. Exceptions: one-letter loop counters, Sender, Self and Result, names matching ignore, public fields (public, published and fields of a record before its first visibility section: the F belongs to fields behind properties, not to TPoint.X), fields before the first visibility section of a form, frame or data module (components), parameters of methods with override or message, of published methods, of event handlers (a parameter named Sender) and of methods implementing an interface method (in the file or the symbol index; unknown interface: silent). Parameters are reported at the declaration only. Quick fix: rename to the expected name in the routine or the block after an inline declaration, for parameters also in declaration and implementation, for private fields and class constants in the class and its methods (also Self.Name and TType.Name), for implementation constants in the whole unit. No fix when the new name already occurs where the rename applies (the routine with its parameter list, for fields the class and its methods, for implementation constants the file), the scope contains with, a nested routine redeclares the name, or the name is visible outside the file. While the rule runs, other quick fixes and refactorings build new names with these prefixes (introduce constant → cNewConstant, introduce variable and resolve with → mValue, parameters of “create method” → APreis), so the rule does not report them right away.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `local` | string | `"m"` | Prefix of local variables; empty = not checked. |
| `field` | string | `"F"` | Prefix of fields; empty = not checked. |
| `parameter` | string | `"A"` | Prefix of parameters; empty = not checked. |
| `constant` | string | `"c"` | Prefix of constants; empty = not checked. |
| `ignore` | string-list | `[]` | Name patterns (* and ?) that are never reported, e.g. fields of API records. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TKunde.Laden(const Datei: string);
var
  Zeilen: TStringList;
begin
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TKunde.Laden(const ADatei: string);
var
  mZeilen: TStringList;
begin
end;
```

<a id="boolean-assign-if"></a>
### boolean-assign-if

**if that only assigns True or False** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

if B then X := True else X := False (also with begin/end and the values swapped), if B then Exit(True) else Exit(False) and if B then Exit(True); followed by Exit(False); can be written directly. Complements bool-compare. High confidence. Quick fix: X := B, X := not B or Exit(B); negations are simplified (not B becomes B), compound conditions are wrapped in parentheses. Without a fix when comments stand inside the if.

Flagged:

```pascal
if Betrag > 0 then
  Result := True
else
  Result := False;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Result := Betrag > 0;
```

<a id="case-insensitive-compare"></a>
### case-insensitive-compare

**Comparison through UpperCase or LowerCase** · Code smell · Hint · Group Performance

UpperCase(A) = UpperCase(B), LowerCase(A) <> 'x', AnsiUpperCase(...) or A.ToUpper = B.ToUpper creates two strings for one comparison. SameText (AnsiSameText for the Ansi functions and the Unicode string helpers) is shorter and faster. A literal is only accepted if the conversion does not change it; UpperCase(A) = LowerCase(B) and UpperCase(A) = 'ja' are left alone because they are different mistakes. Does not replace the string helper modernisation of #71. High confidence. Quick fix: SameText(A, B) or not SameText(A, B).

Flagged:

```pascal
if UpperCase(Land) = UpperCase(Standard) then
```

Correct:

```pascal
if SameText(Land, Standard) then
```

<a id="magic-number"></a>
### magic-number

**Number without a name in code** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

Number literals (decimal, $hex, floating point, with sign) in statements, outside const sections, types, array bounds and subranges. Numbers that speak for themselves stay silent: the DPI base 96 in MulDiv, full bit masks ($FF, $FFFF, $FFFFFFFF), time units 24, 60, 1000 and 3600 in *, /, div and mod, and arguments of calls in an array literal whose arguments are all numbers (point lists such as [P(3.5, 1.5), P(9.5, 1.5)]); SetBounds(12, 9, 360, 23) is still reported. Allowed values are configurable; DFM coordinates and tests can be excluded through exclude. Off by default and low confidence: it shows up once the rule is switched on and minConfidence is low. Quick fix: Introduce constant (#50) in the const section of the routine.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowed` | number-list | `[0,1,-1,2,10,100]` | Numbers that are never reported. |

Flagged:

```pascal
if Tage > 42 then
  Mahnen;
```

Correct:

```pascal
const
  Mahnfrist = 42;
...
if Tage > Mahnfrist then
  Mahnen;
```

<a id="line-length"></a>
### line-length

**Line too long** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

Lines longer than the limit max (default 120 characters, matching the rulers from #710). Tabs count up to the next tab stop of format.indentSize. Lines that are only too long because of a string or, with ignoreComments, a comment are not reported. Lines over 1023 characters are always reported as a warning regardless of max, because depending on the Delphi version the compiler stops there; lines over 4095 characters as an error, because Delphi 13 stops there too (F2069). Off by default. Quick fix: Wrap line after commas or before binary operators, where the pieces then fit.

Matches the compiler message `F2069`.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `120` | Maximum line length in characters. |
| `ignoreStrings` | boolean | `true` | Do not report lines that are only too long because of a string. |
| `ignoreComments` | boolean | `false` | Do not report lines that are only too long because of a comment. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Ergebnis := Rechner.Berechne(Kunde.Rabattstufe, Bestellung.Positionen, Lager.Verfuegbarkeit(Artikel), Steuer.Satz(Land));
```

Correct:

```pascal
Ergebnis := Rechner.Berechne(Kunde.Rabattstufe, Bestellung.Positionen, Lager.Verfuegbarkeit(Artikel),
  Steuer.Satz(Land));
```

<a id="metric-class-size"></a>
### metric-class-size

**Class with too many members** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The class has more members than the limit (default 40): field names, methods, properties with their own type and class var. Properties that only raise visibility (property Align;) and nested types do not count; a nested class is measured on its own. Also shown in the metrics lens above the class. Split the class or move parts into helper classes; Move class to own unit (#103) helps when the unit is crowded too.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `40` | Maximum number of members per class. |

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TfrmHaupt = class(TForm)
    { 87 members }
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TfrmHaupt = class(TForm)
    { 25 members; data access in TKundenDaten }
  end;
```

<a id="metric-unit-length"></a>
### metric-unit-length

**Unit too long** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The unit has more lines than the limit (default 2000), counted up to the final end. Only units are measured, not programs or packages. Also shown in the metrics lens above the unit name. Move classes into their own units with Move class to own unit (#103).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `2000` | Maximum number of lines per unit. |

Flagged:

```pascal
unit Kasse.Haupt;  { 2140 lines }
```

Correct:

```pascal
unit Kasse.Haupt;  { 900 lines; TBeleg in Kasse.Beleg }
```

<a id="metric-uses-count"></a>
### metric-uses-count

**Too many units in uses** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The unit uses more units than the limit (default 30), interface and implementation together. The finding sits on the first uses. Programs and libraries are not checked, because their uses lists the units of the project. Also shown in the metrics lens above uses. Many dependencies usually mean the unit does too much; Move class to own unit (#103) splits it.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `30` | Maximum number of units in all uses clauses. |

Flagged:

```pascal
uses
  { 34 units in interface and implementation }
```

Correct:

```pascal
uses
  { 12 units; reports in Kasse.Berichte }
```

<a id="metric-inheritance-depth"></a>
### metric-inheritance-depth

**Inheritance too deep** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The class has more ancestors of its own than the limit (default 5). Ancestors from RTL, VCL, FMX and other libraries do not count (by unit name or because the index loaded them from the library path), nor does TObject. Ancestors are looked up in the file and in the symbol index. If the chain breaks at an unknown ancestor before reaching a library, the value is a lower bound and the message says at least; it is only reported when that bound already exceeds the limit. Units of a library itself are not checked. The finding sits on the class name; the metrics lens shows the depth next to the member count.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max` | integer | `5` | Highest allowed number of own ancestors. |

Flagged:

```pascal
TBasis = class(TForm) ... TStufe6 = class(TStufe5)  { 6 own ancestors }
```

Correct:

```pascal
TStufe2 = class(TBasis)
  FDruck: IDruck;  { composition instead of more levels }
```

<a id="public-field"></a>
### public-field

**Public field** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

Fields in the public or published section of a class. The section without a visibility keyword only counts for classes without an ancestor or with TObject, because the form designer puts its fields there. Records are excluded unless includeRecords is set; class var and helper classes declared under a private or protected type section are not reported. Off by default. Quick fix: Encapsulate field moves the field as FName into a private section at the end of the class and adds property Name read FName write FName in the old section; accesses from outside stay valid.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `includeRecords` | boolean | `false` | Also report fields of records. |

Flagged:

```pascal
TKunde = class
public
  Name: string;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TKunde = class
private
  FName: string;
public
  property Name: string read FName write FName;
end;
```

<a id="legacy-tlist"></a>
### legacy-tlist

**Non-generic list with cast** · Code smell · Information · Group Style and metrics

A non-generic TList (System.Classes) or TObjectList (System.Contnrs) whose items are read with a cast: T(List[I]), T(List.Items[I]), T(List.First), List[I] as T and so on. TList<T> and TObjectList<T> from System.Generics.Collections check the type at compile time and need no cast. The rule reports once per list, at its declaration when it is in this file, otherwise at the first cast; with several target types, all of them are named. Only when name resolution finds the list type and the target type for sure; casts to Pointer are ignored. Off by default; for code that must still compile with old Delphi versions, use exclude. Quick fix (#1105): converts the list to TList<T> or TObjectList<T> in one step: declaration, every Create (arguments are kept), every cast and System.Generics.Collections in the uses clause; Contnrs is removed when nothing else uses it. Only when every read has the same cast and no member without a generic counterpart is used (Sort with TListSortCompare, Assign with TListAssignOp, List), no Pointer or Integer values are stored, Add, Extract, Remove and IndexOf get T, and the list is not passed on. If the list is visible outside the unit, the fix also rewrites the other units of the project group or is not offered; "fix all" (source.fixAll) never applies such fixes. Otherwise the finding hover names the reason. Stacks and queues: legacy-stack-queue.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
FPositionen: TList;
...
Pos := TPosition(FPositionen[I]);
```

Correct:

```pascal
FPositionen: TList<TPosition>;
...
Pos := FPositionen[I];
```

<a id="legacy-stack-queue"></a>
### legacy-stack-queue

**Non-generic stack or queue with cast** · Code smell · Information · Group Style and metrics

A non-generic TStack, TQueue, TObjectStack or TObjectQueue (System.Contnrs) whose items are read with a cast: T(Stack.Pop), T(Stack.Peek), Queue.Pop as T and so on. TStack<T>, TQueue<T>, TObjectStack<T> and TObjectQueue<T> from System.Generics.Collections check the type at compile time and need no cast. Like legacy-tlist, the rule reports once per stack or queue, at its declaration when it is in this file, otherwise at the first cast; with several target types, all of them are named. Only when name resolution finds the type and the target type for sure; casts to Pointer are ignored. Off by default; for code that must still compile with old Delphi versions, use exclude. Quick fix: converts in one step like legacy-tlist; for queues Push becomes Enqueue and Pop becomes Dequeue. TObjectStack<T> and TObjectQueue<T> own their objects, the Contnrs classes do not: the fix therefore creates them with Create(False) and reads with Extract, because Pop and Dequeue are procedures there that free the object. Without a Create in the unit, with different casts or with members without a generic counterpart (AtLeast) there is no fix; the finding hover names the reason.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
FOffen: TObjectStack;
...
Knoten := TKnoten(FOffen.Pop);
```

Correct:

```pascal
FOffen: TObjectStack<TKnoten>;
...
FOffen := TObjectStack<TKnoten>.Create(False);
...
Knoten := FOffen.Extract;
```

<a id="global-variable"></a>
### global-variable

**Global variable in interface** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

var or threadvar in the interface section of a unit: every unit that uses it can read and change the value. The form variable created by the designer (frmMain: TfrmMain, for classes derived from TForm, TFrame, TDataModule or any class in a unit with a form file) is excluded unless includeFormVariables is set. Off by default. Move the variable into the implementation section and offer a function or property instead.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `includeFormVariables` | boolean | `false` | Also report the form variables created by the designer. |

Flagged:

```pascal
interface
var
  AktuellerBenutzer: TBenutzer;
```

Correct:

```pascal
interface
function AktuellerBenutzer: TBenutzer;
implementation
var
  GBenutzer: TBenutzer;
```

<a id="commented-out-code"></a>
### commented-out-code

**Commented-out code** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

At least minLines consecutive comment lines (// per line or a multi-line { } comment) that parse as statements, declarations or class members. Prose at the start and end of the block is ignored, and at least half the lines must look like code (;, :=, begin, end, then, do). XMLDoc (///) and comments before the first token (license headers) are never reported. Confidence medium. Version control keeps the old code; the fix removes the lines.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `minLines` | integer | `3` | Minimum number of consecutive commented-out code lines. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Speichern;
// if Kunde.Aktiv then
//   Kunde.Speichern;
// Log('gespeichert');
```

Correct:

```pascal
Speichern;
```

<a id="default-parameter"></a>
### default-parameter

**Default parameter instead of overload** · Code smell · Warning · Group Style and metrics

A parameter with a default value (Count: Integer = 10). For teams that prefer overloads: default values end up compiled into the caller, do not change with the library and do not mix well with overloads. Reported once per declaration (class member, interface section, forward or a routine only in the implementation part); the body of a declaration is not reported again. The quick fix inserts one overload per omitted trailing default parameter before the declaration, each forwarding with the default value, removes the default values (also repeated ones in the body) and adds overload. No fix when the signature is given from outside (virtual, dynamic, abstract, override, message, external, interface types), when the name is used as a value (@Routine, method pointer), when an overload of the same name exists, inside conditional compilation, or when the body cannot be found unambiguously.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Exportieren(const Datei: string; MitKopf: Boolean = True);
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Exportieren(const Datei: string); overload;
procedure Exportieren(const Datei: string; MitKopf: Boolean); overload;
```

<a id="empty-routine"></a>
### empty-routine

**Empty routine** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A method or routine whose body has neither a statement nor a comment (nor a compiler directive). Either the content is missing or the method can go. Not reported: virtual and dynamic methods (a stand-in for abstract in base classes), message handlers, methods of classes that list interfaces (the compiler requires every interface method), event handlers named in the form file (the IDE removes empty ones when saving) and bodies with a comment, such as overrides that deliberately do nothing. Quick fixes: insert a comment between begin and end; remove the method (declaration with its XML doc and body) only for private methods and routines without a declaration in the interface part that occur nowhere else in the file and fulfil no signature from outside.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Pruefen;
begin
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Pruefen;
begin
  // Absichtlich leer.
end;
```

<a id="trivial-accessor"></a>
### trivial-accessor

**Accessor method only reads or writes a field** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A getter whose body is only Result := FName; (also Exit(FName), Self.FName) or a setter whose body is only FName := Value; (with refactoring.inlineAccessorEqualityCheck also if FName <> Value then FName := Value;). The property can point to the field directly. Only methods that are not virtual, dynamic, override or abstract, not class methods, not overloaded or generic, implement no interface method (also via a method resolution clause), have no index specifier and are not used by other members of the class; the field belongs to the class and has the type of the property. Silent unless refactoring.inlineAccessorDiagnostic is on (default off) and refactoring.inlineAccessor is on. Medium confidence: calls in other units are not visible to the rule. No quick fix in the rule; the code action "Replace accessor GetName with field FName" at the same place checks calls with the reference search and replaces it.

Flagged:

```pascal
property Name: string read GetName write FName;
...
function TCustomer.GetName: string;
begin
  Result := FName;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
property Name: string read FName write FName;
```

<a id="write-only-property"></a>
### write-only-property

**Property without read** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A property has write but no read. It cannot be shown in the Object Inspector or read in the debugger and confuses callers. Properties without a type are skipped (they republish an inherited property whose read comes from the ancestor), as are properties with an attribute from allowAttributes (with or without the Attribute suffix). Quick fix: add read FName when the type declares a field FName of the same type. Reading such a property is reported by readonly-property-assigned.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowAttributes` | string-list | `[]` | Attributes that allow a property without read. |

Flagged:

```pascal
property Passwort: string write SetPasswort;
```

Correct:

```pascal
property Passwort: string read FPasswort write SetPasswort;
```

<a id="xmldoc-mismatch"></a>
### xmldoc-mismatch

**XMLDoc does not match the signature** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A /// comment does not match its declaration or is not well-formed XML, like the compiler warnings W1201 to W1208 with XML documentation enabled. The message names a code only where dcc reports it (#1383): for comments in the interface section of a unit (dcc documents nothing else), and among the XML errors only a stray character after a tag name (W1205); Delphi 13 compiles '<' followed by a space, unknown entities, empty attribute values and unclosed tags without a warning. Reported: <param> for a parameter that does not exist; missing <param> when the block describes parameters at all (like W1208, 'but other parameters do'); with checkReturns, <returns> on a routine without a result and missing <returns> on a function whose block describes parameters. <inheritdoc/> fills in what is missing. Also '<' and '&' outside tags and entities, unknown entities, attribute values without quotes, unclosed and stray end tags, and cref targets that do not exist (W1206, only with an index and when every unit in uses is known; generic targets, unit names and types with unknown ancestors stay silent). With requirePublic, public and published methods and properties of types in the interface section without a /// comment are reported too (not override, not properties without a type). Quick fixes: rename unknown <param> in order to the missing ones, add the rest as empty lines in signature order, remove surplus <param> and stray <returns>; escape the character, add quotes, add or remove the end tag, write an unknown empty tag like TList<T> as text; set cref to the most similar symbol; generate the comment skeleton.

Matches the compiler message `W1201`, `W1202`, `W1203`, `W1204`, `W1205`, `W1206`, `W1207`, `W1208`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `requirePublic` | boolean | `false` | Also report public members without XMLDoc. |
| `checkReturns` | boolean | `true` | Check <returns>. |

Flagged:

```pascal
/// <summary>Sucht einen Kunden.</summary>
/// <param name="Nummer">Kundennummer</param>
function Suchen(const Id: Integer; Aktive: Boolean): TKunde;
```

Correct:

```pascal
/// <summary>Sucht einen Kunden.</summary>
/// <param name="Id">Kundennummer</param>
/// <param name="Aktive"></param>
function Suchen(const Id: Integer; Aktive: Boolean): TKunde;
```

<a id="queue-nil-in-package"></a>
### queue-nil-in-package

**TThread.Queue(nil, …) in a package** · Bug · Warning · Group Threads

TThread.Queue(nil, …) or TThread.ForceQueue(nil, …) in a unit of a design-time or runtime package: the entry belongs to no thread, so nobody removes it when the package is unloaded, and CheckSynchronize may call it afterwards in memory that no longer holds the code. Package units are recognized through the .dpk or .dproj of the loaded project (a .dpk on its own without project). A line with the comment from okMarker (default mainthread-ok) is exempt. High confidence. Quick fix: rewrite to the wrapper from the wrapper option (default TAtlasMainThread.Queue, ForceQueue for ForceQueue); with a parenthesis the wrapper text is put in front of the remaining arguments. ForceQueue with a delay gets no quick fix. The command-line linter reads the .dpk files below the checked folder. Does the same as Tools/Check-MainThreadQueue.ps1 for any plugin project; the script stays for checks without the Atlas server.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `wrapper` | string | `"TAtlasMainThread.Queue"` | Replacement for TThread.Queue(nil, in the quick fix. |
| `okMarker` | string | `"mainthread-ok"` | Comment that exempts a line. |

Flagged:

```pascal
TThread.Queue(nil,
  procedure
  begin
    FPanel.Aktualisieren;
  end);
```

Correct:

```pascal
TAtlasMainThread.Queue(
  procedure
  begin
    FPanel.Aktualisieren;
  end);
```

<a id="ota-notifier-not-removed"></a>
### ota-notifier-not-removed

**IDE notifier without RemoveNotifier** · Bug · Warning · Group Packages and tests

AddNotifier on IOTAServices, IOTAEditorServices, IOTAModule and so on (or another pair from registerPairs) whose index never reaches RemoveNotifier: the call is a statement without assignment, or the variable it is assigned to is never passed to RemoveNotifier in the unit (in finalization, a destructor or Destroyed). The IDE then calls the notifier after the package is unloaded, in memory that no longer holds the code. Register… calls inside the procedure Register are exempt: the IDE removes those wizards itself. Medium confidence. Quick fix in methods: keep the index in a field (FNotifierIndex in the private section) and add RemoveNotifier to the destructor, which is created if missing; in procedures of the unit: a global variable initialized with -1 and RemoveNotifier in finalization. No quick fix when the receiver is a parameter or local variable.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `registerPairs` | json | `[{"open":"AddNotifier","close":"RemoveNotifier"},{"open":"RegisterPackageWizard","close":"RemoveWizard"}]` | Pairs of the Open Tools API as [{"open": "AddNotifier", "close": "RemoveNotifier"}]; replaces the default. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Register;
begin
  (BorlandIDEServices as IOTAServices).AddNotifier(TSpeicherNotifier.Create);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
var
  NotifierIndex: Integer = -1;

procedure Register;
begin
  NotifierIndex := (BorlandIDEServices as IOTAServices).AddNotifier(TSpeicherNotifier.Create);
end;

initialization

finalization
  if NotifierIndex >= 0 then
    (BorlandIDEServices as IOTAServices).RemoveNotifier(NotifierIndex);
```

<a id="dunitx-test-without-assert"></a>
### dunitx-test-without-assert

**DUnitX test without assert** · Code smell · Hint · Group Packages and tests

A [Test] or [TestCase] method that calls no check, neither itself nor through its local routines, methods of the same class, routines of the unit or free routines of other units known to the symbol index (AssertTextPair from a shared test unit): it is always green. Calls matching assertPatterns (default Assert.*, WillRaise*, Check*), System.Assert and calls on classes with Assert in their name (TRuleAssert.Reports) count as checks. Tests with [WillRaise] or [Ignore] stay silent. High confidence. Quick fixes: insert Assert.Pass as a placeholder, or jump to the method.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `assertPatterns` | string-list | `["Assert.*","WillRaise*","Check*"]` | Calls that count as a check, as patterns with * and ?; replaces the default. |

Flagged:

```pascal
[Test]
procedure TImportTests.LeereDateiWirdGelesen;
begin
  FImport.Laden('leer.csv');
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
[Test]
procedure TImportTests.LeereDateiWirdGelesen;
begin
  FImport.Laden('leer.csv');
  Assert.AreEqual(0, FImport.Anzahl);
end;
```

<a id="dunitx-testcase-mismatch"></a>
### dunitx-testcase-mismatch

**TestCase does not match the method** · Bug · Warning · Group Packages and tests

A [TestCase] whose values DUnitX cannot pass to the method, which only shows when the tests run: the number of values differs from the number of parameters (DUnitX calls through RTTI, default values do not count), a value does not fit the type of its parameter, or the method has no parameters at all. Values are split like DUnitX does: at every character of the separator (third argument, default comma; no quotes), an empty text gives no value. Checked types: integers (decimal or hex, $FF, 0xFF), floating point (point or comma as decimal separator, DUnitX turns a comma into a point), Boolean (True or False) and enumerations declared in the same file (by name); other types are not checked, nor are values that are not a string literal (a constant, 'a' + 'b'). Besides TestCase('Name', 'Values'[, 'Separator']) the newer forms TestCase('Values'[, 'Separator'[, Trim]]) and NamedTestCase('Name', 'Values') are recognized. Only in test units (uses DUnitX.TestFramework, TestFramework or TestInsight.*). High confidence. No quick fix: what was meant is unclear.

Flagged:

```pascal
[Test]
[TestCase('Rest', '7,2')]
procedure Teile(A, B, Rest: Integer);
```

Correct:

```pascal
[Test]
[TestCase('Rest', '7,2,1')]
procedure Teile(A, B, Rest: Integer);
```

<a id="dunitx-fixture-not-registered"></a>
### dunitx-fixture-not-registered

**DUnitX fixture never registered** · Bug · Warning · Group Packages and tests

A test class DUnitX never sees, so its tests are silently missing. DUnitX finds test classes through RegisterTestFixture or, when the runner in the .dpr sets UseRTTI := True (DUnitX project template), through the [TestFixture] attribute. 1. Not registered: a class with [Test] or [TestCase] methods that neither the initialization section of the unit nor a routine of the unit called from there names (TDUnitX.RegisterTestFixture(TClass), own helpers such as Register([TA, TB])) and no other unit of its projects names, and that either has no [TestFixture] or belongs to a project whose .dpr creates a runner (TDUnitX.CreateRunner) without UseRTTI := True. A call from the option registrationCalls in initialization (an own registration function that finds all classes) counts for all classes of the unit. Generic and abstract classes and ancestors of other classes in the file stay silent. Quick fixes: add TDUnitX.RegisterTestFixture(TClass); to initialization, creating the section if needed; with an RTTI runner also add [TestFixture] in front of the class. 2. Unit in no test project: the group has test projects, but none contains the unit. Quick fix with exactly one test project: add the unit to the uses of the .dpr and as DCCReference to the .dproj. Only in test units and only with the projects of the group known; without them the rule stays silent. Medium confidence.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `registrationCalls` | string-list | `[]` | Own registration functions that register all test classes of a unit when called in initialization, as Name or Class.Name. |

Flagged:

```pascal
[TestFixture]
TImportTests = class
  …
end;
…
end.
```

Correct:

```pascal
[TestFixture]
TImportTests = class
  …
end;
…
initialization
  TDUnitX.RegisterTestFixture(TImportTests);
end.
```

<a id="event-call-without-assigned"></a>
### event-call-without-assigned

**Event called without Assigned check** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

FOnChange(Self) or OnChange(Self) is called in a method of the class without Assigned(FOnChange) or FOnChange <> nil guarding the call: without a handler an access violation follows. Events are fields and properties of the class with a method pointer or reference type (procedure ... of object, reference to ..., TNotifyEvent, TProc/TFunc, types ending in Event, procedural types of the unit or the index). Guards: the then branch of a condition with Assigned (also while), the else branch of not Assigned, "Assigned(F) and" before the call, or "if not Assigned(F) then Exit/raise" before it, including a flag variable or a parameterless method of the class whose Result contains Assigned(F). Fields that a constructor sets to a value (injected dependencies such as FReadText := AReadText), properties reading them and local names that shadow the event stay silent. High confidence. Quick fix: wrap the call in "if Assigned(F) then" when it is a statement of its own in a block.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TZaehler.Erhoehen;
begin
  Inc(FWert);
  FOnChange(Self);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TZaehler.Erhoehen;
begin
  Inc(FWert);
  if Assigned(FOnChange) then
    FOnChange(Self);
end;
```

<a id="owned-item-freed"></a>
### owned-item-freed

**Item of an owning list freed as well** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

An item of a TObjectList/TObjectList<T> (OwnsObjects, default True) or of a TObjectDictionary with doOwnsValues is freed with Free, FreeAndNil, Destroy or DisposeOf, directly (List[I].Free, List.Items[I].Free) or through a variable taken from List[I], List.First/Last or a for-in loop over the list or Dictionary.Values. The list frees the item itself on Delete, Remove, Clear, an assignment to List[I], or at the latest when it is freed: a double free. A list is owning when the unit creates it that way (TObjectList without False, TObjectDictionary with doOwnsValues) or declares it as TObjectList without creating it, and no OwnsObjects := False follows. Extract in the same routine keeps the rule silent. High confidence. Quick fixes: remove the Free (the whole loop when it only frees), Delete or Remove instead of Free, Extract instead of Remove.

Flagged:

```pascal
Auftraege := TObjectList<TAuftrag>.Create;
…
Auftraege[I].Free;
Auftraege.Delete(I);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Auftraege := TObjectList<TAuftrag>.Create;
…
Auftraege.Delete(I);
```

<a id="create-without-assignment"></a>
### create-without-assignment

**Object created and lost at once** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

TStringList.Create; as a statement of its own: the object is created but stored nowhere and never freed (memory leak). Silent for components with an owner (first argument Self, Owner, AOwner, Application, or a TComponent descendant with an argument other than nil), threads (they free themselves with FreeOnTerminate), records, unknown classes with arguments and classes listed in selfRegistering. High confidence. Quick fix: assign to a new local variable and wrap the following statements of the block in try/finally with Free.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `selfRegistering` | string-list | `[]` | Classes that register themselves in their constructor, as patterns with * and ?; Create as a statement stays silent for them. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Starten;
begin
  TImportProtokoll.Create;
  Laden;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Starten;
var
  ImportProtokoll: TImportProtokoll;
begin
  ImportProtokoll := TImportProtokoll.Create;
  try
    Laden;
  finally
    ImportProtokoll.Free;
  end;
end;
```

<a id="raw-memory-leak"></a>
### raw-memory-leak

**GetMem or New without release** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A local variable receives memory from GetMem, AllocMem, ReallocMem, GetMemory, New, StrAlloc or StrNew, but the routine never releases it with FreeMem, FreeMemory, Dispose, StrDispose or ReallocMem(P, 0), and the pointer neither leaves the routine nor is stored: not returned (Result, Exit), not assigned to another variable or field, not passed by address (@P), not handed to a method that keeps it (Add, Insert, Push …), not captured by an anonymous method and not used in a local routine. Passing the pointer to read or fill it (Checksum(P, N), Stream.ReadBuffer(P^, N)) is fine; a routine whose name contains Free, Frei, Release, Dispose, Destroy or Dealloc counts as the release. A release without try/finally is reported by pair-without-finally. Medium confidence. Quick fix: wrap the following statements of the block in try … finally with the matching release.

Flagged:

```pascal
GetMem(Puffer, Groesse);
Strom.ReadBuffer(Puffer^, Groesse);
Ergebnis := Pruefsumme(Puffer, Groesse);
```

Correct:

```pascal
GetMem(Puffer, Groesse);
try
  Strom.ReadBuffer(Puffer^, Groesse);
  Ergebnis := Pruefsumme(Puffer, Groesse);
finally
  FreeMem(Puffer);
end;
```

<a id="local-object-not-freed"></a>
### local-object-not-freed

**Local object never released** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A local variable (also an inline variable) receives T.Create(…) of a class without an owner, but the routine never releases it, and the object does not leave the routine: not returned (Result, Exit), not assigned to anything else (field, other variable, list element), not passed as an argument (except to routines that only read it: Assign, AddStrings, CopyFrom, LoadFromStream, SaveToStream, IndexOf, Contains, Equals), not raised, not converted with as, not used with @, in a set or array constructor or with with, not given a Parent or Owner, not captured by an anonymous method and not used in a local routine. Free, Destroy, DisposeOf, Release, FreeAndNil and routines with Free, Release, Destroy, Dispose or Frei in their name count as the release; Show, Close, Start, Resume, Run and Execute count as handing the object over (a form with caFree, a thread). Silent for interface variables, records and dynamic arrays (TPoint.Create, TArray<string>.Create), threads, components with an owner and unknown classes with arguments (like create-without-assignment); routines with asm or with stay silent. High confidence when the ancestors of the class are known up to TObject, otherwise medium. Quick fix: wrap the following statements of the block in try … finally with Free.

Flagged:

```pascal
var
  Lines: TStringList;
begin
  Lines := TStringList.Create;
  Lines.Add('a');
  Memo.Text := Lines.Text;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
var
  Lines: TStringList;
begin
  Lines := TStringList.Create;
  try
    Lines.Add('a');
    Memo.Text := Lines.Text;
  finally
    Lines.Free;
  end;
end;
```

<a id="created-result-not-freed"></a>
### created-result-not-freed

**New object from a function never released** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A local variable receives the result of a function that always returns a new object, and the routine neither releases nor passes on that object (same rules as local-object-not-freed). A function of the same file counts when every assignment to Result is T.Create(…) without an owner, Result is otherwise only used through its members or released (cleanup in except) and never passed on, and every Exit(…) creates an object as well; overloads of the same name must all qualify. It is matched when called without qualification, through Self or through a type name; methods of other objects are only known from the option factories. Medium confidence. Quick fix: wrap the following statements of the block in try … finally with Free.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `factories` | string-list | `[]` | Further functions that always return a new object owned by the caller, as Name, Class.Name, Object.Name or Unit.Name. |

Flagged:

```pascal
function NewList: TStringList;
begin
  Result := TStringList.Create;
end;
…
  Lines := NewList;
  Memo.Lines.Assign(Lines);
```

Correct:

```pascal
  Lines := NewList;
  try
    Memo.Lines.Assign(Lines);
  finally
    Lines.Free;
  end;
```

<a id="stringlist-objects-leak"></a>
### stringlist-objects-leak

**Objects in a TStringList without ownership** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

AddObject or InsertObject puts a newly created object (T.Create(…) directly, or a local variable created that way and otherwise only read) into a TStringList (or THashedStringList) that the unit creates without OwnsObjects, or into the Items of a TListBox, TComboBox, TCheckListBox, TComboBoxEx or TColorListBox field; nobody frees these objects. A list is owning with TStringList.Create(True) or OwnsObjects := True. Silent when a routine of the unit reads List.Objects[…] and frees something, when the list is passed on (FreeObjects(List), assigned elsewhere), when the object has an owner (Self, Owner, AOwner, Application or a TComponent descendant with an argument) and for lists not created in the unit. Medium confidence. Quick fix: TStringList.Create(True) (also instead of Create(False)), with other constructors OwnsObjects := True after the creation; none for control items.

Flagged:

```pascal
FKunden := TStringList.Create;
…
FKunden.AddObject(K.Name, TKunde.Create(K));
```

Correct:

```pascal
FKunden := TStringList.Create(True);
…
FKunden.AddObject(K.Name, TKunde.Create(K));
```

<a id="message-handler-without-inherited"></a>
### message-handler-without-inherited

**Message handler without inherited** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A method with message WM_... or CM_... calls neither inherited nor DefaultHandler (or DefWindowProc, CallWindowProc). The default handling of the VCL or Windows is lost: no realignment on WM_SIZE, no closing on WM_CLOSE, no painting on WM_PAINT. The message names the consequence for common messages. Silent for classes without an ancestor, for messages listed in swallow (default WM_ERASEBKGND) and for handlers that set Msg.Result themselves (deliberate answer such as MA_NOACTIVATE). Medium confidence. Quick fix: insert inherited; at the start.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `swallow` | string-list | `["WM_ERASEBKGND"]` | Messages that may be handled without inherited; replaces the default. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TfrmHaupt.WMSize(var Msg: TWMSize);
begin
  AnordnungAnpassen;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TfrmHaupt.WMSize(var Msg: TWMSize);
begin
  inherited;
  AnordnungAnpassen;
end;
```

<a id="string-index-base"></a>
### string-index-base

**String index from 0 or Pos result compared with -1** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

The classic string routines count from 1: Copy(S, 0, N) and Insert(T, S, 0) only work like 1 by chance, Delete(S, 0, N) deletes nothing. The string must be known: declared type in the file, literal, or .Text/.Caption; dynamic arrays (0-based) stay silent. Pos, PosEx and AnsiPos return 0 for no match: Pos(...) > -1, >= 0 and <> -1 are always true, = -1 and < 0 never. Conversely S.IndexOf(...) > 0 or >= 1 misses a match at the start, < 1 or <= 0 counts it as not found (medium confidence; LastIndexOf stays silent, where > 0 usually means "not at the start"). High confidence for Delete and Pos. Quick fix: start value 1 or the corrected comparison (> 0, = 0, >= 0, < 0).

Flagged:

```pascal
if Pos(';', Zeile) > -1 then
  Felder := Copy(Zeile, 0, Pos(';', Zeile) - 1);
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Pos(';', Zeile) > 0 then
  Felder := Copy(Zeile, 1, Pos(';', Zeile) - 1);
```

<a id="delimited-text-without-strict"></a>
### delimited-text-without-strict

**DelimitedText without StrictDelimiter** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs
