List.DelimitedText := ... after List.Delimiter := ... in the same routine without StrictDelimiter being set: DelimitedText then also splits at spaces, 'Max Muster;Berlin' becomes three entries instead of two. For fields, StrictDelimiter anywhere in the unit counts (often in the constructor); StrictDelimiter := False is deliberate and stays silent, as does DelimitedText without a Delimiter of its own. High confidence. Quick fix: insert StrictDelimiter := True; before the assignment.

Flagged:

```pascal
Teile.Delimiter := ';';
Teile.DelimitedText := Zeile;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Teile.Delimiter := ';';
Teile.StrictDelimiter := True;
Teile.DelimitedText := Zeile;
```

<a id="self-as-interface-in-constructor"></a>
### self-as-interface-in-constructor

**Self passed as interface during construction** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

Self is passed as an interface while reference counting can already free the object. Only two real error cases are reported: (1) a class with its own reference counting (an _Release in the class chain frees the object, no class overrides NewInstance, the chain is known up to TObject) passes Self as an interface in a constructor or in AfterConstruction: the counter goes from 0 to 1 and back to 0, the object is destroyed before construction ends. (2) A TInterfacedObject descendant passes Self as an interface in AfterConstruction after inherited: inherited AfterConstruction has already removed the protective count from NewInstance. In the constructor of a TInterfacedObject descendant Self is protected and the rule stays silent. Passing counts as Self as IX, IX(Self), an argument whose parameter is an interface in all known overloads, and an assignment to an interface variable. High confidence. Quick fix in case 2: move the statement before inherited.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TBeobachter.AfterConstruction;
begin
  inherited;
  FQuelle.Anmelden(Self);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TBeobachter.AfterConstruction;
begin
  FQuelle.Anmelden(Self);
  inherited;
end;
```

<a id="null-compare"></a>
### null-compare

**Comparison with Null instead of VarIsNull or IsNull** · Code smell · Warning · Group Robustness and patterns

X = Null or X <> Null compares a variant with Null. The result depends on the global System.Variants.NullEqualityRule: with the default ncrLoose the comparison works as expected, with ncrStrict it is never true and with ncrError it raises an exception. A library, a test framework or another part of the project can change the rule. VarIsNull(X) and Field.IsNull check independently of it. Reported only where the comparison stands on its own (condition, argument, assignment); not X = Null binds as (not X) = Null and gets no quick fix. High confidence. Quick fix: Field.IsNull for X.Value on FieldByName, FindField, Fields[...], ParamByName, Params[...] or a field of type T...Field/T...Param, otherwise VarIsNull(X); with <> preceded by not.

Flagged:

```pascal
if qryKunde.FieldByName('Geburtstag').Value = Null then
  Alter := 0;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if qryKunde.FieldByName('Geburtstag').IsNull then
  Alter := 0;
```

<a id="unchecked-enum-cast"></a>
### unchecked-enum-cast

**Number cast to enumeration without check** · Bug · Hint · Group Find bugs

TStatus(N) with an enumeration type declared in the file or the index, where N comes from a call listed in the option sources (FieldByName, ReadInteger, AsInteger, StrToInt): directly in the argument or via a variable that is not read (checked) between its assignment and the cast. A value outside the range yields an invalid enumeration value that falls through case statements. EnsureRange, Min or Max in the argument count as a check; constants, arithmetic and unknown types stay silent. For enumerations with explicit ordinal values the range is given as Ord(Low(T))..Ord(High(T)). Medium confidence. Quick fix for a direct source in a statement of its own: helper variable, InRange check with raise EConvertError before it, System.Math added to uses.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sources` | string-list | `["FieldByName","ReadInteger","AsInteger","StrToInt"]` | Calls whose result counts as unchecked. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Status := TStatus(qry.FieldByName('Status').AsInteger);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Wert := qry.FieldByName('Status').AsInteger;
if not InRange(Wert, Ord(Low(TStatus)), Ord(High(TStatus))) then
  raise EConvertError.CreateFmt('Ungültiger Wert %d für TStatus', [Wert]);
Status := TStatus(Wert);
```

<a id="createprocess-readonly-commandline"></a>
### createprocess-readonly-commandline

**CreateProcess with a read-only command line** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

CreateProcess (CreateProcessW) and CreateProcessAsUser write into the buffer of lpCommandLine. A PChar on memory that cannot be written causes an access violation. Reported for PChar(...)/PWideChar(...) on: a string literal or constant; a concatenation of exactly one literal and one value (if the value is empty, the RTL returns the literal itself); a local string variable whose last assignment before the call is one of these, without UniqueString afterwards; a string parameter (medium confidence, the caller may pass a literal). Function results such as Format and concatenations with several parts are writable heap strings and stay silent, as does CreateProcessA, which copies the command line itself. Quick fix: copy the command line into a local variable and call UniqueString; for a variable, UniqueString before the statement. No quick fix in loop conditions.

Flagged:

```pascal
CreateProcess(nil, PChar('notepad.exe ' + Datei), nil, nil, False, 0, nil, nil, SI, PI);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Befehl := 'notepad.exe ' + Datei;
UniqueString(Befehl);
CreateProcess(nil, PChar(Befehl), nil, nil, False, 0, nil, nil, SI, PI);
```

<a id="form-access-before-create"></a>
### form-access-before-create

**Access to a form that is not created yet** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

The constructor, AfterConstruction or the OnCreate handler of an automatically created form or data module uses the global variable of another form that the .dpr creates later with Application.CreateForm. The variable is still nil at that point. The order comes from the .dpr of the project the unit belongs to; the OnCreate handler from the form file (FormCreate or DataModuleCreate without one). Silent when the routine checks the variable (Assigned, comparison with nil), assigns it itself, creates it with CreateForm or declares a local name of the same name, and for forms that are not created automatically. Each variable is reported once per routine. Medium confidence. No quick fix: change the order in the .dpr or move the access to OnShow.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TfrmHaupt.FormCreate(Sender: TObject);
begin
  lblNutzer.Caption := dmDaten.Benutzer;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
// .dpr: Application.CreateForm(TdmDaten, dmDaten) before TfrmHaupt
procedure TfrmHaupt.FormCreate(Sender: TObject);
begin
  lblNutzer.Caption := dmDaten.Benutzer;
end;
```

<a id="except-handler-order"></a>
### except-handler-order

**General exception handler before a specific one** · Bug · Warning · Group Exceptions

In a try..except with several on handlers the first matching handler wins. A handler for a class that an earlier handler already catches is never reached: its code is dead and the special treatment never runs. Reported when an earlier handler names the same class, an ancestor of it, Exception (covers every exception class) or TObject. Ancestors come from the unit, the project index and a table of known RTL, VCL and FireDAC exception classes (for example EStreamError > EFOpenError, EIntError > ERangeError); unknown classes are only compared with Exception and TObject. Quick fix: move the specific handler before the general one (only when both handlers stand on their own lines). A duplicate handler for the same class is reported without a quick fix.

Flagged:

```pascal
except
  on E: Exception do
    Protokoll(E.Message);
  on E: EConvertError do
    ShowMessage('Ungültige Zahl');
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
except
  on E: EConvertError do
    ShowMessage('Ungültige Zahl');
  on E: Exception do
    Protokoll(E.Message);
end;
```

<a id="reraise-exception-object"></a>
### reraise-exception-object

**raise E instead of raise in a handler** · Code smell · Warning · Group Exceptions

raise E; in an on E: … handler raises the caught exception object again as a new raise. The RTL does not free the object twice, but the exception now starts at the handler: ExceptAddr and the call stack of tools such as madExcept or JclDebug point to the handler instead of the original fault, and the original location is lost. raise; re-raises the current exception unchanged. Also reported for raise E at …, then without a quick fix. Silent when the handler takes the object over with AcquireExceptionObject or assigns a new object to the variable, inside anonymous methods and for handlers without a variable name. High confidence. Quick fix: raise E; → raise;.

Flagged:

```pascal
except
  on E: EDatenbank do
  begin
    Protokoll(E.Message);
    raise E;
  end;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
except
  on E: EDatenbank do
  begin
    Protokoll(E.Message);
    raise;
  end;
end;
```

<a id="blocking-call-in-main-thread"></a>
### blocking-call-in-main-thread

**Blocking call in the main thread** · Code smell · Warning · Group Threads

Sleep, WaitFor, WaitForSingleObject, WaitForMultipleObjects or Wait in methods of forms and frames (classes that inherit from TForm, TCustomForm, TFrame or TCustomFrame) and in event handlers (methods with a Sender parameter) outside thread classes. The user interface freezes while the call waits; if the other thread waits for Synchronize, the program hangs. WaitFor without a timeout on a variable names the risk of the Synchronize deadlock. Sleep, WaitForSingleObject and WaitForMultipleObjects count only without a receiver (or with Winapi.Windows), WaitFor and Wait only on a receiver. A literal timeout below maxSleepMs (Sleep(10), WaitForSingleObject(H, 0)) stays silent, except a short Sleep in a waiting loop without Application.ProcessMessages. Anonymous methods are skipped (TTask.Run, TThread.Queue), nested routines belong to their method. Medium confidence. No quick fix: move the work into TTask.Run and report the result with TThread.Queue.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `maxSleepMs` | integer | `50` | Literal timeouts below this value (milliseconds) are not reported; a short Sleep in a waiting loop still is. |
| `calls` | string-list | `["Sleep","WaitFor","WaitForSingleObject","WaitForMultipleObjects","Wait"]` | Calls that block. Other names are reported regardless of their arguments. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TfrmHaupt.btnExportClick(Sender: TObject);
begin
  FExport.Start;
  FExport.WaitFor;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TfrmHaupt.btnExportClick(Sender: TObject);
begin
  btnExport.Enabled := False;
  TTask.Run(
    procedure
    begin
      FExport.Ausfuehren;
      TThread.Queue(nil, procedure begin btnExport.Enabled := True; end);
    end);
end;
```

<a id="thread-loop-without-terminated"></a>
### thread-loop-without-terminated

**Thread loop without Terminated** · Bug · Warning · Group Threads

A while or repeat loop in TThread.Execute, or in a method of the thread class that is only called from there (also through other such methods, including nested routines), does not check Terminated. Terminate then has no effect, and WaitFor or Free hang when the program shuts down. Reported for endless loops (while True, until False) and for waiting loops with Sleep, WaitFor, WaitForSingleObject, WaitForMultipleObjects, MsgWaitForMultipleObjects, Wait or Yield whose condition and body never mention Terminated or CheckTerminated. Thread classes come from the unit and the index. Bounded loops, for loops, anonymous methods and classes that override TerminatedSet (they end their loops another way) stay silent. High confidence. Quick fix: add Terminated to the loop condition (while True → while not Terminated, otherwise and not Terminated; until False → until Terminated, otherwise or Terminated).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TPoller.Execute;
begin
  while True do
  begin
    Abfragen;
    Sleep(1000);
  end;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TPoller.Execute;
begin
  while not Terminated do
  begin
    Abfragen;
    Sleep(1000);
  end;
end;
```

<a id="anonymous-thread-not-started"></a>
### anonymous-thread-not-started

**Anonymous thread never started** · Bug · Warning · Group Threads

TThread.CreateAnonymousThread(…) creates the thread suspended with FreeOnTerminate set. Without Start the thread never runs, and because it never terminates it is never freed either. Reported for the call as a statement of its own and for a local variable (also an inline variable) that is never started with Start or Resume and not passed on (argument, assignment, Result, field). Setting properties such as FreeOnTerminate or OnTerminate does not count as passing on. High confidence. Quick fix: append .Start; for a variable, T.Start; after the last statement of the same block that mentions it.

Flagged:

```pascal
TThread.CreateAnonymousThread(
  procedure
  begin
    Synchronisieren;
  end);
```

Correct:

```pascal
TThread.CreateAnonymousThread(
  procedure
  begin
    Synchronisieren;
  end).Start;
```

<a id="task-exception-unobserved"></a>
### task-exception-unobserved

**Exception in TTask gets lost** · Code smell · Hint · Group Threads

TTask.Run(…) or TTask.Create(…).Start whose result is never kept or queried with Wait, Status or similar, and whose anonymous method has no try … except. An exception in the task disappears silently. Also reported for a local variable that is never mentioned again (for TTask.Create only in V.Start). Silent for kept results (fields, Result, any other use of the variable) and for method references, whose body the rule cannot see. Medium confidence. Quick fix: wrap the body in try … except and report the message on the main thread through TThread.Queue with ShowMessage (adds Vcl.Dialogs, or FMX.Dialogs in FireMonkey units, and System.SysUtils to the uses clause when missing); only when begin and end of the anonymous method stand on their own lines.

Flagged:

```pascal
TTask.Run(
  procedure
  begin
    Importieren(Datei);
  end);
```

Correct:

```pascal
TTask.Run(
  procedure
  begin
    try
      Importieren(Datei);
    except
      on E: Exception do
      begin
        var Fehler := E.Message;
        TThread.Queue(nil,
          procedure
          begin
            ShowMessage(Fehler);
          end);
      end;
    end;
  end);
```

<a id="dataset-loop-without-next"></a>
### dataset-loop-without-next

**Dataset loop without Next** · Bug · Error · Group Database

while not Q.Eof do without Q.Next in the body, or with Next only on some of the paths that continue the loop (an if without else, Continue before Next). The loop spins forever on the same record. Delete, MoveBy, Last, Close, FindNext and Free also count as moving on; Break, Exit, Abort and raise end the loop. Nested while and for loops are not trusted to run. When Q is passed on in the body (argument, assignment, with), the called code may move the record pointer and the rule stays silent. High confidence when Next is missing entirely, medium when only some paths miss it. Quick fix: insert Q.Next; at the end of the begin … end body (only when Next is missing entirely).

Flagged:

```pascal
qryPositionen.First;
while not qryPositionen.Eof do
begin
  Summe := Summe + qryPositionen.FieldByName('Betrag').AsCurrency;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
qryPositionen.First;
while not qryPositionen.Eof do
begin
  Summe := Summe + qryPositionen.FieldByName('Betrag').AsCurrency;
  qryPositionen.Next;
end;
```

<a id="dataset-edit-without-post"></a>
### dataset-edit-without-post

**Edit or Append without Post** · Bug · Warning · Group Database

After Edit, Append or Insert (without arguments) on a dataset, the same routine calls neither Post nor Cancel (nor CheckBrowseMode). The change stays open and is silently lost on scrolling or saved unexpectedly later; on an exception in between, nothing cancels it. Silent when another routine of the unit posts or cancels the same dataset (an Edit button with a separate Save button) and when the dataset is passed on after Edit (argument, assignment, with). Medium confidence. Quick fix: wrap the following statements that use the dataset in try … except, with Post at the end of the try part and Cancel; raise; in the except part.

Flagged:

```pascal
tblKunde.Edit;
tblKunde.FieldByName('Status').AsInteger := Ord(ksAktiv);
Protokollieren;
```

Correct:

```pascal
tblKunde.Edit;
try
  tblKunde.FieldByName('Status').AsInteger := Ord(ksAktiv);
  tblKunde.Post;
except
  tblKunde.Cancel;
  raise;
end;
Protokollieren;
```

<a id="transaction-without-rollback"></a>
### transaction-without-rollback

**Transaction without Rollback** · Bug · Warning · Group Database

StartTransaction without Rollback in the except part of the try that directly follows. On an exception the transaction stays open and holds locks. Recognized for FireDAC (TFDConnection, TFDTransaction), IBX and dbExpress (StartTransaction/Commit/Rollback), dbExpress from XE (BeginTransaction/CommitFreeAndNil/RollbackFreeAndNil, also as T := Conn.BeginTransaction) and ADO (BeginTrans/CommitTrans/RollbackTrans). The option pairs replaces these pairs with your own. The Rollback may sit in a try … except inside a try … finally that follows directly. Silent when another routine of the unit rolls back the same connection (transaction across several events). High confidence. Quick fix: wrap the statements up to the Commit of the same block in try … except with Rollback (with the arguments of the Commit) and raise; only when a Commit follows in the same block.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pairs` | json | `[{"start":"StartTransaction","commit":"Commit","rollback":"Rollback"},{"start":"BeginTransaction","commit":"CommitFreeAndNil","rollback":"RollbackFreeAndNil"},{"start":"BeginTrans","commit":"CommitTrans","rollback":"RollbackTrans"}]` | Transaction methods per library as [{"start": …, "commit": …, "rollback": …}]; replaces the built-in pairs. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Verbindung.StartTransaction;
Buchen(Auftrag);
Verbindung.Commit;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Verbindung.StartTransaction;
try
  Buchen(Auftrag);
  Verbindung.Commit;
except
  Verbindung.Rollback;
  raise;
end;
```

<a id="sql-write-without-where"></a>
### sql-write-without-where

**UPDATE or DELETE without WHERE** · Bug · Warning · Group Database

An UPDATE or DELETE without WHERE in a string literal, SQL.Text, SQL.Add, CommandText, the first argument of ExecSQL, ExecSQLScalar, Open, ExecuteDirect or Execute, or in SQL.Strings/CommandText of the form file. It affects every row of the table; usually the condition was forgotten. The rule assembles the SQL text per query within a routine (Clear and a new Text start over) and substitutes constants and local values. JOIN, TOP, LIMIT and ROWS restrict the rows as well; a WHERE in a subquery or comment does not count. Unknown parts behind the table name (variables, calls at the place of a clause) keep the statement silent; a dynamic table name reports with medium confidence. Silent when the routine passes the SQL on or another routine of the unit adds to it; form file SQL only counts when the unit does not change it. Literals outside SQL properties count only with DELETE or UPDATE in capitals, so message texts stay silent. High confidence, no quick fix; tables that are emptied on purpose go into allowTables.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowTables` | string-list | `[]` | Tables that are deliberately emptied or changed completely, with or without schema. |

Flagged:

```pascal
qry.SQL.Text := 'DELETE FROM Warenkorb';
qry.ExecSQL;
```

Correct:

```pascal
qry.SQL.Text := 'DELETE FROM Warenkorb WHERE Kunde = :Kunde';
qry.ParamByName('Kunde').AsInteger := KundeId;
qry.ExecSQL;
```

<a id="signature-mismatch"></a>
### signature-mismatch

**Implementation differs from declaration** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

The header in the implementation section has different parameters (count, name, type, const/var/out), a different result type, procedure instead of function or a different calling convention than the declaration in the type or, for a routine of the unit, in the interface section (like E2037). With overloads the nearest declaration is compared (parameter count, then types, names and modifiers); if that is not unique, declared-not-implemented and implemented-not-declared report both sides instead. Unit routines are compared only when the name is unique and neither overload, external nor forward is involved. A body may omit the parameter list, the result type and the calling convention. Methods inside {$IF} blocks are skipped; with include files the confidence is medium. Quick fix: align the implementation header with the declaration, or, as the second action, the declaration with the implementation (default values stay in the declaration). Off with refactoring.syncSignatures = false only removes the quick fixes.

Matches the compiler message `E2037`, `E2211`, `E2065`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Buchen(const Betrag: Currency; Datum: TDate);
…
procedure TKonto.Buchen(Betrag: Currency; Datum: TDateTime);
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Buchen(const Betrag: Currency; Datum: TDate);
…
procedure TKonto.Buchen(const Betrag: Currency; Datum: TDate);
```

<a id="interface-not-implemented"></a>
### interface-not-implemented

**Interface method missing in the class** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A class lists an interface but does not implement all of its methods, including those of ancestor interfaces (like E2291). A method of the class with the same name but a different signature is reported at that method (like E2211). Methods inherited from ancestor classes, method resolution clauses (procedure IFoo.Bar = Baz) and properties with implements count; public TObject methods and IInterface methods of TInterfacedObject and similar RTL bases count as well. If the chain of ancestor classes ends at an unknown class or the interface is unknown, the rule stays silent. A method resolution clause for an interface the class does not list is reported too (like E2265). Classes inside {$IF} blocks are skipped. Quick fix: declare the missing methods and create empty bodies (not offered when a header differs).

Matches the compiler message `E2291`, `E2211`, `E2265`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
TDateiLog = class(TInterfacedObject, ILogger)
public
  procedure Info(const Text: string);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TDateiLog = class(TInterfacedObject, ILogger)
public
  procedure Info(const Text: string);
  procedure Warnung(const Text: string);
  procedure Fehler(const Text: string);
end;
```

<a id="uses-unit-not-found"></a>
### uses-unit-not-found

**Unit in uses not found** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A unit in uses or contains is found neither in the project nor in the folders the compiler searches: folder of the using file, project folder, search paths and DCU output folder of the .dproj, library paths of the installation, each as .pas or .dcu and with every unit scope name in front (SysUtils → System.SysUtils); in packages also the units of the required packages (like F2613). Also reported: a file from in '…' that does not exist (F1026, also without a project, since only the path relative to the using file counts), a package from requires found neither as .dpk nor as .dcp (E2202), a unit that uses itself (F2092), and once a missing unit System, i.e. a missing library path, instead of one finding per unit (F1027). Entries inside {$IF} blocks are skipped; without a known installation, with an open macro in a search path or with a required package of unknown content the rule stays silent. The message names similar units. Quick fixes: use the unit with its namespace (Forms → Vcl.Forms) or a similarly written unit, or add the folder below the project that holds the unit to DCC_UnitSearchPath of the base configuration in the .dproj.

Matches the compiler message `F2613`, `F1027`, `F1026`, `E2202`, `F2092`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `searchPaths` | string | `"project"` | Where units are searched: project (like the compiler: project, search paths of the .dproj, library paths, .pas and .dcu), atlas (what Atlas knows: index and browsing paths of the installation) or both. |

Flagged:

```pascal
uses
  System.SysUtils, Vcl.Forms, Kunden.Daten, Rechnungs.Druck;
```

Correct:

```pascal
uses
  System.SysUtils, Vcl.Forms, Kunden.Daten, Rechnung.Druck;
```

<a id="unresolved-attribute"></a>
### unresolved-attribute

**Attribute not found and silently ignored** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

An attribute [Name] resolves neither to a class NameAttribute nor to Name, usually because a unit is missing in uses. Delphi only warns (W1074, E1074 after {$WARN UNKNOWN_CUSTOM_ATTRIBUTE ERROR}) and skips the attribute: DUnitX tests do not run, JSON or ORM mappings are missing. The message names the unit that declares the attribute in the index; a facade unit that passes the type on from another candidate (DUnitX.TestFramework instead of DUnitX.Attributes) comes first, and the DUnitX attributes are known even without DUnitX in the index. Compiler attributes such as Weak, Unsafe, Ref and Volatile always count. Silent without the unit System in the index, when a unit from uses is not in the index (it could declare the attribute), inside {$IF} blocks and when an ancestor of the surrounding class is unknown; with include files the confidence is medium. Quick fix: add the unit to uses.

Matches the compiler message `W1074`, `E2289`, `E1074`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
uses
  System.SysUtils;
type
  [TestFixture]
  TKundeTests = class
```

Correct:

```pascal
uses
  System.SysUtils, DUnitX.TestFramework;
type
  [TestFixture]
  TKundeTests = class
```

<a id="comparison-as-statement"></a>
### comparison-as-statement

**Comparison instead of assignment as a statement** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A comparison such as Result = 0; or FAktiv = True; stands on its own as a statement; := was meant. Delphi only reports E2014 when compiling. With = and an assignable left side (variable, field, element, pointer target) the quick fix replaces = with :=. Other comparisons and expressions whose value is discarded (A <> B;, A + B;, not A;, a literal) are reported without a fix, as is a call on the left side.

Matches the compiler message `E2014`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix comparison-as-statement`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
Result = 0;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Result := 0;
```

<a id="duplicate-case-label"></a>
### duplicate-case-label

**Duplicate case label** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Two labels of a case statement cover the same value (like E2030), also through ranges (1..5 and 3), constants with the same value, characters ('a' and #97) and enumeration ranges. The same applies to the labels of a variant record part (like E2027). Values are computed from numbers, characters, constants and enumeration values via name resolution; labels whose value is unknown are only compared by their text. A label is only reported when the earlier one is compiled whenever it is: different branches of the same {$IF} never clash. Quick fix: remove the duplicate value from the later branch, or the whole branch when it was its only label; a partial range overlap is reported without a fix.

Matches the compiler message `E2030`, `E2027`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
case Status of
  stNeu, stOffen: Bearbeiten;
  stGeschlossen: Archivieren;
  stOffen: Erinnern;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
case Status of
  stNeu, stOffen: Bearbeiten;
  stGeschlossen: Archivieren;
end;
```

<a id="field-after-method"></a>
### field-after-method

**Field after methods in the same section** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Within a visibility section of a class, object or record, a field declaration follows a method or property. Delphi requires fields first or a new section or var (E2169); FPC accepts it. Quick fixes: move the field with its attributes and comments to the start of the section, or put var in front of it. In records var comes first because moving changes the memory layout. A field is only reported when a method before it is compiled whenever the field is; with conditional branches in between only var is offered.

Matches the compiler message `E2169`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
TKunde = class
private
  function GetName: string;
  FName: string;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TKunde = class
private
  FName: string;
  function GetName: string;
end;
```

<a id="overload-directive-missing"></a>
### overload-directive-missing

**Overload without overload or with the same signature** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Two routines or methods of the same name in the same scope (a type, the unit across interface and implementation, the routines nested in a routine), of which at least one lacks overload (like E2254, or E2267 when the earlier one lacks it), or two overloads with the same parameter kinds and types (like E2252, or E2301 with the same result type as well). A body that implements an earlier declaration (interface, forward) does not count. Operators, method bodies and declarations in different branches of the same {$IF} are skipped. Quick fix: append overload; to every declaration without it, before the other directives.

Matches the compiler message `E2254`, `E2267`, `E2252`, `E2301`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Laden(const Datei: string);
procedure Laden(Strom: TStream);
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Laden(const Datei: string); overload;
procedure Laden(Strom: TStream); overload;
```

<a id="duplicate-declaration"></a>
### duplicate-declaration

**Identifier declared twice** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

An identifier is declared twice in the same scope (like E2004): a local variable, constant, type or label and a parameter of the routine, two fields, a field and a method or property of a type, two types, a constant and a variable of the unit across interface and implementation, two values of the same enumeration (no quick fix). Likewise a unit twice in uses or in the uses clauses of interface and implementation at once (option checkUses). Overloads and bodies of routines, a forward declaration and its type, TList next to TList<T> and declarations in different branches of the same {$IF} do not count. Quick fix: remove the second declaration (the whole var, const, type or label section when it was its only one; for uses the duplicate entry); none for parameters, methods and properties. Default array properties overloaded under the same name are checked by default-property-invalid (#1380).

Matches the compiler message `E2004`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix duplicate-declaration`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `checkUses` | boolean | `true` | Report duplicate entries in uses clauses. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TRechnung.Summieren(Betrag: Currency);
var
  Betrag: Currency;
begin
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TRechnung.Summieren(Betrag: Currency);
begin
```

<a id="conditional-directive-unbalanced"></a>
### conditional-directive-unbalanced

**Conditional directives incomplete or unknown** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

An {$IFDEF}, {$IFNDEF}, {$IFOPT} or {$IF} that is never closed, so the rest of the file depends on its condition (like E2095, E2280 for {$IF}); {$ELSE}, {$ELSEIF}, {$ENDIF} or {$IFEND} without a matching {$IF…}, or after {$ELSE} (like E2279); {$IFDEF}, {$IFNDEF}, {$DEFINE} or {$UNDEF} without a valid symbol (like E2173); an unknown directive or {$WARN} identifier (like E1030, before Delphi 13 W1030; the code follows delphiVersion), with the closest known name as quick fix; {$POPOPT} without {$PUSHOPT} (like W1078) or {$PUSHOPT} without {$POPOPT} (like W1079); an invalid value for {$ALIGN} or {$A} (like E2573). Unknown names are not checked in branches whose condition tests FPC, {$PUSHOPT}/{$POPOPT} only when none of them is inside a conditional branch. Quick fixes: add {$ENDIF} before the first blank line after the open block, remove a surplus directive, correct the typo.

Matches the compiler message `E2095`, `E2280`, `E2279`, `E2173`, `W1030`, `W1078`, `W1079`, `E2573`, `E1030`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
{$IFDEF DEBUG}
  Protokoll.Ausfuehrlich := True;

procedure TDienst.Starten;
```

Correct:

```pascal
{$IFDEF DEBUG}
  Protokoll.Ausfuehrlich := True;
{$ENDIF}

procedure TDienst.Starten;
```

<a id="compiler-directive-invalid"></a>
### compiler-directive-invalid

**Compiler directive invalid or file from {$I}/{$R} missing** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A compiler directive outside conditional branches that dcc rejects or reports itself: {$MESSAGE WARN 'Text'} and {$MESSAGE HINT 'Text'} show their text as a warning or hint (like W1054, H1054); {$WEAKPACKAGEUNIT ON} together with {$DENYPACKAGEUNIT ON} (like E2222); {$DESIGNONLY} or {$RUNONLY} in a unit instead of a package (like E2224); {$OBJTYPENAME} for a plain alias such as TZahl = Integer (like E2560); {$CODEPAGE} with a number above 65535 (like E1030); a file from {$I} or {$INCLUDE} that exists neither next to the unit nor in the search paths of the project (like F1026); an include file that includes itself through unconditional {$I}, directly or via other include files (like E2245); a resource file from {$R} or {$RESOURCE} that cannot be found (like E1026). Files are searched like dcc does: next to the unit, in the project folder, the search paths, DCC_IncludePath, DCC_ResourcePath and the library paths of the installation; without a project, with an unknown installation or an open macro in a path the rule stays silent. Not checked: {$R Name.res Name.rc} and a .res next to a .rc of the same name (the IDE compiles it before the compiler), forms ({$R *.dfm}, form-resource-missing), masks such as {$R *.res}, switches such as {$I+} and {$R-}, and {$MESSAGE} without a kind or with ERROR or FATAL. Directives in conditional branches are skipped, because dcc only reports those it compiles.

Matches the compiler message `W1054`, `H1054`, `E2222`, `E2224`, `E2560`, `E1030`, `F1026`, `E2245`, `E1026`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
unit Kunden;

{$I Gemeinsam.inc}
{$DESIGNONLY}

interface
```

Correct:

```pascal
unit Kunden;

{$I Gemeinsam.inc}

interface
```

<a id="override-target-missing"></a>
### override-target-missing

**override without a matching virtual method** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A method marked override for which no ancestor has a virtual (or dynamic, abstract) method with the same name and parameters (like E2137): often a typo (Destory) or parameters changed in the ancestor. The message names the closest virtual method of the ancestor ("there it is Ausfuehren(const Modus: TModus)") or a similar name. Also: override on a static method (like E2170), on a final one (like E2352), and deriving from a sealed class (like E2353). Ancestors are looked up in the file and in the symbol index; when the chain breaks at an unknown class the rule stays silent. Without System in the index the virtual methods of TObject count as present. Type parameters of generic ancestors, type aliases and synonyms such as Longint match. Quick fix: take over the ancestor's signature in the declaration and the implementation.

Matches the compiler message `E2137`, `E2170`, `E2352`, `E2353`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
TDienst = class
protected
  procedure Ausfuehren(const Modus: TModus); virtual;
end;
TImportDienst = class(TDienst)
protected
  procedure Ausfuehren(Modus: Integer); override;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TImportDienst = class(TDienst)
protected
  procedure Ausfuehren(const Modus: TModus); override;
end;
```

<a id="abstract-class-instantiated"></a>
### abstract-class-instantiated

**Instance of a class with abstract methods** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

Create on a class that declares or inherits abstract methods without overriding them (like W1020). A class declared abstract without abstract methods can be created; dcc does not enforce "class abstract" (E2402 exists only for .NET, #1383). Calling an abstract method raises EAbstractError at run time; the message names the open methods. Classes are looked up in the file and in the symbol index; overloads with the same number of parameters count as one method. Calls on class references, inherited Create and constructor bodies are not checked. Quick fix for a class of this file that inherits the abstract methods: override them there (like the code action "override").

Matches the compiler message `W1020`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
Form := TForm.Create;
Gesamt := Gesamt + Form.Flaeche;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Form := TKreis.Create;
Gesamt := Gesamt + Form.Flaeche;
```

<a id="property-accessor-invalid"></a>
### property-accessor-invalid

**Invalid property getter or setter** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

The name after read or write of a property does not fit: it is declared neither in the class nor in its ancestors, or it is a property (like E2168); the getter or setter has the wrong kind, parameters or types (like E2008, older Delphi versions E2234); the setter takes a var or out parameter (like E2282); the accessor is overloaded and no overload fits (like E2250, older Delphi versions E2271); a class property uses an instance member or a class method without static (like E2355), or an instance property uses a class var or a static class method (like E2356); an array property uses a field (like E2008 in Delphi 13, documented as E2174, index with a field compiles); a field selector goes through a field that is no record or stands behind a class property (like E2459), names a member that is no field (like E2461) or a field the record lacks (like E2562). Names are looked up in the file and, for ancestors, in the symbol index; the rule stays silent when the ancestor chain breaks, the name only exists in an inactive branch, the class cannot see it or the class includes files. Types are compared only when both are built in or declared as types of their own. Quick fix for a missing F<Name>, Get<Name> or Set<Name>: create it (like the completion after read/write, completion.accessorSection).

Matches the compiler message `E2168`, `E2008`, `E2234`, `E2282`, `E2271`, `E2355`, `E2356`, `E2174`, `E2459`, `E2461`, `E2562`, `E2008`, `E2250`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
TKunde = class
private
  FName: string;
public
  property Name: string read FName write SetName;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TKunde = class
private
  FName: string;
  procedure SetName(const Value: string);
public
  property Name: string read FName write SetName;
end;
```

<a id="assign-to-property-member"></a>
### assign-to-property-member

**Assignment to a field of a property record** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Assignment to a field of a record that a property with a getter, a default or array property or a function result only delivers as a copy (like E2064): the assignment has no effect. Assignments to untyped constants are reported as well. Types come from name resolution; the rule only reports when the record type is known for sure. A property that reads a field directly lets Delphi write into that field and stays silent. Quick fix for a property with write: assign through a local variable and write it back; the variable is declared in the routine and the unit of its type is added to uses.

Matches the compiler message `E2064`, `E2453`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
Rahmen.BoundsRect.Left := 0;
```

Correct:

```pascal
R := Rahmen.BoundsRect;
R.Left := 0;
Rahmen.BoundsRect := R;
```

<a id="readonly-property-assigned"></a>
### readonly-property-assigned

**Assignment to a property without write** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Assignment to a property that has no write (like E2129), or reading a property that has no read (like E2130). The rule only reports when name resolution finds the property for sure; redeclarations without read and write (property Caption;) count as unknown. Quick fixes: Count := 0 becomes Clear when the type has Clear; inside a method of the declaring class, assign the field behind read; for a property declared in this file, create the setter and add write.

Matches the compiler message `E2129`, `E2130`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
Liste.Count := 0;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Liste.Clear;
```

<a id="sql-select-star"></a>
### sql-select-star

**SELECT * in code** · Code smell · Hint · Group Database

SELECT * or SELECT alias.* in a column list, in the same places as sql-write-without-where: string literals, SQL.Text, SQL.Add, CommandText, the first argument of ExecSQL, Open and similar calls, constants, local values and SQL.Strings/CommandText of the form file. New columns silently change the dataset, persistent fields no longer match, and more data is transferred than needed. Subqueries count; EXISTS (SELECT * …) and COUNT(*) stay silent, as does a column list from a variable. Literals outside SQL properties count only with SELECT in capitals and a FROM, so message texts stay silent. Off by default (enable in the rules settings or with the strict profile). High confidence. Quick fix: when the star stands in a single literal and the dataset has persistent fields in the form file, replace * with their FieldName list (without calculated and lookup fields).

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
qry.SQL.Text := 'SELECT * FROM Kunden WHERE Ort = :Ort';
```

Correct:

```pascal
qry.SQL.Text := 'SELECT Id, Name, Ort FROM Kunden WHERE Ort = :Ort';
```

<a id="path-concat-without-delimiter"></a>
### path-concat-without-delimiter

**Path joined without delimiter** · Bug · Warning · Group Robustness and patterns

A folder without a trailing path delimiter is joined to a file or folder name with +: results of ExtractFileDir, ExcludeTrailingPathDelimiter, GetCurrentDir, GetHomePath, TPath.GetDocumentsPath and the other TPath.Get…Path folders, TPath.GetDirectoryName, TDirectory.GetCurrentDirectory and TDirectory.GetParent, called directly or held in a local variable that the routine only assigns such values to. The result is C:\Datadatei.txt instead of C:\Data\datei.txt. ExtractFilePath, IncludeTrailingPathDelimiter and TPath.GetTempPath end with a delimiter and stay silent, as do delimiters, extensions (ExtractFileExt, '.bak') and texts that start with a space or punctuation. A literal name reports with high confidence, an identifier with medium confidence. A local variable passed to a procedure other than ForceDirectories, FileExists and similar read-only calls may be changed there and stays silent. Quick fix: TPath.Combine(Folder, Name), adding System.IOUtils to uses.

Flagged:

```pascal
Ordner := TPath.GetDocumentsPath;
Datei := Ordner + 'export.csv';
```

Correct:

```pascal
Ordner := TPath.GetDocumentsPath;
Datei := TPath.Combine(Ordner, 'export.csv');
```

<a id="hardcoded-path"></a>
### hardcoded-path

**Hard-coded file path** · Code smell · Hint · Group Robustness and patterns

A string literal with a drive letter (C:\…, also with /) or a UNC path (\\server\share\…); paths under C:\Users or C:\Documents and Settings get their own message. On other computers the path is missing. Drive roots such as 'C:\' alone and the prefixes \\?\ and \\.\ stay silent, as do comments, test units and units listed in allowUnits (for example a unit with the default paths of an installation). High confidence, no quick fix: take the path from TPath (GetDocumentsPath, GetHomePath, GetSharedDocumentsPath), the settings or an environment variable.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowUnits` | string-list | `[]` | Units in which hard-coded paths are allowed, by unit name; * at the end matches a prefix (App.Config*). |

Flagged:

```pascal
Protokoll.SaveToFile('C:\Users\marco\Desktop\log.txt');
```

Correct:

```pascal
Protokoll.SaveToFile(TPath.Combine(TPath.GetDocumentsPath, 'log.txt'));
```

<a id="filename-compare-case"></a>
### filename-compare-case

**File names compared with =** · Bug · Hint · Group Robustness and patterns

A comparison with = or <> where one side is a file or path name: the result of ExtractFileName, ExtractFilePath, ExtractFileDir, ExtractFileExt, ExpandFileName, ChangeFileExt and related System.SysUtils functions, of TPath.GetFileName, GetExtension, GetDirectoryName, GetFullPath, Combine and related methods, or a variable or property whose name ends in FileName, or Application.ExeName. On Windows Kunde.pas and kunde.PAS are the same file. Comparisons with an empty string, nil or a literal without letters stay silent, as does ExtractFileDir(X) = X (both sides come from the same text). It also stays silent when the same statement compares the same two sides ignoring case (SameText, AnsiSameText, CompareText, AnsiCompareText, SameFileName, AnsiCompareFileName), for example platform-dependent with PathDelim = '\', and in test units (test doubles compare with names the test assigns itself). Medium confidence. Quick fix: SameFileName(A, B), or not SameFileName(A, B) for <>; adds System.SysUtils to uses if needed.

Flagged:

```pascal
if ExtractFileName(Datei) = 'Config.ini' then
```

Correct:

```pascal
if SameFileName(ExtractFileName(Datei), 'Config.ini') then
```

<a id="initialization-not-finalized"></a>
### initialization-not-finalized

**Object created at startup is not freed** · Bug · Warning · Group Robustness and patterns

An object is created with TClass.Create in the initialization section and assigned to a unit variable, or created in a class constructor and assigned to a class var, but the unit never frees it (no Free, Destroy, DisposeOf, Release or FreeAndNil anywhere in the unit). FastMM reports a leak on shutdown; in packages the object stays behind on every unload. Interface and record types, components with an owner (Application, Self, Owner) and objects the unit hands over stay silent: every mention of the variable must be harmless (member access, comparison with nil, Assigned, a new Create or nil assignment); passing it as an argument, assigning it elsewhere, with, a property reading it or FreeOnTerminate silence the rule. For variables in the interface section and public class vars the rule also stays silent when another indexed unit uses the name. High confidence. Quick fix: FreeAndNil at the end of finalization or in the class destructor; a missing finalization section or class destructor (declaration and body) is created and System.SysUtils is added to uses when needed.

Flagged:

```pascal
initialization
  GProtokoll := TProtokoll.Create;
end.
```

Correct:

```pascal
initialization
  GProtokoll := TProtokoll.Create;

finalization
  FreeAndNil(GProtokoll);
end.
```

<a id="compiler-warnings-disabled"></a>
### compiler-warnings-disabled

**Compiler warnings or checks switched off up to the end of the unit** · Code smell · Warning · Group Robustness and patterns

{$WARNINGS OFF}, {$HINTS OFF}, {$WARN X OFF}, {$R-}, {$Q-} (also combined as {$R-,Q-}) or the long forms {$RANGECHECKS OFF} and {$OVERFLOWCHECKS OFF} without a later restore up to the end of the unit. Messages and checks disappear far beyond the intended spot, and the 0/0/0 rule is bypassed. Any later ON, +, DEFAULT or ERROR for the same switch counts as a restore, also inside {$IFOPT}/{$IFDEF} patterns, as does a later {$POPOPT}. A switch whose counterpart stands in another branch of the same {$IF} ({$IFDEF DEBUG}{$R+}{$ELSE}{$R-}{$ENDIF}) is a deliberate setting per configuration and stays silent, as does a directive with no code after it; {$R *.dfm} is a resource, not a range check. Option allow lists switches that may stay off (warning names such as SYMBOL_PLATFORM, WARNINGS, HINTS, R or RANGECHECKS, Q or OVERFLOWCHECKS). High confidence. Quick fix: insert the restore ({$WARNINGS ON}, {$WARN X DEFAULT}, {$R+} …) after the next block; for range and overflow checks only individually, since {$R+} may switch on a check the project has off.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allow` | string-list | `[]` | Switches that may stay off up to the end of the unit: warning names for {$WARN} (SYMBOL_PLATFORM), WARNINGS, HINTS, R or RANGECHECKS, Q or OVERFLOWCHECKS; case is ignored. |

Flagged:

```pascal
{$WARNINGS OFF}
function AlteBerechnung(const Wert: Integer): Integer;
begin
  Result := Wert * 2;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
{$WARNINGS OFF}
function AlteBerechnung(const Wert: Integer): Integer;
begin
  Result := Wert * 2;
end;
{$WARNINGS ON}
```

<a id="dll-export-unsafe"></a>
### dll-export-unsafe

**Exported routine not safe across DLL boundaries** · Bug · Warning · Group Robustness and patterns

A routine of this file listed in exports has no calling convention (stdcall, cdecl, safecall, winapi or pascal; register is the Delphi convention) or takes or returns a string (string, UnicodeString, AnsiString, UTF8String, RawByteString), a dynamic array (TBytes, TArray<T>, …DynArray, array of T declared in the file), an object or an interface without GUID. Callers in other languages or Delphi versions crash; strings and dynamic arrays live in the memory manager of the DLL. WideString, ShortString, PChar, open array parameters and records are fine. Objects are only recognised for certain: classes of the file, of the index and common RTL classes; interfaces only from the file. Routines from other units and overloads that the exports entry does not pin down stay silent. With ShareMem or SimpleShareMem in uses, strings and dynamic arrays are allowed (option allowShareMem). The finding sits on the exports entry; for strings it recommends PChar with buffer and length. High confidence. Quick fix: add stdcall to every header of the routine (only individually, since it changes the interface for existing callers).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowShareMem` | boolean | `true` | With ShareMem or SimpleShareMem in uses, strings and dynamic arrays are allowed in exported routines. |

Flagged:

```pascal
function KundeName(Id: Integer): string;
…
exports
  KundeName;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function KundeName(Id: Integer; Puffer: PChar; Laenge: Integer): Integer; stdcall;
…
exports
  KundeName;
```

<a id="destructor-not-nil-safe"></a>
### destructor-not-nil-safe

**Destructor uses fields that may be nil** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

If a constructor raises, Delphi calls the destructor right away, even though not all fields exist yet. Reported is an access in the destructor (or in a private method that only the destructor calls) to a field such as FLines.Clear, FFile.Position or FLines[0], when a constructor of the class creates the field (TClass.Create) only after a call that can raise or only on some paths. The resulting access violation hides the original exception. Free, FreeAndNil, DisposeOf and accesses after Assigned(FField) or FField <> nil are nil-safe; BeforeDestruction does not run on this path. Constructors of RTL classes that raise at most EOutOfMemory (TStringList.Create, TObjectList<T>.Create(True)), inherited Create and Supports do not count as raising. High confidence. Quick fix: put if Assigned(FField) then in front of the statement. With onlyAfterRaisingCode off, every field created in the constructor counts.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `onlyAfterRaisingCode` | boolean | `true` | Only report when the constructor calls something that can raise before it creates the field. |

Flagged:

```pascal
constructor TImport.Create(const Datei: string);
begin
  inherited Create;
  FStrom := TFileStream.Create(Datei, fmOpenRead);
  FZeilen := TStringList.Create;
end;

destructor TImport.Destroy;
begin
  FZeilen.Clear;
  FZeilen.Free;
  FStrom.Free;
  inherited;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
destructor TImport.Destroy;
begin
  if Assigned(FZeilen) then
    FZeilen.Clear;
  FZeilen.Free;
  FStrom.Free;
  inherited;
end;
```

<a id="handle-leak"></a>
### handle-leak

**Windows handle is not closed** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A Windows handle in a local variable is not closed with its counterpart on some path to the end of the routine: CreateFile, CreateEvent, CreateMutex, OpenProcess, CreateProcess (hProcess, hThread) and CreateToolhelp32Snapshot with CloseHandle, FindFirstFile with FindClose, RegOpenKeyEx/RegCreateKeyEx with RegCloseKey, LoadLibrary with FreeLibrary, GetDC with ReleaseDC, CreatePen/CreateBrush/CreateFont/CreateBitmap with DeleteObject, CreateCompatibleDC with DeleteDC and others. Returning the handle, storing it in a field or global, passing it to an unknown routine or capturing it in an anonymous method counts as handing it on. Branches in which an if check says the open failed (H = 0, H = INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE, RegOpenKeyEx(...) <> ERROR_SUCCESS) and exception paths (see pair-without-finally) do not count. The option pairs adds your own pairs. Medium confidence. Quick fix when the handle is never closed: wrap the statements after the open in try … finally with the close call, or append the close call when the open is the last statement.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pairs` | json | `[]` | Additional open/close pairs as "Open>Close" or {"open": …, "close": …}; the open function returns the handle, the close procedure takes it as its only argument. |

Flagged:

```pascal
H := CreateFile(PChar(Datei), GENERIC_READ, 0, nil, OPEN_EXISTING, 0, 0);
if H = INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE then
  RaiseLastOSError;
ReadFile(H, Puffer, SizeOf(Puffer), Gelesen, nil);
```

Correct:

```pascal
H := CreateFile(PChar(Datei), GENERIC_READ, 0, nil, OPEN_EXISTING, 0, 0);
if H = INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE then
  RaiseLastOSError;
try
  ReadFile(H, Puffer, SizeOf(Puffer), Gelesen, nil);
finally
  CloseHandle(H);
end;
```

<a id="global-formatsettings-modified"></a>
### global-formatsettings-modified

**Global FormatSettings is modified** · Bug · Warning · Group Robustness and patterns

Assignment to the global FormatSettings or one of its fields (also to the deprecated global variables DecimalSeparator, ShortDateFormat, ThousandSeparator … of older Delphi versions). This changes the formatting for the whole application, for every package in the same process (in an IDE plugin the IDE itself) and for all threads, and is not thread-safe; it is often a workaround to force a dot as decimal separator. Reported only when the name is surely the variable from System.SysUtils: no local declaration, no member of the class or its ancestors (silent when the chain of ancestors ends at an unknown class), not inside with. Complements format-without-settings, which reports formatting calls without settings. High confidence. Quick fix when the routine changes the global settings only once and formats something afterwards: declare a local TFormatSettings (TFormatSettings.Invariant for DecimalSeparator := '.', otherwise a copy of FormatSettings with the changed field) and pass it to the formatting calls that follow. With allowInProgram on, assignments in the .dpr are allowed.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowInProgram` | boolean | `false` | Allow the assignments in the .dpr, for example once at program start. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TExport.Schreiben;
begin
  FormatSettings.DecimalSeparator := '.';
  Zeile := FloatToStr(Betrag);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TExport.Schreiben;
var
  FS: TFormatSettings;
begin
  FS := TFormatSettings.Invariant;
  Zeile := FloatToStr(Betrag, FS);
end;
```

<a id="component-interface-dangling"></a>
### component-interface-dangling

**Component stored as interface stays referenced after Free** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A descendant of TComponent (form, frame, data module, own component) implements an interface, but TComponent does not count references. Reported is storing the object as an interface in a field, a global variable or a list (FView as IObserver passed to Bus.Subscribe, FList.Add, Register, Anmelden …, assigned to a field or global, or added to a TList<IFoo>/TInterfaceList) when the same unit frees the object (Free, Release, DisposeOf, FreeAndNil) and nowhere unregisters it: FreeNotification on the object, another call on the same receiver with the object as argument (Unsubscribe, Remove, Abmelden …), Clear, or another assignment to the same field. After the Free the stored interface points to freed memory. Silent for classes with their own _Release and when the chain of ancestors ends at an unknown class. Also reported, without a quick fix: descendants of TInterfacedObject that are stored this way and additionally freed with Free; the stored interface keeps a freed object, and unregistering first would already free it. Medium confidence. Quick fix: insert the unregistration before the first Free (Receiver.Abmelden(…) when the receiver's type has the counterpart, or Field := nil).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `componentClasses` | string-list | `["TComponent"]` | Base classes without reference counting; with TComponent the known VCL and FMX classes (TForm, TFrame, TDataModule …) count as well. |

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TfrmHaupt.AnsichtOeffnen;
begin
  FAnsicht := TfrmKunden.Create(Self);
  Bus.Anmelden(FAnsicht as IBeobachter);
end;

procedure TfrmHaupt.AnsichtSchliessen;
begin
  FAnsicht.Free;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TfrmHaupt.AnsichtOeffnen;
begin
  FAnsicht := TfrmKunden.Create(Self);
  Bus.Anmelden(FAnsicht as IBeobachter);
end;

procedure TfrmHaupt.AnsichtSchliessen;
begin
  Bus.Abmelden(FAnsicht as IBeobachter);
  FAnsicht.Free;
end;
```

<a id="unit-name-mismatch"></a>
### unit-name-mismatch

**Unit name does not match the file name** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

The name after unit differs from the file name, usually after renaming the file outside the IDE (like E1038, W1038). Case does not matter, dots in the name do. In a program, library or package, an entry uses X in 'File.pas' whose file declares a different unit is reported too (like E2085); the rule reads that file. If the unit's own name does not match its file either, Delphi 13 only reports E1038 in the unit, and the uses entry stays silent. Programs, libraries and packages themselves are not compared with their file name, because the compiler does not either. {$WARN UNIT_NAME_MISMATCH OFF} or {$WARNINGS OFF} before the unit name silences the rule like the compiler. Quick fixes: change the unit name to the file name (renaming the file is up to the IDE), or change the uses entry to the unit the file declares.

Matches the compiler message `E1038`, `W1038`, `E2085`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
// File: Kunden.Daten.pas
unit Kunden.Model;

interface
```

Correct:

```pascal
// File: Kunden.Daten.pas
unit Kunden.Daten;

interface
```

<a id="text-after-final-end"></a>
### text-after-final-end

**Text after the final end.** · Code smell · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

Source code after the final end. of a unit, program, library or package. The compiler ignores it (like W1011); usually it is code that ended up at the end of the file while pasting and is never compiled. Comments and directives are not reported (option allowComments). The rule finds the final end. behind the last section of the syntax tree and stays silent while the parser reports errors or when end. is inside an {$IF…} branch; {$WARNINGS OFF} before end. silences it. Quick fix: remove the text after end.

Matches the compiler message `W1011`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowComments` | boolean | `true` | Allow comments and directives after end. |

Flagged:

```pascal
end.

procedure TKunde.Speichern;
begin
```

Correct:

```pascal
end.
```

<a id="exports-invalid"></a>
### exports-invalid

**Invalid exports clause** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

An exports clause inside a routine (like E2191), the export of a threadvar, of a routine declared local, of a constant or of a type (like E2276), or the same export name twice via name '…' (like E2287). Variables and the same entry twice without name compile in Delphi 13 and are not reported. Names are only resolved against global declarations of the file; names from other units and names with a dot stay silent, as do two entries in mutually exclusive {$IF…} branches. Overloaded routines with different parameter lists or export names count as different entries. Complements dll-export-unsafe (#973). Quick fix: remove the duplicate entry.

Matches the compiler message `E2191`, `E2276`, `E2287`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
exports
  Initialisieren name 'Start',
  Berechnen,
  Berechnen name 'Start';
```

Correct:

```pascal
exports
  Initialisieren name 'Start',
  Berechnen;
```

<a id="unit-name-conflict"></a>
### unit-name-conflict

**Unit name conflicts** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A unit is named like a unit reserved for the compiler, System or SysInit (like E2272), or {$NOINCLUDE X} names a unit the compiler does not know (like E2242); the latter needs the project's unit search and stays silent when it gives no sure answer. The RTL sources (source/rtl) are not checked. Not reported: E2447, W1051 and X2421 belong to the namespaces of .NET metadata, which dcc32/dcc64 do not create; for E2399 the documentation gives no condition, and a unit Kunden next to Kunden.Daten compiles without error in Delphi (Spring.pas next to Spring.Collections.pas). Quick fix for {$NOINCLUDE}: use a similarly spelled known unit.

Matches the compiler message `E2272`, `E2242`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
unit System;

interface
```

Correct:

```pascal
unit Kunden.Systemdienste;

interface
```

<a id="implicit-variants-unit"></a>
### implicit-variants-unit

**Variant without System.Variants in uses** · Code smell · Hint · Group Compiler ahead

The unit uses Variant or OleVariant as a type without System.Variants in uses. The compiler then adds the unit silently (like W1040) and so changes the initialization order. The rule counts type references in the syntax tree (variables, fields, parameters, results, type arguments); Variants in uses also counts inside an {$IF…} branch and without namespace. Silent in System, SysInit and Variants themselves, when the file declares its own type Variant, and after {$WARN IMPLICIT_VARIANTS OFF}. Off by default (strict profile). Quick fix: add System.Variants to uses (Variants when the unit names SysUtils or Classes without namespace).

Matches the compiler message `W1040`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix implicit-variants-unit`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
uses
  System.SysUtils;
…
var Wert: Variant;
```

Correct:

```pascal
uses
  System.SysUtils, System.Variants;
…
var Wert: Variant;
```

<a id="file-encoding-unsupported"></a>
### file-encoding-unsupported

**Encoding or character the compiler does not read** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

The file is UTF-32 encoded (like F2438; recognized by its byte order mark, no further findings then), or the code outside strings, comments and directives contains a character the compiler rejects (like E2038): typographic quotes copied from a web page or a word processor, the ASCII characters ! " ? \ ` | ~ and punctuation, symbols and emoji beyond ASCII. Letters, digits and connectors such as U+203F stay allowed in identifiers; asm blocks and {$IF…} branches are skipped; control, format and space characters are left to control-char and invisible-char. Quick fix: a pair of quotes on one line becomes a string literal; a single character with an unambiguous ASCII counterpart (– − × ÷ ≤ ≥ ≠, full-width ASCII) is replaced. A UTF-32 file has no quick fix; save it as UTF-8 with BOM.

Matches the compiler message `F2438`, `E2038`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
Titel := ‚Bericht‘;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Titel := 'Bericht';
```

<a id="anonymous-capture-invalid"></a>
### anonymous-capture-invalid

**Anonymous method captures what it must not capture** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

An anonymous method uses a var, out or untyped parameter, or Result, of a surrounding routine or anonymous function (like E2555). The name is resolved from the inside out (parameters, var/const/type, local routines, inline variables, for var); inside with and asm the rule stays silent, and declarations in conditional branches that exclude the location do not count. Quick fix: copy the value into a local variable before the statement, use only that variable in the anonymous method, and write it back after the statement when the method assigns it.

Matches the compiler message `E2555`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Load(var Count: Integer);
begin
  TTask.Run(procedure begin Inc(Count); end);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Load(var Count: Integer);
var
  CountCopy: Integer;
begin
  CountCopy := Count;
  TTask.Run(procedure begin Inc(CountCopy); end);
  Count := CountCopy;
end;
```

<a id="operator-declaration-invalid"></a>
### operator-declaration-invalid

**Operator declared incorrectly** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A class operator in a record is declared in a way Delphi rejects: unknown operator name (like E2393), wrong number of parameters (E2517), own type missing from the parameters (E2518) or from parameters and result for Implicit/Explicit (E2521), Inc/Dec with a result type other than the first parameter (E2519) or Assign whose first parameter is not a var parameter of the own type (E2464, E2617). Bodies of an unknown operator or of a class operator in a class match no declaration (E2037). In classes, interfaces and object types Delphi 13 rejects every class operator; the rule syntax reports that (E2123). Types count as equal when their names match without unit prefix and type arguments; aliases of the own type declared in the file keep the rule silent, and True, False and OnesComplement are accepted. Comparison operators with a result other than Boolean compile (Spring4D criteria) and stay silent. Quick fixes: nearest operator name, result like the first parameter, var before the first parameter, parameter list from the template; each in the declaration and the implementation.

Matches the compiler message `E2393`, `E2464`, `E2517`, `E2518`, `E2519`, `E2521`, `E2617`, `E2037`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
TGeld = record
  class operator Inc(const A: TGeld): Integer;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TGeld = record
  class operator Inc(const A: TGeld): TGeld;
end;
```

<a id="generic-declaration-invalid"></a>
### generic-declaration-invalid

**Generic declaration not allowed** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A generic declaration that Delphi rejects: class and record constraints together (like E2507), a constraint twice (E2603), record together with constructor (E2516), a class type next to class or record (E2552), two class types (E2553), a constraint type that is neither class nor interface (E2510); type parameters on an enumeration, set, subrange, pointer, class reference, alias or helper (E2508), on a constructor of a class, a destructor or a class constructor (E2528), an operator (E2529), a global routine (E2530), a virtual, dynamic, message or override method (E2533) or an interface method (E2535); class of a parameterized type (E2548), a generic class derived from TCustomAttribute (E2549), a parameterized type as attribute (E2565); a type parameter that hides one of the enclosing type (H2509); a call with a number of type arguments no overload has (E2551, classes and records of the file with their ancestors in the file); a method of a generic type or a generic method declared in the interface section that uses a symbol of the implementation section (E2506; silent inside with and when the ancestor chain is not known); a local routine in a generic method or a method of a generic type (E2570); type before an instantiated type such as type TList<Integer> (E2574, quick fix: remove type and declare an alias). Types count when they are built in, declared in the file or, for class constraints (E2552, E2553), found in the symbol index; unknown types keep the rule silent.

Matches the compiler message `E2506`, `E2507`, `E2508`, `E2510`, `E2516`, `E2528`, `E2529`, `E2530`, `E2533`, `E2535`, `E2548`, `E2549`, `E2551`, `E2552`, `E2553`, `E2565`, `E2570`, `E2574`, `E2603`, `H2509`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Swap<T>(var A, B: T);
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TSwap = record
    class procedure Swap<T>(var A, B: T); static;
  end;
```

<a id="type-restriction-violated"></a>
### type-restriction-violated

**Language rule for records, inline and helpers violated** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Fixed language rules that only the compiler reports: virtual methods (like E2379) or other virtual directives (override, abstract, reintroduce, message, final; E2463) in records, parameterless constructors (E2394), constructors whose parameters all have defaults (E2471) and destructors (E2465) in records, class methods in records without static (E2398); in record helpers virtual methods (E2598) and the other virtual directives (E2475); fields in helpers (class var is allowed, E2599), a destructor in a class helper (E2295; class constructors and helper constructors without a constructor call compile, Skia4Delphi), a class helper descending from a helper whose type the new target does not descend from (E2294, only with a known ancestor chain), a helper used as type of a declaration (E2527) or declared inside a generic type (E2589); several class constructors (E2359, exclusive conditional branches count once), class constructors with parameters (E2360) or virtual, dynamic or message (E2534); methods, properties, class members, types and constants in local or anonymous records (E2433 to E2437); inline on virtual or dynamic methods (E2425), on constructors and destructors (E2442), with open array parameters (E2439), with an asm body (E2426), declared in the interface section and using a variable or typed constant of the implementation section (E2441; true constants, enumeration values, types and routines compile, mORMot2, Clipper2, dcc run of 29.10.2026), as local routine reading variables of the enclosing routine (E2449) or calling its own local routines (E2501); local, absolute or initialized threadvar (E2189, E2190, E2194). The checks apply to all supported Delphi versions (10 Seattle to 13). Option disabledChecks leaves out single codes. Quick fixes: remove the directive, add static.

Matches the compiler message `E2189`, `E2190`, `E2194`, `E2294`, `E2295`, `E2359`, `E2360`, `E2379`, `E2394`, `E2398`, `E2425`, `E2426`, `E2433`, `E2434`, `E2435`, `E2436`, `E2437`, `E2439`, `E2441`, `E2442`, `E2449`, `E2463`, `E2465`, `E2471`, `E2475`, `E2501`, `E2527`, `E2534`, `E2589`, `E2598`, `E2599`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `disabledChecks` | string-list | `[]` | Compiler codes the rule does not check, e.g. ["E2425"]. |

Flagged:

```pascal
TPoint2 = record
  X, Y: Integer;
  function Length: Double; virtual;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TPoint2 = record
  X, Y: Integer;
  function Length: Double;
end;
```

<a id="attribute-usage-invalid"></a>
### attribute-usage-invalid

**Attribute used incorrectly** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A compiler attribute is used where Delphi rejects it: [Weak] on a field or variable whose type is certainly neither a class nor an interface (built-in value types, records, enumerations, sets, arrays, pointer types of the file, aliases followed; like E2584), [Volatile] on a type, routine, method or property (E2325), Weak, Unsafe, Volatile or Ref twice on the same declaration (E2326; branches that exclude each other do not count), or an attribute argument that is certainly a variable or a routine of the file (E2448; visible before the attribute, not shadowed by a constant, type or other declaration of the same name). Custom attributes may be repeated; a type Weak or WeakAttribute declared in the file keeps the rule silent for that name. No quick fix.

Matches the compiler message `E2325`, `E2326`, `E2448`, `E2584`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
TCounter = class
private
  [Weak] FCount: Integer;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TCounter = class
private
  [Weak] FOwner: IInterface;
  FCount: Integer;
end;
```

<a id="typeinfo-invalid"></a>
### typeinfo-invalid

**TypeInfo or Default with an invalid argument** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

TypeInfo or Default receives a variable instead of a type (like E2133, E2537: parameters, local, inline and unit variables, fields, properties, Result, for and exception variables), TypeInfo receives a type without RTTI (enumeration with explicit values, file type, Text/TextFile; E2134), or Default receives a file type (E2538; Delphi 13 also reports E2015 for the assignment). Names are resolved from the inside out; inside with, in helpers, in methods of types outside the file, for declarations in other conditional branches and when the file or the index declares its own TypeInfo or Default the rule stays silent. Quick fix: insert the variable's declared type.

Matches the compiler message `E2133`, `E2134`, `E2537`, `E2538`, `E2015`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
Name := GetEnumName(TypeInfo(Status), Ord(Status));
```

Correct:

```pascal
Name := GetEnumName(TypeInfo(TStatus), Ord(Status));
```

<a id="missing-begin-end"></a>
### missing-begin-end

**Condition or loop without begin … end** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The body of if … then, else, for, while or with is a single statement without begin … end. With the rule, conditions and loops always have a block, so a second statement added later does not end up outside by accident. else if stays allowed (the inner if needs no block of its own); repeat … until, case … end, try … end and asm … end are blocks already. An empty statement (if X then ;) is reported by empty-statement. Bodies with {$IFDEF} are not reported. While the rule is on, redundant-begin-end reports nothing. Quick fixes: put begin … end around the statement, for the whole method or for the whole file, indented by the format settings; a ; before else moves into the block (no E2153); with format.elsePlacement "sameLine", else follows the new end on the same line (end else).

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix missing-begin-end`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `statements` | string-list | `["if","else","for","while","with"]` | Checked statements; add case for the branches of case statements. |
| `allowSingleLineExit` | boolean | `false` | Allow Exit, Continue, Break and raise on the same line as the condition (if … then Exit;). |
| `allowSameLine` | boolean | `false` | Allow a single statement on the same line as the condition or loop header. |

Flagged:

```pascal
if Kunde.Gesperrt then
  Protokoll.Warnung('Gesperrt')
else
  Speichern(Kunde);
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Kunde.Gesperrt then
begin
  Protokoll.Warnung('Gesperrt');
end
else
begin
  Speichern(Kunde);
end;
```

<a id="inherited-constructor-after-try"></a>
### inherited-constructor-after-try

**Inherited constructor only inside try** · Bug · Hint · Group Compiler ahead

A constructor enters a try block before it has called the inherited constructor (like H2505). Self is not initialised there yet; an exception in the block can hit half-created fields. Calls count as the inherited constructor when they are inherited without a name, with the constructor's own name or with a name starting with Create. Branches of {$IFDEF} are checked separately. Quick fix when the call is the first statement of the try block: move it before try.

Matches the compiler message `H2505`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
constructor TDienst.Create;
begin
  try
    inherited Create;
    FListe := TList<TAuftrag>.Create;
```

Correct:

```pascal
constructor TDienst.Create;
begin
  inherited Create;
  try
    FListe := TList<TAuftrag>.Create;
```

<a id="variant-record-managed-field"></a>
### variant-record-managed-field

**Managed type in the variant part of a record** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

The variant part of a record (case … of) holds a string, dynamic array, interface, Variant or a record with such fields (like E2154), a custom managed record with class operator Initialize or Finalize (like E2418), or a type parameter without record constraint (like E2569); likewise after file of (like E2155, E2417). Types are resolved in the file and the index; unknown types stay silent. Quick fixes: move the field before case into the fixed part, use ShortString, or constrain the type parameter with record.

Matches the compiler message `E2154`, `E2155`, `E2418`, `E2417`, `E2569`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
TWert = record
  case Art: TArt of
    waZahl: (Zahl: Double);
    waText: (Text: string);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TWert = record
  Text: string;
  case Art: TArt of
    waZahl: (Zahl: Double);
end;
```

<a id="guid-syntax-invalid"></a>
### guid-syntax-invalid

**GUID with invalid syntax** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

The GUID of an interface or of a typed TGUID constant is not of the form '{8-4-4-4-12}' (like E2204): missing braces, wrong number or length of blocks, characters that are not hex digits, often after editing by hand. Only single literals are checked; concatenations and constants stay silent. Quick fixes: generate a new GUID; when only the braces are missing, add them. Complements interface-guid and duplicate-interface-guid.

Matches the compiler message `E2204`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
ILogger = interface
  ['{6A1F3B2C-9D4E-4F5A-8B7C-1D2E3F4A5B6}']
```

Correct:

```pascal
ILogger = interface
  ['{6A1F3B2C-9D4E-4F5A-8B7C-1D2E3F4A5B6C}']
```

<a id="untyped-pointer-refcount"></a>
### untyped-pointer-refcount

**Untyped pointer bypasses reference counting** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

An interface is assigned through a typecast to Pointer (Pointer(FIntf) := P, like W1070). Reference counting does not take part: the object is freed too early or never.
