The rule reports only when name resolution knows the interface type for sure; strings (string, AnsiString …) are reported as well, with the note "dcc does not report this", since Delphi warns about them only under NEXTGEN. Fields that already carry [weak] or [unsafe] stay silent. Quick fixes for an interface field of this file with Pointer(Y) or nil on the right: mark the field [weak] or [unsafe] and assign without Pointer.

Matches the compiler message `W1070`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
Pointer(FBesitzer) := Pointer(Besitzer);
```

Correct:

```pascal
[weak] FBesitzer: IBesitzer;
…
FBesitzer := Besitzer;
```

<a id="type-too-large"></a>
### type-too-large

**Type exceeds a size limit** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A static array or record is 2 GB or larger (like E2100), or an instance of a generic type of this file is (like E2543). Atlas computes a lower bound from the declarations: index ranges via constant expressions, constants and enumerations of the file, built-in types, records from the index; unknown parts count as 0, so the rule reports only for sure. Only the innermost type that crosses the limit is reported. E2419 (records over 1 MB) applies only to Delphi for .NET and is not reported. No quick fix; use a dynamic array or a pointer instead.

Matches the compiler message `E2100`, `E2543`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
type TPuffer = array[0..High(Integer)] of Int64;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type TPuffer = array of Int64;
```

<a id="comparison-always-constant"></a>
### comparison-always-constant

**Comparison always true or always false** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

A comparison of a variable, field, property or function result of a built-in integer type with a constant outside or at the edge of its value range (like W1021, W1022): Cardinal >= 0 is always true, Byte > 255 always false. Case labels outside the range of the case expression are reported as well (like W1018); single labels that do not fit the width of the type even with the other sign are an error for dcc and are reported by constant-expression-error (like E1012). The rule only reports when the type is known for sure and its declaration is always compiled together with the comparison. NativeInt, LongInt and their unsigned counterparts count with the widest range of all platforms; subrange types are not reported. Length(...) < 0 and Length(...) >= 0 are reported as well, although dcc does not warn there; the message says so.

Matches the compiler message `W1021`, `W1022`, `W1018`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
while Rest >= 0 do  // Rest: Cardinal
```

Correct:

```pascal
while Rest > 0 do
```

<a id="constant-initializer-mismatch"></a>
### constant-initializer-mismatch

**Initializer of a constant does not match** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A typed constant or initialized variable of a static array type has more or fewer elements than its index range (like E2072), typically after adding a value to an enumeration (array[TStatus] of string); checked per dimension and for named array types of the file. Record constants whose fields are in a different order than declared are reported as well (like E2083). Index ranges: subranges with constant bounds, enumerations without own values, Boolean, Byte, Char and so on. Declarations with conditional directives count as unsure. Quick fixes: over an enumeration, add missing or remove surplus entries at their position (recognized by texts, identifiers and comments naming the value; otherwise appended and labelled as estimated), new strings get the text of the value name, other types a default value with a {TODO value} comment (#667); over other ranges, add the missing entries with a placeholder; put the fields in the order of the declaration.

Matches the compiler message `E2072`, `E2083`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
StatusText: array[TStatus] of string = ('Neu', 'Offen', 'Geschlossen');  // TStatus has 4 values
```

Correct:

```pascal
StatusText: array[TStatus] of string = ('Neu', 'Offen', 'Geschlossen', ''{stArchiviert});
```

<a id="case-selector-not-ordinal"></a>
### case-selector-not-ordinal

**case or for without an ordinal type** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

case over an expression that is not ordinal (like E2001): strings, floating point numbers, classes and interfaces; a for loop whose counter is not ordinal (like E2032); case labels and array bounds that are variables, fields, properties, parameters or typed constants instead of constants (like E2026). The rule only reports when the type is known for sure and its declaration is always compiled together with the statement. Quick fix for strings whose labels are all string literals: case IndexStr(S, [...]) of with the positions as labels, System.StrUtils is added to uses.

Matches the compiler message `E2001`, `E2032`, `E2026`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
case Befehl of 'start': Starten; 'stop': Stoppen; end;  // Befehl: string
```

Correct:

```pascal
case IndexStr(Befehl, ['start', 'stop']) of 0: Starten; 1: Stoppen; end;
```

<a id="suspicious-string-pointer-cast"></a>
### suspicious-string-pointer-cast

**Pointer and character width of a string do not match** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

PChar or PWideChar on an AnsiString, RawByteString or UTF8String, and PAnsiChar on a string, UnicodeString or WideString (like W1044): pointer and character width do not match, a typical mistake after the Unicode migration; the API then reads garbage or only the first character. The rule only reports when the type of the argument is known for sure and its declaration is always compiled together with the cast. string(PAnsiChar) converts correctly and a pointer to TBytes may be intended; both are not reported. Quick fixes: declare an own variable with the matching string type (PChar(Pfad) with Pfad: AnsiString becomes Pfad: string), wrap the argument in string(...) or AnsiString(...), use the matching pointer type.

Matches the compiler message `W1044`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
var Pfad: AnsiString; ... DeleteFile(PChar(Pfad));
```

Correct:

```pascal
var Pfad: string; ... DeleteFile(PChar(Pfad));
```

<a id="string-conversion"></a>
### string-conversion

**Conversion between Unicode and ANSI strings** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

Implicit and explicit conversions between string, UnicodeString or WideString on one side and AnsiString, RawByteString or UTF8String on the other, like the compiler warnings: implicit to string (like W1057) or to AnsiString with possible data loss (like W1058) in assignments, inline variables with a type and arguments for value and const parameters of routines without overloads; explicit casts string(A) (like W1059) and AnsiString(S) (like W1060); a string constant with characters the ANSI code page does not know assigned to an AnsiString (like W1058 and W1062); a WideChar in a set expression of character literals such as C in ['0'..'9'] (like W1050); changing a character of a string directly (S[1] := 'x', like W1068). Like dcc, W1059, W1060 and W1068 are only reported after {$WARN EXPLICIT_STRING_CAST ON}, {$WARN EXPLICIT_STRING_CAST_LOSS ON} or {$WARN IMMUTABLE_STRINGS ON}; {$WARN ... OFF} and {$WARNINGS OFF} silence the rule from their position, {$WARN ... ERROR} reports an error. Only reported when both types are known for sure (variables, fields, properties, function results and typed constants of the built-in string types from System, also via a plain alias) and their declarations are always compiled with the statement. Silent: concatenations, casts as source, conversions between two ANSI types (AnsiString, RawByteString, UTF8String; dcc converts by code page), own string types such as type AnsiString(28591), {$H-} and Delphi before 2009. W1062 depends on the ANSI code page of the compiling machine (option ansiCodePage, default 1252; 0 turns this check off); only characters Windows does not map to a best-fit replacement are reported: Hebrew to Thai, Hangul, kana and CJK, symbols and dingbats U+2600-U+27BF and characters outside the BMP (emoji). Project options of the .dproj (DCC_IMPLICIT_STRING_CAST = false, true or error and the like) count as with dcc. Quick fixes: convert explicitly (string(A), AnsiString(S)); use CharInSet and add System.SysUtils to uses.

Matches the compiler message `W1050`, `W1057`, `W1058`, `W1059`, `W1060`, `W1062`, `W1068`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ansiCodePage` | integer | `1252` | ANSI code page of the machine that compiles; characters it surely lacks are reported like W1062 when assigned to an AnsiString. Only 1252 is known; 0 or another value turns this check off. |

Flagged:

```pascal
function IstZiffer(const Zeichen: Char): Boolean;
begin
  Result := Zeichen in ['0'..'9'];
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function IstZiffer(const Zeichen: Char): Boolean;
begin
  Result := CharInSet(Zeichen, ['0'..'9']);
end;
```

<a id="unsafe-code"></a>
### unsafe-code

**Unsafe code** · Code smell · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

Code that dcc reports as unsafe since Delphi for .NET: the @ operator on a variable, field or parameter (like W1047) and a cast of an untyped Pointer to a class, TObject(P) or TCustomer(P) (like W1048). dcc switches these warnings off by default; the rule only reports where they are on as in dcc: {$WARN UNSAFE_CODE ON} or {$WARN UNSAFE_CAST ON} before the position, or DCC_UNSAFE_CODE or DCC_UNSAFE_CAST = true in the .dproj; ERROR turns the finding into an error, OFF and {$WARNINGS OFF} silence it. @ on routines and methods, typed pointers, casts as the argument (TObject(Pointer(I))) and interfaces stay silent. The rule only reports when the type is known for sure and its declaration is always compiled together with the position.

Matches the compiler message `W1047`, `W1048`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
{$WARN UNSAFE_CAST ON}
...
Kunde := TKunde(Zeiger);
```

Correct:

```pascal
{$WARN UNSAFE_CAST ON}
...
Kunde := Kunden[I]; // TObjectList<TKunde> statt TList mit Zeigern
```

<a id="inaccessible-member"></a>
### inaccessible-member

**Access to a member that is not visible** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Access A.B to a member that is not visible at that point: private or strict private of another unit or class (like E2361), protected or strict protected outside the declaring unit and its descendants (like E2362, E2389). Inside a class method or static method, an instance field, method or property of the class is not reachable (like E2124). A property that refers to a private member of an ancestor in the same unit is reported as a warning (like W1045). The rule only reports when name resolution knows the receiver and its type for sure and finds the member only without regard to visibility; protected members stay visible through a class declared in the same unit (TAccess(Control).Click). A private class constructor does not hide an inherited Create, and a class overload of a method stays reachable from a class method. Protected members reached through a generic receiver (TNode<T>) compile from generic code in other units as well (Spring4D); from non-generic code dcc reports E2362. Declarations in conditional branches, methods of helpers and nested types stay silent.

Matches the compiler message `E2361`, `E2362`, `E2389`, `E2124`, `W1045`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
class function TKonfig.Standard: TKonfig;
begin
  Result := TKonfig.Create;
  Result.Pfad := FBasisPfad;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
class function TKonfig.Standard: TKonfig;
begin
  Result := TKonfig.Create;
  Result.Pfad := Result.FBasisPfad;
end;
```

<a id="unused-private-member"></a>
### unused-private-member

**Private member never used** · Code smell · Hint · Group Compiler ahead

A private or strict private field, method, constant or property is never used in the unit (like H2219, which the compiler only reports for methods; fields too unless includeFields is off). Any occurrence of the name as an identifier counts as a use, also in inactive conditional branches and behind read/write; declarations and method headers do not. Exempt: methods with message, virtual, dynamic, override or abstract, methods of types that implement interfaces, constructors and destructors, members with attributes (RTTI), names from the form file and units with include files. Quick fix: remove the declaration (with its /// documentation) and the body of a method; not when several members share the declaration or the method is overloaded.

Matches the compiler message `H2219`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `includeFields` | boolean | `true` | Also report private fields that are never used (the compiler reports only methods). |

Flagged:

```pascal
  TImport = class
  private
    FAltFormat: Boolean;
    procedure KonvertiereAlt;
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
  TImport = class
  private
  end;
```

<a id="unused-public-member"></a>
### unused-public-member

**Public member never used in the project** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A public or published method, constant or property (optionally also protected members, fields and class variables), or a routine, constant, type or global variable of the interface section (option unitKinds) is used nowhere in the projects of the project group. The search is project-wide via the symbol index: only files whose index usages contain the name are parsed, and every occurrence is resolved by name resolution; an occurrence that cannot be resolved counts as a use. Names in form files (.dfm/.fmx) and in string literals count as uses. Considered used: interface implementations and members of interfaces, virtual, dynamic, override, abstract and message methods, constructors, destructors, operators, enumerators, default properties, implements, members with attributes, published members of TPersistent descendants (streaming), of classes registered with RegisterComponents, RegisterClass or RegisterClasses and of test fixtures, published methods while a form file cannot be read, property accessors, exported routines, class helpers, members of classes with an unknown ancestor. Silent while projects are loading or the index is incomplete, when a file cannot be read, for units with include files and for units of packages or libraries (external users) unless projectKinds allows them. For types, occurrences in their own declaration and in the bodies of their own methods (including the TType.Name header) do not count; types registered with RegisterComponents, RegisterClass or RegisterClasses count as used, and for attribute classes (XyzAttribute) the short form [Xyz] counts. deadCode.keepAttributes and deadCode.keepPatterns from the settings apply in addition to keepAttributes and keepPatterns. Quick fix: mark as intentionally public (lsp-ignore comment).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `visibilities` | string-list | `["public","published"]` | Visibilities that are checked: public, published, protected. |
| `memberKinds` | string-list | `["method","constant","property"]` | Member kinds of types that are checked: method, constant, property, field, classVar. Unit-level declarations follow unitKinds. |
| `projectKinds` | string-list | `["application"]` | Project kinds whose units are checked: application, package, library. Packages and libraries have users outside the project group, so they are off by default. |
| `unitLevel` | boolean | `true` | Also check declarations of the interface section of a unit (kinds from unitKinds). |
| `unitKinds` | string-list | `["routine","constant","type","variable"]` | Unit-level kinds that are checked with unitLevel: routine, constant, type, variable. Helpers are never reported (they are used through the extended type). |
| `scope` | string | `"projectGroup"` | Where uses are searched: projectGroup (all projects of the group) or project (only the projects of the file). |
| `testsCountAsUse` | boolean | `true` | Uses in test projects count; when off, a member used only by tests is reported as such. |
| `stringLiteralsCountAsUse` | boolean | `true` | The name as a string literal ('Name') counts as a use (RTTI, MethodAddress, scripts). |
| `keepAttributes` | string-list | `[]` | Attributes on the member or its type that mark it as used, e.g. "Keep" or "Api". |
| `keepPatterns` | string-list | `[]` | Name patterns (* and ?) for members that are always considered used, e.g. "TPlugin.*" or "*Callback". |

Flagged:

```pascal
  TExport = class
  public
    procedure ExportiereAlsXml;
  end;
// ExportiereAlsXml is called nowhere in the project group
```

Correct:

```pascal
  TExport = class
  public
    procedure ExportiereAlsJson; // called by TMainForm
  end;
```

<a id="unused-declaration"></a>
### unused-declaration

**Declaration in the implementation section never used** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A routine, type, constant or global variable declared in the implementation section of a unit (or in a program or library) is used nowhere in the unit; the compiler does not report these. Any occurrence of the name as an identifier counts as a use, also in inactive conditional branches; not counted are the declarations themselves (including forward declarations), occurrences in the routine's own body (recursion) and, for types, occurrences in their own declaration and in the bodies of their own methods including the TType.Name header. For attribute classes (XyzAttribute) the short form [Xyz] counts. Considered used (service shared with unused-public-member): exported routines, declarations with attributes, names matching deadCode.keepPatterns, types with an attribute from deadCode.keepAttributes, and names from the form file. Bodies of interface routines are left to unused-public-member. Silent for units with include files. Quick fix: mark as intentionally present (lsp-ignore comment).

Flagged:

```pascal
implementation

procedure AltesFormat(const AText: string);  // called nowhere
begin
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
implementation

procedure NeuesFormat(const AText: string);
begin
end;

procedure Exportieren;
begin
  NeuesFormat('x');
end;
```

<a id="unused-unit"></a>
### unused-unit

**Unit not used by any other unit** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A unit of an application is used by no other unit of the uses graph: at most programs name it in their uses clause, and they use none of its identifiers outside the uses clause (Application.CreateForm(TForm1, Form1) counts as a use). Units with an initialization or finalization section (registrations, side effects at start-up) count as used, as do units with a [TestFixture] class that DUnitX finds through RTTI. Medium confidence: code outside the project group, scripts or packages loaded at run time can still use the unit. Silent for units of packages and libraries (users outside the group) unless projectKinds allows them, without projects, while projects are loading or the index is incomplete, and for units the uses graph does not know. Reported at the unit name in the header.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `projectKinds` | string-list | `["application"]` | Project kinds whose units are checked: application, package, library. |

Flagged:

```pascal
program App;
uses
  Haupt,
  AltExport;  // AltExport is used nowhere else
```

Correct:

```pascal
program App;
uses
  Haupt;
```

<a id="unused-uses"></a>
### unused-uses

**Unused unit in uses** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

Nothing from the unit of a uses entry is used: no identifier outside the uses clauses matches one of its interface symbols (including members) or the last part of its name. Same analysis as the dimmed uses entries in the editor (#621): units the index does not know, units with an initialization or finalization section, units from usesKeep or the keep option, units the form file refers to (a global variable of the unit named like the module in Connection = Form1.Connection, see dfm-reference-unit-missing) and entries in inactive {$IFDEF} branches stay. High confidence. Safe quick fix: remove the entry (with its comma; the only entry removes the whole uses clause), also for bulk fixing with --fix unused-uses. Silent in the editor, where the same entries are dimmed (the dimming follows the rule: off, exclude and lsp-ignore remove it as well), without a symbol index (--no-index) and with syntax errors.

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix unused-uses`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `keep` | string-list | `[]` | Units that never count as unused, in addition to usesKeep; also for the dimming in the editor and Clean up uses. |

Flagged:

```pascal
unit Bericht;

interface

uses
  System.SysUtils,
  System.Classes;  // nothing from System.Classes is used

function Titel(const AName: string): string;
```

Correct:

```pascal
unit Bericht;

interface

uses
  System.SysUtils;

function Titel(const AName: string): string;
```

<a id="uses-only-in-implementation"></a>
### uses-only-in-implementation

**Unit belongs in the uses of the implementation part** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A unit in the interface uses is only needed by the implementation part: no identifier of the interface part (outside the uses clauses) refers to it. Such an entry lengthens compile times, because every unit that uses this one is recompiled after a change to it, and creates needless dependencies and cycles. Same analysis as the hint in the editor (#621) and as unused-uses, with name resolution: units the index does not know, units with an initialization or finalization section (their order counts) and entries in inactive {$IFDEF} branches stay. High confidence. Quick fix: move the entry to the uses of the implementation part (creates it if needed, sorted with sortUses). It is not a safe fix: in the implementation uses the unit comes later and can hide identifiers of the same name from other units (TBitmap from Winapi.Windows and Vcl.Graphics). Silent in the editor, where the same entries show the hint (the hint follows the rule: off, exclude and lsp-ignore remove it as well), without a symbol index (--no-index), with syntax errors and outside units.

Flagged:

```pascal
unit Import;

interface

uses
  System.Classes, System.JSON;  // System.JSON is only used below implementation

type
  TImport = class
    procedure Laden(AListe: TStrings);
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
unit Import;

interface

uses
  System.Classes;

type
  TImport = class
    procedure Laden(AListe: TStrings);
  end;

implementation

uses
  System.JSON;
```

<a id="message-method-invalid"></a>
### message-method-invalid

**Message method declared incorrectly** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A message procedure of a class does not have exactly one var parameter (like E2138; the parameter type is not checked, an untyped var parameter is fine), its message number is less than 1 (like E2139), or an earlier message method of the same class already handles the same number (like E2140, usually after copying). Numbers count as equal when their values are known (numbers, constants from the index) or when the expressions are the same text. Methods in mutually exclusive {$IF} branches are not duplicates; the same number in a descendant overrides the handler and is fine. Quick fix for E2138: make a single parameter var, otherwise replace the list with (var Msg: TMessage); the method body in the same file is adjusted as well.

Matches the compiler message `E2138`, `E2139`, `E2140`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure WMSize(var Msg: TWMSize); message WM_SIZE;
procedure WMGroesse(var Msg: TWMSize); message WM_SIZE;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure WMSize(var Msg: TWMSize); message WM_SIZE;
procedure WMMove(var Msg: TWMMove); message WM_MOVE;
```

<a id="interface-declaration-invalid"></a>
### interface-declaration-invalid

**Invalid declaration in an interface** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

An interface type declares something interfaces cannot have: fields (like E2209), visibility sections or method directives such as virtual, dynamic, abstract, override, message or reintroduce (like E2210), the property clauses stored, nodefault or default with a value (like E2207; default; after an array property is fine), or a property an ancestor interface already declares, listed again without a type (like E2206; a redeclaration with a type compiles, also with inherited accessors). A class, record or enumeration as the ancestor of an interface or in the interface list of a class is reported as well (like E2205), and so is the same interface twice in that list (like E2208). Types are resolved through the uses clauses; aliases, unknown types, dispinterfaces and declarations inside conditional branches stay silent.

Matches the compiler message `E2205`, `E2206`, `E2207`, `E2208`, `E2209`, `E2210`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
IKunde = interface
  FName: string;
  function GetName: string;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
IKunde = interface
  function GetName: string;
  property Name: string read GetName;
end;
```

<a id="implements-clause-invalid"></a>
### implements-clause-invalid

**Invalid implements clause** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Delegation with implements breaks one of its fixed rules: implements only in classes (like E2258), only on properties of class or interface type (like E2259; reported for built-in types, records and enumerations), not together with index (like E2260), only on readable properties (like E2261), the getter without a calling convention other than register (like E2262) and not dynamic or message (like E2263; Delphi 13 also reports E2148), each interface implemented by one property only (like E2257) and no method resolution clause for an interface implemented that way (like E2264). Types are resolved through the uses clauses; unknown types, aliases and properties in conditional branches stay silent.

Matches the compiler message `E2257`, `E2258`, `E2259`, `E2260`, `E2261`, `E2262`, `E2263`, `E2264`, `E2148`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
property Logger: ILogger write FLogger implements ILogger;
```

Correct:

```pascal
property Logger: ILogger read FLogger write FLogger implements ILogger;
```

<a id="calling-convention-required"></a>
### calling-convention-required

**Calling convention does not fit** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

The compiler prescribes the calling convention here: constructors and destructors of a class only with register (like E2236), getters and setters of published properties as well (like E2270), and varargs only with cdecl (like E2277 for external routines, otherwise E2591); without external, varargs is allowed on Win32 not at all (like E2277), which the rule reports only when the platform is known. Explicit register is fine; class constructors, accessors outside published sections and inherited accessors stay silent. Quick fix: remove the convention in the declaration and in the method body of the same file, or set cdecl for varargs.

Matches the compiler message `E2236`, `E2270`, `E2277`, `E2591`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
function Formatieren(Muster: PAnsiChar): Integer; stdcall; varargs; external 'msvcrt.dll';
```

Correct:

```pascal
function Formatieren(Muster: PAnsiChar): Integer; cdecl; varargs; external 'msvcrt.dll';
```

<a id="automation-invalid"></a>
### automation-invalid

**OLE Automation declared or called wrongly** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Declarations the compiler rejects in the automated section of a class: fields (like E2175), constructors and destructors (like E2177), dynamic and message methods (like E2178), calling conventions other than register (like E2179), the same dispid twice in one class (like E2180), a property redeclared without type (like E2181), index, stored, default and nodefault on properties (like E2182), read or write pointing to a field of the class (like E2096), a dispid on an override method (like E2185) and records, pointers and sets as type of parameters, results and properties (like E2176). A dispid on a method outside automated is reported as E2185, as Delphi 13 does (documented as E2183). Calls through Variant or OleVariant (V.Open(...), also V.Document.Open(...)): a record without operators or a pointer as argument (like E2160 for OleVariant, like E2281 for Variant) and an unnamed argument after a named one (like E2166). Members in conditional branches and types the name resolution does not know for sure stay silent. That automated itself is deprecated is reported by deprecated-symbol.

Matches the compiler message `E2096`, `E2160`, `E2166`, `E2175`, `E2176`, `E2177`, `E2178`, `E2179`, `E2180`, `E2181`, `E2182`, `E2183`, `E2185`, `E2281`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
TAuto = class
  automated
    procedure Run; stdcall;
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TAuto = class
  automated
    procedure Run;
  end;
```

<a id="dispinterface-invalid"></a>
### dispinterface-invalid

**Invalid dispinterface declaration** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A dispinterface breaks one of its fixed rules: no ancestor in parentheses (like E2228), a GUID is required (like E2229), methods take no directives except dispid and the hints deprecated, platform, library and experimental (like E2230; reported are calling conventions and binding directives such as virtual, overload stays silent) and properties take no read or write (like E2231; use readonly, writeonly or dispid). Forward declarations and declarations in conditional branches stay silent. Quick fixes: remove the ancestor, insert a GUID, remove the directive.

Matches the compiler message `E2228`, `E2229`, `E2230`, `E2231`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
DAnzeige = dispinterface(IBasis) ['{6A0F0D5E-1C11-4B8E-9A43-3F1D4C2A2228}'] procedure Machen; stdcall; dispid 1; end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
DAnzeige = dispinterface ['{6A0F0D5E-1C11-4B8E-9A43-3F1D4C2A2228}'] procedure Machen; dispid 1; end;
```

<a id="abstract-declaration-invalid"></a>
### abstract-declaration-invalid

**abstract, final or static combined wrongly** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A method directive the compiler rejects: abstract without virtual, dynamic or override (like E2167), a body in the implementation section for an abstract method (like E2136), final on a method that is not virtual (like E2351), abstract together with final (like E2371), a class declared abstract sealed (like E2540), an abstract method in a sealed class (like E2383, E2540) or a sealed class that leaves an inherited abstract method open (like E2539, ancestors via the symbol index) or static on an instance method or a virtual class method (like E2376). A body only counts for an abstract method when no non-abstract method of the same name in the same class is compiled with it (overloads, {$IFDEF} branches). Quick fixes: insert virtual; before abstract, remove the body.

Matches the compiler message `E2167`, `E2136`, `E2351`, `E2371`, `E2383`, `E2540`, `E2539`, `E2376`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
TForm = class
  function Flaeche: Double; abstract;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TForm = class
  function Flaeche: Double; virtual; abstract;
end;
```

<a id="declaration-invalid"></a>
### declaration-invalid

**Declaration the compiler rejects** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A declaration Delphi rejects for its form alone: packed before a type other than array, record, object, class, set, file or string (like E2006), a routine returning file (like E2024), a virtual constructor in an object type (like E2062), a property without read, write or index in an interface other than a dispinterface or in an automated section (like E2128), or two constructors with different names and the same parameter types when the project produces C++ output (DCC_CBuilderOutput or -J switch in the .dproj, like W1029). Property redeclarations without a type stay silent. No quick fix: the right correction depends on the intent.

Matches the compiler message `E2006`, `E2024`, `E2062`, `E2128`, `W1029`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TZahl = packed Integer;

function Lesen: file;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TZahl = Integer;

procedure Lesen(var F: file);
```

<a id="unsatisfied-forward"></a>
### unsatisfied-forward

**Forward declaration never satisfied** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A routine declared in the interface section or with forward has no body (like E2065), or a forward declaration TFoo = class; or IFoo = interface; is never completed in the same type section (like E2086, E2420). A record containing itself as a field and a class or interface using a type that is only declared forward as ancestor or in its interface list are incomplete types as well (like E2086). Methods are checked by declared-not-implemented. A body fits when it has the same name; with overload the number of parameters must match too. An external declaration in the implementation section counts as a body. Declarations inside a {$IFDEF} branch are skipped; with include files the rule does not check routines. Quick fix: create an empty body before initialization or the final end.

Matches the compiler message `E2065`, `E2086`, `E2420`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
interface

function Pruefsumme(const Daten: TBytes): Cardinal;

implementation

end.
```

Correct:

```pascal
interface

function Pruefsumme(const Daten: TBytes): Cardinal;

implementation

function Pruefsumme(const Daten: TBytes): Cardinal;
begin
  Result := 0;
end;

end.
```

<a id="member-hides-ancestor"></a>
### member-hides-ancestor

**Member hides a member of the ancestor** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

A property has the name of a field of an ancestor (like W1009), a field has the name of a field or property of an ancestor, or a non-virtual method has the name and parameters of a non-virtual method of an ancestor (the last two without a code: Delphi 13 compiles them silently). Code that accesses the object through the ancestor type still sees the old member. Virtual methods are reported by hides-virtual, local names by shadowed-name. Republished properties (property Caption;), properties with a new type over an ancestor property, overload, reintroduce, constructors, class methods and methods implementing a method of an interface in the class's own ancestor list (QueryInterface over TInterfacedObject) are not reported. Members the descendant cannot see (private in another unit, strict private with ignoreStrictPrivate) are skipped; ancestors come from the file and the symbol index. Quick fix for fields and properties: rename the member in the class declaration and the bodies of its methods in this unit (FName becomes FKundeName).

Matches the compiler message `W1009`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ignoreStrictPrivate` | boolean | `true` | Do not report hidden strict private members of the ancestor. |

Flagged:

```pascal
TBasis = class
  FName: string;
end;
TKunde = class(TBasis)
  FName: string;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TBasis = class
  FName: string;
end;
TKunde = class(TBasis)
  FKundeName: string;
end;
```

<a id="published-type-invalid"></a>
### published-type-invalid

**Invalid member in the published section** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A member behind an explicit published that the compiler rejects: a property of a static array, pointer or plain procedural type (like E2188; records and dynamic arrays compile), of type Real48 (like E2186) or of a set larger than 4 bytes (like E2187), a field that is neither a class nor an interface (like E2217), more than one published overload (like E2266), a class property (like E2545) or a name with non-ASCII characters (like E2452). Types are resolved through the file and the index, aliases are followed; type parameters, unknown types and sections whose published is in another conditional branch than the member stay silent. The section without visibility specifier is not checked, because the compiler silently demotes such members to public there. Quick fix: move the member into the public section.

Matches the compiler message `E2188`, `E2217`, `E2186`, `E2187`, `E2266`, `E2545`, `E2452`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
TKarte = class(TCustomControl)
published
  property Mitte: TPointF read FMitte write SetMitte;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TKarte = class(TCustomControl)
public
  property Mitte: TPointF read FMitte write SetMitte;
end;
```

<a id="override-case-mismatch"></a>
### override-case-mismatch

**Spelling or visibility differs from the ancestor** · Code smell · Hint · Group Compiler ahead

An overriding method spells its name differently from the ancestor (destructor destroy, like H2365) or has a lower visibility than the overridden method (like H2269, only for classes in the interface section; visibilities are compared in tiers private, protected, public), a getter or setter is named Get<Property> or Set<Property> but spelled differently, or an inline method accesses a member that is less visible than the method itself (these two without a code: Delphi 13 reports neither H2367 nor H2440 there). Matters for C++Builder users of the units and for consistent code. The docwiki lists H2269 and H2367 as X2269 and X2367 because their level can be changed; dcc reports them as hints by default. H2368 and H2369 are not covered, since it is not documented when dcc reports them. Off by default; switch it on in atlas.json. Quick fixes: adopt the ancestor's spelling, rename the accessor.

Matches the compiler message `H2365`, `H2269`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
TKunde = class
  destructor destroy; override;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TKunde = class
  destructor Destroy; override;
end;
```

<a id="default-property-invalid"></a>
### default-property-invalid

**default property declared wrongly or missing** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

default; behind a property without index parameters (like E2132), a second default property of the same class with another name or the same index types (like E2131; overloading by index type under the same name is allowed), or Obj[I] on a class that has no default property in its whole ancestor chain (like E2149). The type of Obj is resolved by name resolution; unresolvable types, broken ancestor chains and classes with a class helper in the unit stay silent. Property redeclarations without a type (property Items; default;) take the index from the ancestor and are not checked.

Matches the compiler message `E2131`, `E2132`, `E2149`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
TListe = class
  property Name: string read FName; default;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TListe = class
  property Items[I: Integer]: string read GetItem; default;
end;
```

<a id="property-redeclaration-invalid"></a>
### property-redeclaration-invalid

**Redeclared property missing in the ancestor** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A property redeclaration without type such as property Caption; (to change the visibility or the default value) names a property that no ancestor has (like E2147), or the ancestor's property is not accessible from here: strict private, or private in another unit (like E2233). Only reported when the whole ancestor chain is known; a field or method of the same name and declarations in inactive conditional branches stay silent. Quick fix: use the most similar property name of the ancestors.

Matches the compiler message `E2147`, `E2233`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
TMeinPanel = class(TCustomPanel)
published
  property Captoin;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TMeinPanel = class(TCustomPanel)
published
  property Caption;
end;
```

<a id="inline-not-expanded"></a>
### inline-not-expanded

**Inline function is not expanded** · Code smell · Hint · Group Compiler ahead

A call of an inline routine from another unit that the compiler does not expand: its body needs a unit that is missing from uses here (like H2443, typically System.Generics.Collections for TJSONObject.Count), the calling routine is inline itself and the unit of the callee is only in the implementation uses (like H2445), or the unit of the callee uses this unit directly (like H2456) or through other units (like H2457; only if the body refers to more than built-in types, intrinsics and System). Calls, properties with an inline getter or setter (also the default property behind List[0]) and for-in loops (GetEnumerator, MoveNext, Current) are checked. The index records what each inline body refers to; the rule only reports what it resolves for sure (unknown references and members inherited from other units stay silent; an alias of a System declaration such as tkRecord in System.TypInfo does not need its unit). Access to private or strict private members is not reported: Delphi 13 expands such calls. Silent with {$INLINE OFF} in the file. Quick fix: add the missing units to the uses clause of the section.

Matches the compiler message `H2443`, `H2445`, `H2456`, `H2457`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix inline-not-expanded`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
uses
  System.JSON;
...
for Wert in Feld as TJSONArray do
```

Correct:

```pascal
uses
  System.JSON, System.Generics.Collections;
...
for Wert in Feld as TJSONArray do
```

<a id="compiler-hints"></a>
### compiler-hints

**Compiler hint without its own rule** · Code smell · Hint · Group Compiler ahead

Hints and warnings of the compiler that belong to no other rule. published in a class without RTTI: the compiler switches on {$M+} for it (like W1055); reported once per class at the first member behind the first explicit published, silent with {$M+} or {$TYPEINFO ON} at the declaration, with an ancestor that has RTTI (known descendants of TPersistent, {$M+} at its declaration or own published members) and after {$WARN TYPEINFO_IMPLICITLY_ADDED OFF} or {$WARNINGS OFF}; an ancestor whose chain is not known up to TObject or whose file cannot be read keeps the rule silent, as do generic and nested classes. Initialize or Finalize with a variable of a built-in type without managed parts such as Integer, Double or Boolean, also through aliases (like H2243), or with a variable of a pointer to a managed type such as PString or PVariant, where P^ was probably meant (like H2244); only calls with one plain identifier whose type is known for sure, silent after {$HINTS OFF} and when Initialize or Finalize can mean an own routine or a member (with, method of a class with an unknown ancestor).

Matches the compiler message `W1055`, `H2243`, `H2244`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TEinstellung = class
  published
    property Wert: Integer read FWert;
  end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TEinstellung = class(TPersistent)
  published
    property Wert: Integer read FWert;
  end;
```

<a id="directive-typo"></a>
### directive-typo

**Unknown directive (typo)** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A word that is not a directive stands where a directive belongs: after the ';' of a routine or method header (overide;, virutal;, overlaod;, stdcal;, reintroduces;) or after a property, where only default is allowed (defualt;). The compiler stops there (like E2070). The message suggests the closest directive when at most two characters differ. Silent in {$IF} branches that test FPC, which knows further directives such as nostackframe. Quick fix: replace with the suggested directive.

Matches the compiler message `E2070`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Paint; overide;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Paint; override;
```

<a id="ui-call-in-console-or-service"></a>
### ui-call-in-console-or-service

**Dialog in a console program or service** · Bug · Warning · Group Robustness and patterns

ShowMessage, ShowMessageFmt, MessageDlg, TaskMessageDlg, InputBox, InputQuery, Application.MessageBox or TDialogService in a program with {$APPTYPE CONSOLE} (or DCC_ConsoleTarget in the .dproj), in a Windows service or in a DLL without forms. In a service the dialog blocks invisibly; a console program waits for a click nobody sees when it runs unattended. A service is a unit with a class derived from TService, or any unit of a project whose .dpr uses Vcl.SvcMgr without Vcl.Forms. A DLL without forms is a library whose .dpr lists no form and does not use Vcl.Forms; there the rule reports with medium confidence, because only the host decides whether there is a user interface. Units with a form file, calls under if IsConsole and routines of the same name declared in the unit stay silent. Counterpart of writeln-in-gui-app. High confidence. No quick fix: log the message instead of showing it.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TDatenDienst.ServiceExecute(Sender: TService);
begin
  if not Verbinden then
    ShowMessage('Keine Verbindung');
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TDatenDienst.ServiceExecute(Sender: TService);
begin
  if not Verbinden then
    LogMessage('Keine Verbindung', EVENTLOG_ERROR_TYPE);
end;
```

<a id="load-without-encoding"></a>
### load-without-encoding

**Loading or saving text without an encoding** · Bug · Warning · Group Robustness and patterns

LoadFromFile, SaveToFile, LoadFromStream or SaveToStream of a TStrings (TStringList, Memo.Lines, ListBox.Items, Query.SQL) and TFile.ReadAllText or TFile.ReadAllLines without an encoding: a file without BOM is read with the ANSI code page of the machine, and SaveToFile writes ANSI, so umlauts get lost between machines. The receiver must be a declared string list or a Lines, Items, SQL or Strings property; bitmaps, streams and datasets stay silent. Also silent when the list sets DefaultEncoding anywhere in the unit or was loaded with an encoding earlier in the same routine (SaveToFile then keeps that encoding). TFile.WriteAllText, TStreamReader and TStreamWriter already default to UTF-8 and are not reported. Medium confidence. Quick fix: add TEncoding.UTF8 (option defaultEncoding) as the last argument and System.SysUtils to uses.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `defaultEncoding` | string | `"UTF8"` | TEncoding property the quick fix inserts: UTF8, Unicode, BigEndianUnicode, ANSI, ASCII, UTF7 or Default. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Zeilen.LoadFromFile(Pfad);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Zeilen.LoadFromFile(Pfad, TEncoding.UTF8);
```

<a id="registry-hklm-write"></a>
### registry-hklm-write

**Writing to HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE** · Bug · Hint · Group Robustness and patterns

RootKey := HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE on a TRegistry followed in the same routine by a writing access on the same object: OpenKey(…, True), CreateKey, Write…, DeleteKey, DeleteValue, RenameValue, MoveKey, SaveKey or RestoreKey. Without administrator rights this fails or silently lands in the registry virtualization. Use HKEY_CURRENT_USER for user settings; exclude installer code through exclude. Reading (OpenKeyReadOnly, Read…, OpenKey(…, False)), another RootKey before the write and writes in the other branch of {$IFDEF} stay silent. High confidence. Quick fix: RootKey := HKEY_CURRENT_USER.

Flagged:

```pascal
Reg.RootKey := HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE;
Reg.OpenKey('Software\Firma\Programm', True);
Reg.WriteString('Sprache', Sprache);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Reg.RootKey := HKEY_CURRENT_USER;
Reg.OpenKey('Software\Firma\Programm', True);
Reg.WriteString('Sprache', Sprache);
```

<a id="now-for-elapsed-time"></a>
### now-for-elapsed-time

**Elapsed time measured with Now** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A variable set to Now and later used in Now - Start, End - Start or MilliSecondsBetween, SecondsBetween, MinutesBetween, HoursBetween, DaysBetween or the …Span functions in the same routine: it measures elapsed time with the wall clock, which is coarse and wrong across daylight saving changes or clock synchronisation. TStopwatch is precise and monotonic. Assignments in another routine (fields) and in the other branch of {$IFDEF} stay silent. High confidence. Quick fix when the variable is a local TDateTime (also var Start := Now) used only for MilliSecondsBetween, SecondsBetween or MinutesBetween with Now: TStopwatch.StartNew and ElapsedMilliseconds (div 1000 or div 60000), the variable becomes Uhr when the name is free, System.Diagnostics is added to uses.

Flagged:

```pascal
Start := Now;
Importieren;
Log(MilliSecondsBetween(Now, Start).ToString + ' ms');
```

Correct:

```pascal
Uhr := TStopwatch.StartNew;
Importieren;
Log(Uhr.ElapsedMilliseconds.ToString + ' ms');
```

<a id="money-as-float"></a>
### money-as-float

**Money amount as floating point** · Bug · Hint · Group Style and metrics

Field, variable, parameter or property whose name denotes a money amount (option namePatterns) with type Double, Single, Extended or Real: binary floating point rounds cent amounts wrongly (0.1 + 0.2), Currency calculates them exactly. The last part of the name counts (GesamtPreis, Nettobetrag, FNetto, Preise; not NettoGewicht); rates, factors, quotas, shares (Steuersatz, PriceFactor) and checksums stay silent. Ambiguous words (Amount, Summe, Sum, Total: a quantity or sum of anything) only count with a money context in the type or routine: another money name, Currency or words like Cent, Euro, Rabatt, Invoice, Payment, Kosten (Lighten(AColor, AAmount: Double) stays silent). Off by default and low confidence: it shows up once the rule is switched on and minConfidence is low. Quick fix when all names of the declaration are money amounts: type Currency, for parameters also in the header of the implementation, for fields and properties also the property or the field behind read and write; no fix when a getter or setter would have to change too. With checkRound the rule also reports Round with a money amount in the argument (banker's rounding: 2.5 becomes 2).

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `namePatterns` | string-list | `["Betrag","Preis","Summe","Netto","Brutto","Steuer","Amount","Price"]` | Name parts that denote a money amount; plurals included. |
| `checkRound` | boolean | `false` | Also report Round on money amounts. |

Flagged:

```pascal
TPosition = record
  Menge: Integer;
  Preis: Double;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
TPosition = record
  Menge: Integer;
  Preis: Currency;
end;
```

<a id="string-if-chain"></a>
### string-if-chain

**Long if chain over the same string** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

An if … else if chain that compares the same string (identifier or A.B, no function calls) with at least min literals: S = 'x', 'x' = S, SameText(S, 'x'), also combined with or, in at least two branches (a single if with or stays silent). case IndexStr(S, [...]) of or a dictionary is shorter and faster. Only comparisons that are always compiled together with the head count ({$IFDEF} branches inside the chain do not). Off by default, high confidence. Quick fix as in the code action if chain to case (#646): case IndexStr(…) of, IndexText(…) for SameText, comparisons joined with or share one label, the rest of the chain becomes else, System.StrUtils is added to uses. No fix when comments or directives sit between the branches, a literal appears twice or = and SameText are mixed.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `min` | integer | `4` | Number of comparisons of the same string from which the rule reports; at least 2. |

Flagged:

```pascal
if Befehl = 'neu' then Neu
else if Befehl = 'oeffnen' then Oeffnen
else if Befehl = 'speichern' then Speichern
else if Befehl = 'beenden' then Beenden;
```

Correct:

```pascal
case IndexStr(Befehl, ['neu', 'oeffnen', 'speichern', 'beenden']) of
  0: Neu;
  1: Oeffnen;
  2: Speichern;
  3: Beenden;
end;
```

<a id="prefer-case"></a>
### prefer-case

**if chain can be written as case** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

An if … else if chain with at least min branches (the final else counts) that compares the same ordinal expression (integer, character, enumeration; no side effects, no function call) with constants: X = a, a = X, X in [a, b..c], (X >= a) and (X <= b), also combined with or. Labels must be literals, constant expressions, untyped constants or enumeration values, without duplicates or overlaps. The type comes from name resolution; chains with unknown types and chains with directives stay silent. Off by default, high confidence. Quick fix: the code action In case umwandeln (#646, refactoring.ifCase); begin … end only where a branch has several statements, comments in the branches stay.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `min` | integer | `4` | Number of branches (the final else included) from which the rule reports; at least 2. |

Flagged:

```pascal
if Kind = 1 then Neu
else if (Kind = 2) or (Kind = 3) then Oeffnen
else if (Kind >= 4) and (Kind <= 6) then Speichern
else Beenden;
```

Correct:

```pascal
case Kind of
  1: Neu;
  2, 3: Oeffnen;
  4..6: Speichern;
else
  Beenden;
end;
```

<a id="constant-condition"></a>
### constant-condition

**Condition is always the same** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

The condition of if, while or until is fixed: True, False, comparisons of numbers and untyped constants of the file, also through and, or, xor and not (X or True is always true, X and False always false). The branch is dead code or always runs. while True do and repeat … until False count as deliberate endless loops (infinite-loop checks their exit). Constants listed in switchConstants, constants inside a {$IF…} branch and constants from other units count as unknown. Quick fix for if: remove the dead branch or the condition.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `switchConstants` | string-list | `[]` | Constants that deliberately serve as switches; conditions on them are not reported. |

Flagged:

```pascal
if (Count > 0) or True then
  Process;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Count > 0 then
  Process;
```

<a id="infinite-loop"></a>
### infinite-loop

**Loop without exit** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

while True do, repeat … until False or another loop whose condition never ends it, without an exit in the body: no Break (a Break in a nested loop only leaves that loop), Exit, raise, goto, Halt, Abort or RunError, no call of a routine of the file that raises an exception or ends the program, and no check of Terminated (thread loops are covered by thread-loop-without-terminated). Anonymous methods in the body do not count. The action jumps to the loop head.

Flagged:

```pascal
while True do
begin
  Line := Reader.ReadLine;
  Process(Line);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
while not Reader.EndOfStream do
begin
  Line := Reader.ReadLine;
  Process(Line);
end;
```

<a id="loop-condition-unchanged"></a>
### loop-condition-unchanged

**Loop condition never changes in the body** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

No variable of the while or until condition changes in the body: no assignment, no Inc/Dec, no argument (possibly var), no method or member access on the object of the condition, no for loop over it. Typically a forgotten Inc(I) or Next. The rule only reports when every variable of the condition is a local variable or value parameter of the routine and at least one appears directly (not only as X.Property). Conditions with function calls, fields, global variables, var and out parameters and Terminated are not reported, nor loops with an exit (Break, Exit, raise …), waiting loops (Sleep, ProcessMessages …), with, calls of local routines or variables captured by an anonymous method. Changes through X[…] count only when the condition reads X[…].

Flagged:

```pascal
I := 0;
while I < List.Count do
begin
  Check(List[I]);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
I := 0;
while I < List.Count do
begin
  Check(List[I]);
  Inc(I);
end;
```

<a id="loop-runs-at-most-once"></a>
### loop-runs-at-most-once

**Loop runs at most once** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

Every path through the body of a for, for-in, while or repeat loop ends with Exit, Break, raise, Halt, Abort, RunError, ExitProcess or a call of a routine of the file that raises, so the loop runs at most once. Usually an if is missing before the jump, or Continue was meant. A path counts as a jump for if with else and both branches, case with else and all branches, try … finally with the try or the finally part and try … except with the try part and every handler. Silent with Continue of this loop, goto or labels and conditional directives in the body; Break of inner loops does not count. Complements infinite-loop and loop-condition-unchanged. No quick fix, the intent is unclear; if the jump directly follows an if without else, the message asks whether it belongs into the if branch. High confidence.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `allowFirstElement` | boolean | `false` | Do not report a for-in loop of simple statements that ends with Exit or Break (fetching the first element). |

Flagged:

```pascal
for mCustomer in FCustomers do
begin
  if mCustomer.Id = AId then
    Result := mCustomer;
  Exit;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
for mCustomer in FCustomers do
  if mCustomer.Id = AId then
    Exit(mCustomer);
```

<a id="unconditional-recursion"></a>
### unconditional-recursion

**Recursion without a stop** · Bug · Error · Group Find bugs

A routine calls itself on every path before it can return: there is no path without a self-call and no Exit or raise before it. The result is a stack overflow. Paths without a call are: an if without else or a case without else, the body of while and for, the right side of and/or, branches of if expressions and anonymous methods. Getters and setters of their own property are reported by setter-recursion; overloaded routines stay silent (the call may go to another overload). The margin marker from #623 shows every recursion.

Flagged:

```pascal
function TTree.Depth(N: TNode): Integer;
begin
  Result := 1 + Max(Depth(N.Left), Depth(N.Right));
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function TTree.Depth(N: TNode): Integer;
begin
  if N = nil then
    Exit(0);
  Result := 1 + Max(Depth(N.Left), Depth(N.Right));
end;
```

<a id="pos-arguments-swapped"></a>
### pos-arguments-swapped

**Arguments of Pos swapped** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

Pos(Text, ';'): Pos, PosEx, AnsiPos, StartsText, StartsStr, EndsText and EndsStr (also with Ansi prefix) expect the searched part first and the text second; ContainsText and ContainsStr expect the text first. Reported when a short literal (up to maxLiteralLength characters, default 3; '' counts as one character, #9 as well) stands where the text belongs and a certain string stands where the part belongs: a variable or field of a string type declared in the file, .Text or .Caption, a string function of the RTL (Trim, UpperCase, Copy-like LeftStr, Format …) or a concatenation with a string part. Characters stay silent, because Pos(C, ';,') deliberately checks whether C is one of the separators; so do unknown types, two literals and methods of other objects (Liste.Pos). Medium confidence. Quick fix: swap the arguments in one edit; comments between them stay.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `maxLiteralLength` | integer | `3` | Longest literal in the place of the text that is still taken as swapped. |

Flagged:

```pascal
if Pos(Zeile, ';') > 0 then
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Pos(';', Zeile) > 0 then
```

<a id="integer-division-to-float"></a>
### integer-division-to-float

**Integer division assigned to a floating-point number** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

Average := Sum div Count with Average: Double: div cuts off the fraction before the value is assigned. Also Percent := Part div Total * 100, Result of a function with a floating-point result and var X: Double := A div B. The div is searched through parentheses, signs and + - * /, not inside calls (Round(A div B)), indexes, mod or comparisons. The target must be a variable, field (also Self.X) or Result whose type Single, Double, Extended, Real, Currency … is declared in the file. TDateTime, TDate and TTime stay silent (whole days are usually intended there), as do assignments inside with and Trunc(A) div Trunc(B) (also Round, Floor, Ceil: explicitly converted, so integer division is intended). High confidence. Quick fix: replace div with / (same precedence).

Flagged:

```pascal
var Average: Double;
…
Average := Sum div Count;
```

Correct:

```pascal
var Average: Double;
…
Average := Sum / Count;
```

<a id="ignored-result"></a>
### ignored-result

**Result of a function without side effects discarded** · Bug · Warning · Group Find bugs

A function without side effects is called as a statement on its own, so its result is lost: Trim(S); does not change S, S.Replace('a', 'b'); does not either. Reported are the standard routines listed below (also with the unit in front, SysUtils.Trim), methods of TStringHelper without side effects when the receiver is surely of type string, and the functions from the option pureFunctions. If name resolution finds the name in a unit outside the RTL, or the file declares a routine of that name itself (an own Trim with a var parameter), the rule stays silent; likewise inside with blocks. Routines with var or out parameters and routines that raise on bad input on purpose (StrToInt or S.ToInteger used as a check) are not on the list. High confidence. Quick fix "Assign the result": if the function returns the type of its first argument or of the helper receiver (Trim, StringReplace, Copy, S.Replace …) and that is assignable, it becomes S := Trim(S); otherwise a new inline variable var Value := … (only directly in a begin … end block and from Delphi 10.3 on). Standard routines by unit: System: Abs, Assigned, Chr, Concat, Copy, Frac, High, Int, Length, Low, Odd, Ord, Pos, Pred, Round, SizeOf, Sqr, Sqrt, StringOfChar, Succ, Trunc, UpCase; System.SysUtils: IntToStr, IntToHex, FloatToStr, CurrToStr, BoolToStr, Format, FormatFloat, QuotedStr, AnsiQuotedStr, Trim, TrimLeft, TrimRight, LowerCase, UpperCase, AnsiLowerCase, AnsiUpperCase, StringReplace, SameText, SameStr, CompareText, CompareStr, AnsiSameText, AnsiSameStr, AnsiCompareText, AnsiCompareStr, AnsiPos, StrToIntDef, StrToInt64Def, StrToFloatDef, StrToBoolDef, ExtractFileName, ExtractFileExt, ExtractFilePath, ExtractFileDir, ExtractFileDrive, ChangeFileExt, ChangeFilePath, IncludeTrailingPathDelimiter, ExcludeTrailingPathDelimiter, IncludeTrailingBackslash, ExcludeTrailingBackslash, AdjustLineBreaks, IsDelimiter, LastDelimiter, string.Join, string.Create, string.Format, string.IsNullOrEmpty, string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace, string.Compare, string.CompareOrdinal, string.CompareText, string.LowerCase, string.UpperCase, string.EndsText; System.StrUtils: PosEx, ContainsText, ContainsStr, StartsText, StartsStr, EndsText, EndsStr, MatchText, MatchStr, IndexText, IndexStr, LeftStr, RightStr, MidStr, ReplaceStr, ReplaceText, AnsiReplaceStr, AnsiReplaceText, AnsiContainsText, AnsiStartsText, AnsiEndsText, DupeString, ReverseString, SplitString, IfThen; System.Math: Max, Min, Ceil, Floor, Power, IntPower, Sign, EnsureRange, InRange, SameValue, IsZero, RoundTo, SimpleRoundTo, Log10, Log2; System.IOUtils: TPath.Combine, TPath.GetFileName, TPath.GetFileNameWithoutExtension, TPath.GetExtension, TPath.GetDirectoryName, TPath.ChangeExtension. TStringHelper: Trim, TrimLeft, TrimRight, TrimStart, TrimEnd, ToUpper, ToLower, ToUpperInvariant, ToLowerInvariant, Replace, Substring, PadLeft, PadRight, Insert, Remove, QuotedString, DeQuotedString, Contains, StartsWith, EndsWith, IndexOf, IndexOfAny, LastIndexOf, LastIndexOfAny, IsEmpty, Split, ToCharArray, Equals, CompareTo, CountChar, IsDelimiter, LastDelimiter, GetHashCode.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pureFunctions` | string-list | `[]` | Further functions without side effects that are reported like the standard routines, as Name, Class.Name or Unit.Name. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Trim(Input);
S.Replace('a', 'b');
```

Correct:

```pascal
Input := Trim(Input);
S := S.Replace('a', 'b');
```

<a id="empty-finally"></a>
### empty-finally

**Empty finally block** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A finally block without statements: usually the release got lost during a rework. If the statement right before try is X := T….Create, the message names X.Free. A block with an explaining comment counts as deliberate and is not reported (as with empty-except); a block with a compiler directive is skipped as well, because another branch may contain code. High confidence. Quick fix: remove try, finally and end and outdent the body (when try, finally and end each stand on their own line); as the only statement of an if, while, for, with or case branch with several statements in the body, try becomes begin.

Flagged:

```pascal
Liste := TStringList.Create;
try
  Liste.LoadFromFile(Pfad);
finally
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Liste := TStringList.Create;
try
  Liste.LoadFromFile(Pfad);
finally
  Liste.Free;
end;
```

<a id="form-resource-missing"></a>
### form-resource-missing

**Form unit without {$R *.dfm}** · Bug · Error · Group Forms and data

A .dfm or .fmx lies next to the unit and the unit declares the class of its root object, but no {$R …} directive includes a form file: creating the form raises EResNotFound. Conversely, a {$R *.dfm} or {$R Name.dfm} whose file does not exist next to the unit is reported (the compiler stops with F1026). A directive in any branch of conditional compilation counts as included. Silent when the form file's class is not declared in the unit and when the rule cannot see files next to the unit. Quick fix: insert {$R *.dfm} (or {$R *.fmx}) after implementation.

Matches the compiler message `F1026`.

Flagged:

```pascal
implementation

procedure TfrmKunde.FormShow(Sender: TObject);
```

Correct:

```pascal
implementation

{$R *.dfm}

procedure TfrmKunde.FormShow(Sender: TObject);
```

<a id="form-autocreate-and-manual"></a>
### form-autocreate-and-manual

**Form created automatically and by hand** · Bug · Warning · Group Forms and data

The .dpr creates a form with Application.CreateForm, and the code also assigns TfrmX.Create(…) to the same global variable. There are then two instances; the first one stays invisible in memory. The automatically created forms come from the .dpr of the project the unit belongs to; when the rule can read the .dpr, the class must match the CreateForm call. Silent when the routine checks the variable (Assigned, comparison with nil) or frees it before (Free, FreeAndNil, Release), for local names and for fields of the own class with the same name, and for units without a project. High confidence. Quick fix: remove the Application.CreateForm line from the .dpr (the form is then only created by the code).

Flagged:

```pascal
// Projekt.dpr: Application.CreateForm(TfrmBericht, frmBericht);
frmBericht := TfrmBericht.Create(Application);
frmBericht.ShowModal;
```

Correct:

```pascal
// Projekt.dpr without CreateForm for TfrmBericht
frmBericht := TfrmBericht.Create(Application);
frmBericht.ShowModal;
```

<a id="inherited-form-handler-without-inherited"></a>
### inherited-form-handler-without-inherited

**Event handler in an inherited form without inherited** · Bug · Warning · Group Forms and data

With form inheritance, an ancestor form assigns a handler of the same name to the same event of the same component, but the handler in the descendant does not call inherited (any more). The IDE generates inherited; when the handler is created; once it is deleted, the logic of the ancestor is lost silently. The ancestor's form file comes from the index (file of the class) or from the units in the uses clause next to the unit; the chain follows inherited forms up to the first form with object. Handlers with a different name count as a deliberate replacement. Silent without the ancestor's form file. High confidence. Quick fix: insert inherited; at the start.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TfrmKundeBearbeiten.btnSpeichernClick(Sender: TObject);
begin
  FKunde.Speichern;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TfrmKundeBearbeiten.btnSpeichernClick(Sender: TObject);
begin
  inherited;
  FKunde.Speichern;
end;
```

<a id="self-free-in-event-handler"></a>
### self-free-in-event-handler

**Form frees itself in its own event** · Bug · Error · Group Forms and data

Free, Destroy, DisposeOf or FreeAndNil on Self, or on the global variable of the form's own class, as a statement in an event handler of the form or one of its controls. After the handler returns, the VCL still accesses the object: access violation. Correct is Release, or Action := caFree in OnClose. Event handlers are the methods named in the form file and methods with a Sender: TObject parameter; a form is the root class of the form file with ClientWidth or ClientHeight, or a class derived directly from TForm or TCustomForm (frames and data modules have no Release). Statements in anonymous methods and with blocks do not count. Silent without the form file. High confidence. Quick fix: replace Free with Release; in OnClose (var Action: TCloseAction) with Action := caFree.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TfrmMeldung.btnOKClick(Sender: TObject);
begin
  Free;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TfrmMeldung.btnOKClick(Sender: TObject);
begin
  Release;
end;
```

<a id="dfm-reference-unit-missing"></a>
### dfm-reference-unit-missing

**Form file refers to a module whose unit is not in the uses clause** · Bug · Warning · Group Forms and data

A property in the form file points to a component of another form, frame or data module (Connection = Form1.Connection), but the unit of that module is neither in the interface nor in the implementation uses clause. When the form is opened, the IDE only opens the modules from the uses clause along with it; otherwise the reference stays empty and is lost when the form is saved. At run time the VCL only resolves the reference while a module of that name exists. The module is the name of the root component (Form1, not TForm1). Its unit comes from the .dpr of the project (Unit1 in 'Unit1.pas' {Form1}, high confidence) or else from the index: a global variable of that name in the interface of a unit that declares its type as a class (medium confidence). Silent for references to the own form, to own components (embedded frames such as Frame11.Button1) and to modules whose unit is known neither from the .dpr nor from the index. Reported at the field of the component that holds the reference, otherwise at the form class; the line in the form file is shown as a related location. Quick fix: add the unit to the implementation uses clause, as the IDE offers.

Flagged:

```pascal
// Unit2.dfm: Connection = Form1.Connection
implementation

{$R *.dfm}
```

Correct:

```pascal
// Unit2.dfm: Connection = Form1.Connection
implementation

uses
  Unit1;

{$R *.dfm}
```

<a id="parallel-for-shared-write"></a>
### parallel-for-shared-write

**Shared variable written in TParallel.For** · Bug · Warning · Group Threads

The body of TParallel.For, or of a TTask.Run/Create/Future started inside a loop, writes a variable from outside without a lock: Summe := Summe + …, Inc/Dec or another assignment, or changes a collection from outside (Add, Delete, Clear, Insert, Remove …). The iterations run at the same time, so additions get lost or the list is corrupted. Outside means a local variable or parameter of the enclosing routine, a field of its class or a global variable of the unit; names of unknown origin stay silent. Bodies with a lock (TMonitor, Enter, Acquire, Lock, LockList, BeginWrite, TInterlocked), writes through an index (Werte[I] := …) and nested anonymous methods (Synchronize, Queue) do not count. Types listed in threadSafeTypes count as thread-safe. Medium confidence. Quick fixes: TInterlocked.Add/Increment/Decrement for Integer, Int64 and Cardinal (adds System.SyncObjs), System.TMonitor.Enter/Exit in try … finally around the collection call.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `threadSafeTypes` | string-list | `["TThreadList","TThreadedQueue"]` | Types that count as thread-safe; their collection calls stay silent. |

Flagged:

```pascal
TParallel.For(0, High(Werte), procedure(I: Integer)
  begin
    Summe := Summe + Werte[I];
  end);
```

Correct:

```pascal
TParallel.For(0, High(Werte), procedure(I: Integer)
  begin
    TInterlocked.Add(Summe, Werte[I]);
  end);
```

<a id="thread-unsafe-increment"></a>
### thread-unsafe-increment

**Counter in a thread without TInterlocked** · Bug · Warning · Group Threads

Inc, Dec, X := X + 1 or X := X - 1 without a lock on a global variable in Execute of a TThread descendant, or on a field or global variable in the body of TTask.Run/Create/Future outside loops and of TThread.CreateAnonymousThread. Inc is not atomic: when several threads count at the same time, increments get lost. Thread classes come from the unit (also through intermediate classes) and from the index. Own fields of the thread in Execute and captured local variables of a single task have only one writer and stay silent, as do bodies with a lock (TMonitor, Enter, Acquire, Lock, LockList, BeginWrite, TInterlocked) and nested anonymous methods (Synchronize, Queue). Tasks inside loops and TParallel.For are reported by parallel-for-shared-write. Medium confidence. Quick fix: TInterlocked.Increment/Decrement/Add for Integer, Int64 and Cardinal (adds System.SyncObjs).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TArbeiter.Execute;
begin
  while not Terminated do
    if Holen then Inc(GVerarbeitet);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TArbeiter.Execute;
begin
  while not Terminated do
    if Holen then TInterlocked.Increment(GVerarbeitet);
end;
```

<a id="constant-expression-error"></a>
### constant-expression-error

**Error in a constant expression** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A constant expression divides by zero (like E2098) or overflows (like E2099: beyond Int64 when all operands are signed, such as High(Int64) + 1, otherwise beyond UInt64), a range has its low bound above its high bound (array[5..1], like E2011), a constant violates an integer type of fixed size or a subrange type (like E1012; values that only fit the same width with the other sign, such as $FFFFFFFF for Integer or -1 for Cardinal, like W1012 as a warning; assignments only from values outside 0..127), a constant index lies outside a static array of the file or a single case label outside the integer type of the case expression (like E1012), a hex, binary or octal literal needs more than 64 bits (like E2102; assigned to Int64 additionally like W1012), a set has more than 256 elements (set of 0..300, set of Word, like E2028) or a {$RTTI} visibility set contains numbers or strings instead of vcPrivate, vcProtected, vcPublic and vcPublished (like E2572). Atlas evaluates literals, constants (also from other units) and SizeOf/High/Low of types with a fixed size; a constant from an {$IF} branch only counts where the checked place is compiled together with it. Expressions with unknown parts stay silent.

Matches the compiler message `E2098`, `E2099`, `E2011`, `W1012`, `E2102`, `E1012`, `E2028`, `E2572`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
const
  Seiten = 0;
  ProSeite = 100 div Seiten;
```

Correct:

```pascal
const
  Seiten = 5;
  ProSeite = 100 div Seiten;
```

<a id="default-parameter-invalid"></a>
### default-parameter-invalid

**Invalid default value of a parameter** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Default values the compiler rejects: several names sharing one default value (A, B: Integer = 0, like E2237), a parameter without a default value after one with a default value (like E2238), var and out parameters with a default value (like E2239) records or static arrays with a default value (like E2268) and sets larger than 4 bytes with a default value (like E2146, only small sets are allowed; medium confidence). Applies to routines, methods and procedural types. Quick fixes: declare the parameters one by one; move the parameters with a default value to the end, also in the matching declaration or implementation header of the same file (calls are not changed).

Matches the compiler message `E2237`, `E2238`, `E2239`, `E2268`, `E2146`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Senden(const Text: string = ''; Ziel: TZiel);
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure Senden(Ziel: TZiel; const Text: string = '');
```

<a id="signed-unsigned-mix"></a>
### signed-unsigned-mix

**Signed and unsigned mixed** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

Comparison or arithmetic (+, -, *, div, mod) with one signed and one unsigned operand whose types are known for certain (variables, fields, properties, function results, own aliases). With UInt64 the whole expression is computed unsigned and a negative value becomes a huge number (like W1073, comparison like W1023); with Cardinal/DWORD and a signed type up to 32 bits both operands are widened to Int64 (like W1023/W1024). Silent for Byte and Word, Cardinal with Int64, literals, constants, NativeInt/NativeUInt and unknown types. Medium confidence. Quick fix: convert the unsigned operand to Int64.

Matches the compiler message `W1023`, `W1024`, `W1073`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
if Groesse + Delta < Grenze then  // Groesse: UInt64; Delta: Int64
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Int64(Groesse) + Delta < Grenze then
```

<a id="narrowing-assignment"></a>
### narrowing-assignment

**Assignment with possible data loss** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

An assignment or an inline variable with a value whose type is wider than the target, without an explicit conversion: Int64 to Integer, NativeInt to Integer (Win64) and, via the option types, e.g. Double to Single or Extended to Double. Like W1072 (floating point) and W1066 (Extended to Double); integers without a code, because Delphi 13 does not report W1071 even with {$WARN IMPLICIT_INTEGER_CAST_LOSS ON}. Only reported when both types are known for certain (variables, fields, properties, function results, own aliases and integer arithmetic built from them); literals and explicit conversions stay silent. Off by default; medium confidence. Quick fixes: convert explicitly; widen the type of the target's declaration (single variable declared in the same file).

Matches the compiler message `W1072`, `W1066`.
