Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `types` | string-list | `["Int64>Integer","NativeInt>Integer"]` | Reported pairs source type>target type, e.g. Double>Single or Extended>Double. |

Flagged:

```pascal
Anzahl := Strom.Size;  // Anzahl: Integer
```

Correct:

```pascal
Anzahl := Integer(Strom.Size);
```

<a id="typed-constant-modified"></a>
### typed-constant-modified

**Typed constant modified** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

A typed constant (const Counter: Integer = 0) is assigned (like W1017, also an element or a record field of it) or passed as a var or out argument, including Inc, Dec, Include, Exclude, Delete, SetLength and the like (like W1016). Usually a variable was meant; this only compiles with {$J+}, and with {$J+} in effect before the declaration the rule stays silent like dcc. When {$J-} or {$WRITEABLECONST OFF} is in effect before the declaration in the same file or in a file it includes before that point, it is reported as an error (like E2064 or E2197). Also reports 0 as the value of a pointer constant or an initialised pointer variable (like W1013). Quick fixes: turn a unit-level constant into a variable with an initial value (the const section is split if needed; local typed constants keep their value between calls and get no fix); write nil instead of 0.

Matches the compiler message `W1016`, `W1017`, `W1013`, `E2064`, `E2197`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
const
  Zaehler: Integer = 0;
…
Inc(Zaehler);
```

Correct:

```pascal
var
  Zaehler: Integer = 0;
…
Inc(Zaehler);
```

<a id="string-length-limit"></a>
### string-length-limit

**String limit exceeded** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

A string literal is truncated to fit a string[N] or ShortString target: assignment, typed constant or initialised variable, also via own aliases, string[Constant] and fields from other units (like W1014). Before Delphi 12 (delphiVersion below 23) a quoted literal may have at most 255 characters (like E2056), and a resourcestring may have at most 4096 characters, literals joined with + included (like E2381); both are errors. Characters are counted as written, '' and #13 count as one. Quick fixes: declare the target as string (single variable or typed constant of the same file; record fields keep their size); split the literal into pieces of 255 characters joined with +.

Matches the compiler message `W1014`, `E2056`, `E2381`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
var Kennung: string[8];
…
Kennung := 'KUNDE-2026-0001';
```

Correct:

```pascal
var Kennung: string;
…
Kennung := 'KUNDE-2026-0001';
```

<a id="dynamic-array-misuse"></a>
### dynamic-array-misuse

**New, Dispose or SetLength misused on a dynamic array** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

New (like E2255) or Dispose (like E2008 in Delphi 13, documented as E2256) on a dynamic array, SetLength without a length (like E2246), New with a length on a type that is not a dynamic array (like E2008 in Delphi 13, documented as E2307) and New(TArrayType) without a length (like E2308). Typical for old code that switched from pointers to dynamic arrays. The rule only reports when name resolution knows the variable or type for sure; aliases are followed (TValues = array of Double), TBytes and the other dynamic arrays of the RTL count even without an index; open array parameters and pointers stay silent. Quick fixes: Dispose(A) becomes A := nil, New(A) becomes SetLength(A, 1).

Matches the compiler message `E2255`, `E2008`, `E2256`, `E2246`, `E2307`, `E2308`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
Dispose(Werte);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Werte := nil;
```

<a id="local-routine-as-callback"></a>
### local-routine-as-callback

**Local routine as procedure variable** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A local (nested) routine is assigned to a variable or passed to a parameter of a procedural type without of object (like E2094), or a typed constant of a routine takes the address of a local variable (like E2278). Usually an anonymous method or a routine of the unit was meant. The rule only reports when name resolution knows the target type for sure; method pointers and method references stay silent because the compiler reports them with other codes, and so does @Routine (an untyped pointer). Quick fix: move the routine in front of the enclosing routine as a routine of the unit, if it uses nothing from it (no local names further out, no class members without a prefix).

Matches the compiler message `E2094`, `E2278`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TListe.Sortieren;
  function Vergleich(A, B: Pointer): Integer;
  begin
    Result := 0;
  end;
begin
  FListe.Sort(Vergleich);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function Vergleich(A, B: Pointer): Integer;
begin
  Result := 0;
end;

procedure TListe.Sortieren;
begin
  FListe.Sort(Vergleich);
end;
```

<a id="hresult-integer-mix"></a>
### hresult-integer-mix

**Integer and HRESULT interchanged** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

A class implements an interface method with an HRESULT result through a method with an Integer (or LongInt) result, or the other way round (like W1008; the Delphi help calls it X1008). Both are 32 bits wide, so it goes unnoticed, but the meaning of the values (S_FALSE as success) is lost; important for COM and Open Tools API code. Methods are matched by name and parameter types, including resolution clauses such as function IFoo.Bar = Baz; interfaces and their ancestors come from the file or the index. If variants stand in conditional branches, the rule only reports when every variant differs. Quick fix: align the result type in declaration and implementation with the interface.

Matches the compiler message `W1008`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
IExport = interface
  function Schreiben: HRESULT; stdcall;
end;
…
  function Schreiben: Integer; stdcall;
```

Correct:

```pascal
IExport = interface
  function Schreiben: HRESULT; stdcall;
end;
…
  function Schreiben: HRESULT; stdcall;
```

<a id="invalid-var-argument"></a>
### invalid-var-argument

**Invalid argument for a var or out parameter** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A var or out parameter receives something that is not a variable: a constant, a constant expression such as cMax + 1, a const parameter, a property or a function result (like E2197), a constant for an open array (like E2192) or an expression (like E2036); @ before an arithmetic or logical expression is reported as well (like E2036). A string element for a character parameter (S[1]) is a variable for dcc and stays silent (E2354 exists only for .NET, #1383). Reported only for routines without overloads whose header is known from the file or the index; built-in routines such as Inc or Move and untyped var parameters accept more and stay silent. Typed constants count as constants unless {$J+} is set. Quick fix: pass a local variable of the parameter type, and write it back for properties with write.

Matches the compiler message `E2197`, `E2192`, `E2036`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Zaehlen(var Wert: Integer);
…
Zaehlen(Liste.Count);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Anzahl := Liste.Count;
Zaehlen(Anzahl);
```

<a id="condition-not-boolean"></a>
### condition-not-boolean

**Condition is not Boolean** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

The condition of if, while, until or a conditional expression has a type that is surely not Boolean: a number, a pointer, a class, an interface, a string or a character (like E2012). Unlike C, Delphi needs a Boolean. Reported only when the type is certain; Boolean types such as LongBool, enumerations, aliases and unknown types stay silent. Quick fix: add <> 0 (alternative > 0), <> nil (alternative Assigned), <> '' or <> #0.

Matches the compiler message `E2012`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
if Liste.Count then
  Verarbeiten;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Liste.Count > 0 then
  Verarbeiten;
```

<a id="type-mismatch"></a>
### type-mismatch

**Incompatible types** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

An assignment, argument, var parameter, operator, typecast or procedural type uses incompatible types, and both types are certain: Integer := 'abc', a class parameter with default 0, a static array := nil, a procedure without result as a value, string + Integer or [1] + ['a'] (like E2010); an operator that is not defined for an operand, such as string - Char, Double div 2, not 'a' or X is Integer (like E2015); a var argument whose type differs from the parameter, such as Byte for Integer (like E2033); Byte(S) of a long string and an ordinal typecast of a floating-point value (like E2089); Inc/Dec with a floating-point or string step and Ptr(S) (like E2008); a routine with another parameter list for a procedural type, or a method for a type without of object and vice versa (like E2009); of overloaded routines none fits the procedural type (like E2010); a routine for a method reference (reference to, also generic such as TFunc<string, TArray<string>>) whose parameters differ in count, const/var/out or type, assigned or passed as an argument, and likewise an anonymous method such as procedure(const S: string) for TProc<string> (like E2010); a parameterless function whose result has exactly the procedural type of the target, assigned or passed without () (like E2010: Delphi takes the function itself). As in dcc, Result has the type from the interface part, and of two declarations of the same local name the last one counts. Reported only when both sides are certain and their declarations are always compiled with the statement; records (class operator Implicit), Variant, pointers, subranges, generics, overloaded routines and with blocks stay silent. Quick fix only for the function without (): add (). Otherwise no quick fix: only the developer knows which side is wrong.

Matches the compiler message `E2010`, `E2008`, `E2009`, `E2015`, `E2033`, `E2089`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
var
  Anzahl: Integer;
begin
  Anzahl := 'drei';
```

Correct:

```pascal
var
  Anzahl: Integer;
begin
  Anzahl := 3;
```

<a id="for-in-not-enumerable"></a>
### for-in-not-enumerable

**for-in over a type without enumerator** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A for-in loop runs over a type that cannot be enumerated: a number, pointer, character or Boolean, or a class, record or interface without a public GetEnumerator, also not inherited or from a helper (like E2430); or the enumerator returned by GetEnumerator lacks a public MoveNext or Current (like E2431). Strings, arrays and sets are fine. Reported only when the type and all its ancestors are known; generic types and unknown types stay silent. No quick fix; if the type has Count, the message suggests an indexed loop.

Matches the compiler message `E2430`, `E2431`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
for F in Felder.Dataset do
```

Correct:

```pascal
for F in Felder do
```

<a id="type-kind-required"></a>
### type-kind-required

**Expression or declaration lacks the required kind of type** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

An operation needs a certain kind of type that the operand surely does not have: an index on a number, Boolean, character or enumeration (like E2016); ^ on such a value, a class, a record or an interface (like E2017); a dot or with on such a value (like E2018); an object type whose ancestor is not an object type, TypeOf on a simple value and Dispose with destructor on a pointer to a simple type (like E2019); a class whose ancestor is a record, class reference, enumeration or simple type (like E2021); a class helper whose ancestor is not a helper (like E2022); New with constructor on a pointer to a simple type (like E2467); a record helper for a class or interface (like E2474); a method without class called through the class type outside methods of that class and its descendants (like E2076); Slice outside an open array argument (like E2193); a record inside an array passed to array of const (like E2150); Write/Writeln or Read/Readln with a class, record or interface (like E2054, E2055). Reported only when the type is certain: unknown types, conditional declarations, visible helpers, units missing from the index, records with an Implicit operator and unknown ancestors stay silent. Quick fixes: remove ^ before a dot for classes, records and interfaces; write Object.ToString in Write/Writeln.

Matches the compiler message `E2016`, `E2017`, `E2018`, `E2019`, `E2021`, `E2022`, `E2467`, `E2474`, `E2076`, `E2193`, `E2150`, `E2054`, `E2055`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
Kunde^.Name := 'A';
Writeln(ErrOutput, Kunde);
```

Correct:

```pascal
Kunde.Name := 'A';
Writeln(ErrOutput, Kunde.ToString);
```

<a id="inherited-abstract-call"></a>
### inherited-abstract-call

**inherited calls an abstract method** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

inherited with an explicit method name, such as inherited Zeichnen(C), although the method is abstract in the ancestor: the call raises EAbstractError at runtime. The compiler accepts it without an error. A bare inherited; is skipped silently by the compiler for abstract methods and is not reported. The method is resolved by name resolution including overloads; the rule only reports when every matching overload is abstract. Quick fix: remove the call when it is a statement of its own in a begin…end block.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TKreis.Zeichnen(C: TCanvas);
begin
  inherited Zeichnen(C);
  C.Ellipse(FRahmen);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TKreis.Zeichnen(C: TCanvas);
begin
  C.Ellipse(FRahmen);
end;
```

<a id="override-only-inherited"></a>
### override-only-inherited

**Override only calls inherited** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

An overriding method whose body only calls the inherited method: a bare inherited;, inherited Name or inherited Name(A, B) with its own parameters in unchanged order, in functions only Result := inherited Name(…). It does nothing of its own and pretends a changed behaviour. Silent when the method changes the visibility of the ancestor's method (protected to public), carries an attribute, default values or the directives final, deprecated, experimental, platform, library or reintroduce, has local declarations, more than one statement, comments or conditional directives in the body, or when the overridden method is unknown (ancestor not indexed; virtual methods of TObject count as public). High confidence. Quick fix: remove declaration and body, including XMLDoc comments and the blank line after the body.

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TKundenForm = class(TForm)
  protected
    procedure Loaded; override;
  end;

procedure TKundenForm.Loaded;
begin
  inherited Loaded;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TKundenForm = class(TForm)
  end;
```

<a id="inherited-without-ancestor"></a>
### inherited-without-ancestor

**inherited without a method in the ancestor** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A bare inherited; in a method that no ancestor declares (a new method, no override) does nothing. This points to a forgotten override or a misspelled name. Not reported: message handlers (inherited calls DefaultHandler there), class and static methods, constructors and destructors, override and reintroduce, inherited inside anonymous methods, ancestors with a member of that name (also non-virtual) and unknown ancestors. Ancestors from other units come from the symbol index; methods of TObject count as known when System is not indexed. High confidence. Quick fix: remove inherited when it is a statement of its own in a begin…end block.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Vorbereiten;
begin
  inherited;
  FZeilen.Clear;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TImport.Vorbereiten;
begin
  FZeilen.Clear;
end;
```

<a id="redundant-jump"></a>
### redundant-jump

**Redundant Exit or Continue** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A bare Exit; as the last statement of a routine or anonymous method, or a bare Continue; as the last statement of a loop body (for, for-in, while, repeat), also in the last branch of an if at the end (the then branch only without else; then Continue else … guards an else-if chain). The jump changes nothing. Not reported: Exit with a value, jumps inside try…finally or try…except (the jump deliberately triggers the finally part), inside case, qualified names such as System.Continue, statements with a goto label and bodies with conditional directives. High confidence. Quick fix: remove the statement when it stands in a begin…end block or directly in repeat.

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure TPuffer.Leeren;
begin
  FLaenge := 0;
  Exit;
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
procedure TPuffer.Leeren;
begin
  FLaenge := 0;
end;
```

<a id="generic-constraint-violated"></a>
### generic-constraint-violated

**Type argument violates a constraint** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A type argument does not satisfy the constraint of its type parameter: class (like E2511), record (like E2512), constructor (like E2513), an interface (like E2514) or a base class (like E2515). Inside methods of a generic type of this file the rule also reports T.Create without a constructor constraint (like E2568) and method calls on values of a type parameter without any constraint (like E2571). The generic type is resolved by name resolution including the number of type parameters, its constraints come from its declaration in the file or in the symbol index. Arguments that are type parameters themselves, aliases and types whose ancestry cannot be resolved are not reported. Quick fix for a type parameter of this file that stands alone in its group: add the constraint (T: class, constructor or T: class).

Matches the compiler message `E2511`, `E2512`, `E2513`, `E2514`, `E2515`, `E2568`, `E2571`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
  TFabrik<T: class> = class
    function Neu: T;
  end;
…
  Result := T.Create;
```

Correct:

```pascal
  TFabrik<T: class, constructor> = class
    function Neu: T;
  end;
…
  Result := T.Create;
```

<a id="generic-inference-failed"></a>
### generic-inference-failed

**Type arguments cannot be inferred** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A call of a generic method without type arguments whose type parameters cannot be inferred from the arguments: a type parameter that occurs in no parameter, for example only in the result (like E2531), or arguments of different kinds (number, string, Boolean) for parameters whose type is the same type parameter (like E2532). E2531 is reported as a statement, as a call with parentheses and on the right side of an assignment to a value of known, non-procedural type, and only when every overload has such a type parameter. E2532 is reported only for exactly one routine without overload; arguments of unknown type (Variant, classes, expressions) and numbers of different size keep the rule silent. Like argument-count-mismatch it is silent for declarations in conditional branches and names that an unknown ancestor can hide.

Matches the compiler message `E2531`, `E2532`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
Zahl := TFabrik.Neu;
TFabrik.Paar(1, 'a');
```

Correct:

```pascal
Zahl := TFabrik.Neu<Integer>;
TFabrik.Paar<string>('1', 'a');
```

<a id="dunitx-areequal-length"></a>
### dunitx-areequal-length

**Assert.AreEqual with integer and NativeInt** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Assert.AreEqual or Assert.AreNotEqual with an integer on one side and a NativeInt on the other: Length, High or Low of a dynamic array (NativeInt since Delphi 12) or an expression whose type is certainly NativeInt or NativeUInt (TList<T>.Count with the RTL in the index). None of the overloads Integer, Int64, Double … fits unambiguously, so Delphi falls back to AreEqual<T> and cannot infer T (like E2532). Free Pascal compiles the call, so the error only shows when building with Delphi. Strings (Length returns Integer), static arrays, expressions of unknown type, calls with a type argument and a known Delphi version before 12 stay silent. Quick fix (safe, also for the whole file): wrap the NativeInt side in Integer(...).

Matches the compiler message `E2532`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix dunitx-areequal-length`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
Assert.AreEqual(5, Length(mObjects));
```

Correct:

```pascal
Assert.AreEqual(5, Integer(Length(mObjects)));
```

<a id="exit-array-literal"></a>
### exit-array-literal

**Exit with array literal** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Exit([...]) in a function whose result is a dynamic array (TArray<T>, array of T, TBytes, TStringDynArray … or a type of the same unit declared as one of these). Delphi reads the brackets in Exit as a set constructor, not as an array literal, and rejects the call (like E2001 or E2010); Free Pascal compiles it, so the error only shows when building with Delphi. Result := [...] is fine. Exit() inside an anonymous method, an empty [] and functions with another result type stay silent. Quick fix (safe): Result := [...]; Exit;.

Matches the compiler message `E2001`, `E2010`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix exit-array-literal`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
function Namen: TArray<string>;
begin
  Exit(['a', 'b']);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function Namen: TArray<string>;
begin
  Result := ['a', 'b'];
  Exit;
end;
```

<a id="caption-string-helper"></a>
### caption-string-helper

**String helper on TCaption** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A method of TStringHelper (StartsWith, Contains, Trim, ToUpper, Length …) is called on an expression of type TCaption, such as Caption or the Text of a control. TCaption is declared as type string, a distinct type, and the string helper does not apply to it (like E2018). Free Pascal accepts the call. Reported only when the type of the expression is certainly TCaption. Quick fix (safe): wrap the expression in string(...).

Matches the compiler message `E2018`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix caption-string-helper`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
if Button1.Caption.StartsWith('&') then
```

Correct:

```pascal
if string(Button1.Caption).StartsWith('&') then
```

<a id="uses-order-hides-type"></a>
### uses-order-hides-type

**Unit hides a type** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Winapi.Windows (or Windows) comes after Vcl.Graphics, FMX.Graphics or Graphics in the uses clauses and the unit uses TBitmap. The last unit wins, so TBitmap means the record from Winapi.Windows: TBitmap.Create and the methods of the graphics class are not found (like E2003). The uses of the interface and implementation section count together. TBitmap after a dot (Vcl.Graphics.TBitmap) stays silent. Quick fix (safe) only when both units are in the same uses clause: move Winapi.Windows before the graphics unit.

Matches the compiler message `E2003`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Bulk fix: the safe fix can be applied project-wide (`--fix uses-order-hides-type`, MCP `fix_findings`, command "Fix all safe findings …").

Flagged:

```pascal
uses
  Vcl.Graphics, Winapi.Windows;
…
  mBild := TBitmap.Create;
```

Correct:

```pascal
uses
  Winapi.Windows, Vcl.Graphics;
…
  mBild := TBitmap.Create;
```

<a id="interface-uses-missing"></a>
### interface-uses-missing

**Unit only in the uses of the implementation section** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A type in the interface section (field, parameter, result, property, ancestor) comes from a unit that is only listed in the uses of the implementation section. There the name is not known yet (like E2003). Reported when the name does not resolve in the interface section and exactly such a unit declares it in its interface part; each unit once per file. Quick fix: move the unit to the uses of the interface section (offered when the interface section has a uses clause).

Matches the compiler message `E2003`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
interface
uses
  System.SysUtils;
type
  TFenster = class
    FListe: TStringList;
  end;
implementation
uses
  System.Classes;
```

Correct:

```pascal
interface
uses
  System.SysUtils, System.Classes;
type
  TFenster = class
    FListe: TStringList;
  end;
implementation
```

<a id="unresolved-type"></a>
### unresolved-type

**Type in a declaration not found** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A type name in a declaration (field, parameter, result, ancestor, interface list, variable, type argument) is not declared (like E2003, for the target of a pointer type like E2564) or is not a type: a variable or a member after a dot like E2005, a routine like E2007, a constant like E2029 (Delphi reads it as the start of a subrange and expects '..'). Types the compiler knows without a declaration in System.pas (Text, TextFile, Real48, OpenString …) count as declared. Complements unresolved-identifier, which only checks statements. Same preconditions as unresolved-attribute: System in the symbol index, every unit from uses known, not inside {$IF…} blocks. Type parameters, names the file declares as a type anywhere (also nested) and names the index knows as a nested type (inherited inner types) are not reported. Quick fix: add the unit that declares the type (with the same number of type parameters) to uses.

Matches the compiler message `E2564`, `E2563`, `E2005`, `E2003`, `E2007`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
uses
  System.SysUtils;
type
  TKunden = TObjectList<TKunde>;
```

Correct:

```pascal
uses
  System.SysUtils, System.Generics.Collections;
type
  TKunden = TObjectList<TKunde>;
```

<a id="class-reference-nonvirtual-constructor"></a>
### class-reference-nonvirtual-constructor

**Constructor called through a class reference is not virtual** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

Create called through a class reference (class of TBasis) whose constructor is not virtual. On Win32 and Win64 this compiles silently but always runs the constructor of TBasis: fields that descendants set up in their own constructors stay empty (like E2391). The receiver may be a variable, field, parameter or function result whose type is a class reference; the constructor is resolved by name resolution in the nearest class that declares it, including overloads by argument count. The rule stays silent when a matching overload is virtual, dynamic, override or abstract, when a declaration lies in another branch of conditional compilation, and when a type cannot be resolved. Quick fix when the constructor is declared in this file: virtual on it and override on the constructors with the same signature in descendants in this file.

Matches the compiler message `E2391`.

Level B (name resolution across units).

Flagged:

```pascal
type
  TPluginKlasse = class of TPlugin;
  TPlugin = class
    constructor Create;
  end;
...
  P := Klasse.Create;
```

Correct:

```pascal
type
  TPluginKlasse = class of TPlugin;
  TPlugin = class
    constructor Create; virtual;
  end;
  TSpezial = class(TPlugin)
    constructor Create; override;
  end;
...
  P := Klasse.Create;
```

<a id="jump-outside-allowed-scope"></a>
### jump-outside-allowed-scope

**Break, Continue or Exit in a place where it is not allowed** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

Break or Continue outside a loop (like E2097), or Break, Continue or Exit that leave a finally block (like E2126). Delphi only reports both when compiling; it happens often after moving a loop body into a method of its own. A loop that lies entirely inside the finally block may use Break and Continue. Routines and anonymous methods start afresh: a Break in an anonymous method does not belong to the loop around it. Only plain calls are reported (not TMonitor.Exit(FLock)), and not when the file declares a routine or variable of that name. Quick fix outside a finally block: replace Break with Exit.

Matches the compiler message `E2097`, `E2126`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
if Eintrag.Treffer then
  Break;
```

Correct:

```pascal
if Eintrag.Treffer then
  Exit;
```

<a id="reraise-outside-handler"></a>
### reraise-outside-handler

**raise without an argument outside a handler** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

raise; without an exception object is only allowed in an except block (like E2145); Delphi only reports it when compiling. It happens in a local procedure called from the handler, in a finally block or after moving code. Routines and anonymous methods start afresh. The rule stays silent in anonymous methods inside an except block and in routines where the parser reports an error. No quick fix: raise a new exception (raise EImport.Create(…)) or move the code into the handler. reraise-exception-object covers raise E; inside the handler.

Matches the compiler message `E2145`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
Protokoll.Fehler('Import abgebrochen');
raise;
```

Correct:

```pascal
Protokoll.Fehler('Import abgebrochen');
raise EImportAbgebrochen.Create('Import abgebrochen');
```

<a id="label-misuse"></a>
### label-misuse

**Label used incorrectly** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A label is set twice (like E2073), declared and jumped to but never set (E2074), set or jumped to without being declared in the current routine (E2093; a goto to a label declared nowhere and never set is reported by the syntax rule as E2031), or goto jumps into or out of a try block, also between the try part and its except or finally part (E2127). Delphi only reports these when compiling. Labels belong to one routine or the program block; a local procedure cannot jump to a label of the routine around it. Duplicate labels in conditional branches that exclude each other stay silent, as do routines with an asm block, because labels inside asm are not in the syntax tree. No quick fix.

Matches the compiler message `E2073`, `E2074`, `E2093`, `E2127`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
label Weiter;
begin
  try
    if Fertig then goto Weiter;
  finally
    Aufraeumen;
  end;
Weiter:
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
begin
  try
    if Fertig then Exit;
  finally
    Aufraeumen;
  end;
end;
```

<a id="for-variable-misuse"></a>
### for-variable-misuse

**Loop variable changed or not local** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

The variable of a for loop is assigned in the body or changed with Inc/Dec (like E2081), passed as a var or out parameter to a routine of this file (like W1015), or is not a simple local variable: a field of the own class or a local variable of the enclosing routine (like E1019), or a global variable of the unit (like W1019, Delphi only warns). Delphi only reports these when compiling. Parameters, unknown names and loops inside with statements stay silent, as do anonymous methods in the body and routines with parser errors. Quick fixes: convert to a while loop (not when the body uses Continue), or use for var when the routine does not use the name otherwise.

Matches the compiler message `E2081`, `W1015`, `E1019`, `W1019`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
for I := 0 to Zeilen.Count - 1 do
begin
  if Zeilen[I] = '' then
    Inc(I);
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
I := 0;
while I <= Zeilen.Count - 1 do
begin
  if Zeilen[I] = '' then
    Inc(I);
  Inc(I);
end;
```

<a id="for-variable-after-loop"></a>
### for-variable-after-loop

**Loop variable read after the loop** · Bug · Warning · Group Compiler ahead

After a for loop that ends normally (without Break), the loop variable is undefined. The rule reports the first read of the variable on a path from the normal loop exit, before it is assigned again (like W1037). Only simple local variables of the routine are checked; for var loops, fields, globals, reads only inside the body, arguments of routines that might be out parameters, and routines whose control flow is incomplete stay silent. Quick fix: convert to a while loop, where the counter has a defined value afterwards.

Matches the compiler message `W1037`.

Level A (the parser is enough).

Flagged:

```pascal
for I := 0 to High(Werte) do
  if Werte[I] = Gesucht then
    Break;
Gefunden := I <= High(Werte);
```

Correct:

```pascal
I := 0;
while I <= High(Werte) do
begin
  if Werte[I] = Gesucht then
    Break;
  Inc(I);
end;
Gefunden := I <= High(Werte);
```

<a id="argument-count-mismatch"></a>
### argument-count-mismatch

**Wrong number of arguments** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A call passes more arguments than the routine has parameters (like E2034) or fewer than its parameters without default value (like E2035), also as a statement without parentheses. Checked only when the name resolves to exactly one routine: overloads, varargs, typecasts, variables of procedural type, System identifiers without source and declarations in conditional branches are left to the compiler (E2250). A function called with too many arguments is only reported when its result type is known and not procedural. Quick fix: Signature help opens the parameter hint inside the parentheses (adds () when missing).

Matches the compiler message `E2034`, `E2035`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
Versenden(Kunde.Mail, 'Rechnung');
```

Correct:

```pascal
Versenden(Kunde.Mail, 'Rechnung', Text);
```

<a id="overload-resolution"></a>
### overload-resolution

**Overload not resolvable or ambiguous** · Bug · Error · Group Compiler ahead

A call of an overloaded routine or method matches no overload (like E2250), for example a floating-point value where only Integer and string are offered, or is ambiguous (like E2251) because the matching overloads only differ in parameters with default values. Checked only when the list of overloads is certainly complete: System and all units in uses are indexed, no other unit declares a free routine of the same name, ancestors are known, no conditional, generic, nested or varargs declarations, no with. Only built-in type families without implicit conversion count as mismatching (float to integer, string to Char, Boolean to number …), aliases like their base type; records with an Implicit operator, classes, pointers, open arrays, Variant and overloaded calls inside the argument are left to the compiler. Ambiguous means: all matching overloads of the same class or unit have exactly the argument types for the passed arguments. Quick fix: Signature help shows all overloads.

Matches the compiler message `E2250`, `E2251`.

Level T (needs expression types; can be turned off with "group:compiler": {"typeLevel": "off"}).

Flagged:

```pascal
procedure Nimm(AWert: Integer); overload;
procedure Nimm(const AWert: string); overload;

Nimm(1.5);  // no overload takes a float
```

Correct:

```pascal
Nimm(Round(1.5));
```

<a id="method-without-self"></a>
### method-without-self

**Method does not need Self** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

A method of a class or record uses neither Self nor instance members (fields, properties, instance methods, inherited). It is not virtual, has no attribute and is not published (DUnitX tests and RTTI call it on the instance), is not an event of the form file, does not implement an interface and is not used as a method pointer. Experimental: only active when switched on in atlas.json; silent with refactoring.methodKind off. Quick fix: turn into a class method (class … static with refactoring.methodKindStatic); the code actions also offer calls via the class, a free routine and a record helper for the type of the first parameter, each with a preview of all changed calls.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

Flagged:

```pascal
function TRechner.Doppelt(A: Integer): Integer;
begin
  Result := A * 2;  // no field, no Self
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
class function TRechner.Doppelt(A: Integer): Integer; static;
begin
  Result := A * 2;
end;
```

<a id="method-order"></a>
### method-order

**Method bodies not in class order** · Code smell · Hint · Group Style and metrics

The method bodies of a class or record in the implementation section are not in the order that the command "Order implementation like class" and class completion produce, or the type comment above them is missing. Units written before the class order (#1836) and the type comment (#1835) can be found and tidied up one class at a time this way, so related methods stand together and the bodies follow the class declaration. One finding per type at the type name in its first body: "Method bodies of TKunde are not in class order (grouped)". The order comes from the option placement (default grouped as editor.methodPlacement; declaration, alphabetical; off switches the rule off), constructors and destructors first with classCompletion.lifecycleFirst. Only the bodies of one type among themselves count; the order of the types in the unit does not. Bodies that stay in place when arranging (inside a region or {$IF…}, sharing a line with other code) do not count as wrong. A second finding "Comment { TKunde } is missing" when classCompletion.typeComment is not off and there is no type comment of any form above the bodies. Off by default (experimental, only in the strict profile or when switched on). Quick fixes: Arrange bodies of TKunde (only this type, same logic as the command) and Insert comment { TKunde } in the form of classCompletion.typeComment.

Experimental: the rule runs only in the `strict` profile or when it gets a severity under `rules`.

| Option | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `placement` | string | `"grouped"` | Order of the bodies like editor.methodPlacement: grouped, declaration, alphabetical or off. |

Flagged:

```pascal
{ TKunde }

procedure TKunde.Speichern;
begin
end;

constructor TKunde.Create;
begin
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
{ TKunde }

constructor TKunde.Create;
begin
end;

procedure TKunde.Speichern;
begin
end;
```

<a id="pointer-integer-cast"></a>
### pointer-integer-cast

**Pointer in a 32-bit integer** · Bug · Warning · Group Migration

Win64 migration (#112): Integer(P), Cardinal(Obj), LongInt(@X), DWORD(Pointer(X)) and other casts to a 32-bit integer type with a pointer, an object, a class reference or an interface as argument. Under Win64 pointers are 8 bytes and the upper 32 bits are lost. The reverse is reported as well: TKunde(I) or IKunde(I) with I: Integer means I held a pointer in 32 bits. Pointer(I) stays silent (numbers in pointer lists are intended and lossless). The rule only reports when the type is known for sure and its declaration is always compiled together with the cast; casts in inactive branches and in branches by platform ({$IFDEF CPUX86}, {$IFDEF WIN32}, {$IF SizeOf(Pointer) = 4} and their {$ELSE}) are silent, as are Delphi versions before XE2. Quick fix: NativeInt or NativeUInt instead of the 32-bit type.

Flagged:

```pascal
Liste.Add(IntToStr(Integer(Kunde)));
```

Correct:

```pascal
Liste.Add(IntToStr(NativeInt(Kunde)));
```

<a id="handle-as-integer"></a>
### handle-as-integer

**Handle or message parameter in a 32-bit integer** · Bug · Warning · Group Migration

Win64 migration (#112): parameters wParam or lParam (also AWParam, ALParam) declared as Longint, Integer, Cardinal or DWORD, and the result of such a routine (window procedure): under Win64 WPARAM, LPARAM and LRESULT are 64 bits wide. Also the assignment of a handle (THandle, HWND, HDC, HMODULE ...), WPARAM, LPARAM, LRESULT or *_PTR value to a variable of a 32-bit integer type; the type comes from the declaration of the source, so it also works without the Windows sources. NativeInt to Integer is reported by narrowing-assignment. Silent in inactive branches, in branches by platform and before Delphi XE2. Quick fixes: WPARAM, LPARAM or LRESULT as the type; for a local variable declared alone, take over the type of the source.

Flagged:

```pascal
function FensterProc(Wnd: HWND; Msg: UINT; wParam: Longint; lParam: Longint): Longint; stdcall;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function FensterProc(Wnd: HWND; Msg: UINT; wParam: WPARAM; lParam: LPARAM): LRESULT; stdcall;
```

<a id="window-long-ptr"></a>
### window-long-ptr

**…LongPtr instead of …Long for pointers and handles** · Bug · Warning · Group Migration

Win64 migration (#112): SetWindowLong, GetWindowLong, SetClassLong and GetClassLong (also with A or W) with an index that reads or writes a pointer or handle: GWL_WNDPROC, GWL_USERDATA, GWL_HINSTANCE, GWL_HWNDPARENT, DWL_MSGRESULT, DWL_DLGPROC, DWL_USER and the GCL_ indices for icons, cursor, background, module, menu name and window procedure. Under Win64 these functions read and write only 32 bits. Indices with 32-bit values (GWL_STYLE, GWL_EXSTYLE, GWL_ID) are not reported. Silent in inactive branches, in branches by platform and before Delphi XE2. Quick fix: ...LongPtr with the GWLP_, DWLP_ or GCLP_ index; a 32-bit cast of the new value becomes LONG_PTR.

Flagged:

```pascal
SetWindowLong(Handle, GWL_WNDPROC, Integer(@NeueProc));
```

Correct:

```pascal
SetWindowLongPtr(Handle, GWLP_WNDPROC, LONG_PTR(@NeueProc));
```

<a id="asm-without-win64"></a>
### asm-without-win64

**Assembler without Win64 variant** · Bug · Warning · Group Migration

Win64 migration (#112): asm block outside a branch by platform ({$IFDEF CPUX86}, {$IFNDEF CPUX64}, {$IFNDEF PUREPASCAL} ...). dcc64 compiles neither x86 assembler nor asm inside begin ... end (E1025). Routines whose asm uses 64-bit registers (rax, r8 ...) are already Win64 code and are not reported. Files for Free Pascal ({$MODE ...}, {$ASMMODE ...}) and Delphi versions before XE2 are silent. No quick fix: the Pascal variant has to be written by hand.

Flagged:

```pascal
function Tausche(Wert: Cardinal): Cardinal;
asm
  bswap eax
end;
```

Correct:

```pascal
function Tausche(Wert: Cardinal): Cardinal;
{$IFDEF CPUX86}
asm
  bswap eax
end;
{$ELSE}
begin
  Result := Swap(Wert shr 16) or (Cardinal(Swap(Wert)) shl 16);
end;
{$ENDIF}
```

<a id="char-count-as-bytes"></a>
### char-count-as-bytes

**Character count as byte count** · Bug · Warning · Group Migration

Unicode migration (#117): Length(S) or StrLen(P) as the byte count of Move, FillChar, CopyMemory, MoveMemory, ZeroMemory, FillMemory, CompareMem, BlockRead, BlockWrite, ReadFile, WriteFile or the stream methods Read, ReadBuffer, Write and WriteBuffer with a buffer from the same string (S[1], @S[1], PChar(S), Pointer(S)^), and GetMem or ReallocMem of a PChar with Length(S) (+ 1). Since Delphi 2009 a Char has 2 bytes: only half of the characters is copied, or half the memory allocated. S must be string, UnicodeString or WideString for sure, P PChar or PWideChar; Length(S) may be shifted by a number with + or -. Silent in inactive branches, with {$H-} and before Delphi 2009. Quick fix: multiply by SizeOf(Char); not safe, because a target buffer of AnsiChar then overflows and needs a conversion instead.

Flagged:

```pascal
Move(S[1], Puffer, Length(S));
```

Correct:

```pascal
Move(S[1], Puffer, Length(S) * SizeOf(Char));
```

<a id="ansi-api-call"></a>
### ansi-api-call

**Ansi function with converted string** · Bug · Warning · Group Migration

Unicode migration (#117): call of an ...A function (MessageBoxA, CreateFileA ...) with PAnsiChar(AnsiString(S)) and S being string for sure: characters outside the ANSI code page are lost. The function without A takes the string unchanged. Mixing AnsiString and string (string-conversion, W1057/W1058) and PChar on AnsiString (suspicious-string-pointer-cast, W1044) are separate rules. Silent in inactive branches, with {$H-} and before Delphi 2009. Quick fix: the function without A with PChar(S); offered when the function is not resolved or comes from a Winapi unit and no other argument is an Ansi type for sure.

Flagged:

```pascal
MessageBoxA(0, PAnsiChar(AnsiString(Text)), 'Atlas', MB_OK);
```

Correct:

```pascal
MessageBox(0, PChar(Text), 'Atlas', MB_OK);
```
